Summary

Lena and Kara are married, working and living happily together in Austin, TX. Things are good... almost too good. Literal blasts from the past show up and turn everyone's 'happily ever afters' upside down, but the Sisters Danvers will move heaven and earth to protect their families. At any cost.

[A sequel to Lone Star Lover]
Secrets, betrayals, twists, domestic fluff, emotional transformations, lots of danger, and lots of sex.

Notes

Hello again! So nice to be back. :'D As promised, here is the sequel to Lone Star Lover. Expect chapter updates once a week. Now, before we dive in, there are a few things that need to be said:

This story is graphic. It will contain scenes of physical violence as well as gun violence.
This story also uses pieces of Northern Irish history specifically known as "The Troubles", and doing so will sort of warp what you may have in your heads as far as a timeline, but I ask that you suspend your disbelief and just roll with it. It shouldn't take you out of the story completely, as I've worked hard to be as ambiguous as possible about each character's actual age. Go with your gut, fam.
This story will also contain just a few chapters of Alex POV. I will have guest comments turned off, because it took one incredibly ugly guest commenter on Lone Star Lover to inspire an ENTIRE 50k AgentReign prequel fic, purely out of spite (and to the heartfelt wishes of a particularly kind reader.) I will make you look foolish as hell if you leave a rude comment simply for the sake of being rude. With that being said, this story is a culmination of parts one and two of the Lone Star Saga. If you haven't read the prequel "Anger is a Brief Lunacy" (An AgentReign story) and "Lone Star Lover" (A Supercorp story), I would implore you to go and read both of those and then come back! Although, I suppose you can just fill in the blanks for yourselves if you're set on reading this one first, you live your life, darlin'. (A nod to Kara's smooth southern charm) I will however mention that part one will take care of things left unsaid in part two, so, up to you. The combined pieces of this saga fill each other in nicely.

Nobody dies! Expect the same level of spicy that exists in Lone Star Lover, but maybe more? This final piece to this Irish-Texan tapestry that began when a dark haired millionaire CEO broke down outside of a tiny, no-named town in the middle of Texas is very mysterious, very sexy and will try to maintain as close a relationship with Supercorp characterization as possible, while carrying its own voice altogether. I've added plenty of additional tags already so readers have an idea of what exists in this story, but I will add more as I continue to publish chapters.

The first half of chapter one will bring you up to speed on what has transpired for our ladies in the span of five years, and the second half will be the beginnings of what to expect as far as NSFW in this story. (Oh, we are just getting started here. ;D)

Please enjoy. This was a labor of love.

    Chapter One: Perfect

     

    The northerly winds that brought cool and dry air off the plains were a brief respite from the prevailing winds that were typically blown in from the east this time of year, which usually brought in a thick moisture off of the Gulf of Mexico. Hence, the mugginess. This April in particular was thick with humidity. Storms were meant to follow not long after, but instead the cool and gentle breeze from the north kissed the blonde’s rose-colored cheeks as it swept past her in short, calm gusts. Kara’s meteorological clock was finely attuned to this phenomenon and was the exact reason why she was standing in the middle of her auto repair shop parking lot as if she was stuck in time.

    Her hands perched themselves at each hip and rested comfortably there for long moments as she turned her head back and forth into the rays of the sun, soaking as much vitamin D into her skin as possible. A pleased smile sat beneath the flared nostrils of her nose. The earthy scent of winter wheat grown in the farming fields of Oklahoma blew in with the friendly wind, as well as small notes of sweetcorn grown even further north as far as Kansas and Nebraska. ‘Smelling the world’ was a skill that Kara had honed from childhood. Other children had mocked her and claimed that there was no possible way to smell farm crop from three states away, but smell them she did. Local farmers in her small hometown claimed to be able to do the same, whose word she often took as gospel. Who better to believe than those who could sow and reap thousands of pounds of crop from the earth year by year, until old and grey.

    Life was good.

    She was married to the woman of her dreams and her niece was turning five in April. Relocating to Austin had been the best decision she had ever made, apart from asking Lena Luthor to marry her. Shortly after their brief interlude in Port Aransas after selling Danvers Ranch, it was as if Kara could not bear to wait another moment before avowing the rest of her life and the fullest extent of her love to her. They were married not two days after returning to Austin from the coast and honeymooned in mountainous Colorado for two weeks before returning again to sweep through the threshold of their new home.

    All was right in the world.

Biologically, they both were Ruby’s mothers and she bore so much of a resemblance to each of them. She had the beautifully round shape of Sam’s face with her thick mane of dark brunette hair, thanks to an anonymous Colombian sperm donor whose profile all but matched Sam right down to the smooth, honey brown color of her skin. But she also had Alex’s dark oval eyes and button nose.

Whenever Ruby was puzzled or confused, the lines of her forehead and shape of her eyes would draw together in consternation; no doubt a strong Danvers gene there. Ruby didn’t need to be genetically linked to Sam to be able to smile like her, as it was more than likely a result of seeing her mother happy for nearly every day since her birth. The theories surrounding these phenomena were often a source of much discussion and debate when the large, blended family would gather in its entirety. She was, simply put, a walking miracle.

A gap from the loss of one of her front teeth stuck out like a sore thumb and was something that Kara felt could make even the grumpiest person smile.

    “…Just as long as you agree to quit losing those teeth.” Kara added with a wink.

    Ruby rolled her eyes.

    That’s new. Wonder where she learned that…

Word quickly spread of her fast-growing business and was of course met with disdain by what is known to be a male-dominated industry, but Kara never relented in her pursuance of hiring women to work in her shop. What was once a cause of much indignation and disgust, however, was now a boon to her woman-owned and operated business, which happened to be the state of Texas’s piss poor legislation that a business owner was not required by law to provide an explanation for a no-hire situation, nor an employee’s termination.     I apologize for the amount of tissues you may use for this chapter, but I hope you find some meaning in it. Also contains further background thickening for our Luthor siblings which hinges on events that took place in parts of Northern Ireland, known as "The Troubles". Lena and Lex speak Gaeilge (Irish Gaelic, otherwise just known as “Irish”) with one another. I am of Irish heritage and spent the formative part of my childhood around Irish speaking family members, but in my adulthood I am hardly what you would consider fluent. Look to the chapter end notes for translations.

    CW: Mentions of death, violence and abuse

    Chapter Three: Sorrow Is on Me

       

    Kara was overwhelmed on the inside, but on the outside appeared stoic and undisturbed as always. She had to be, the four-year-old walking at her knee had her by a thumb in one hand, and by her heart in the other. It was hot for nearly April and the cicadas were out in full force by mid-day as she, Alex and Ruby pushed past the rusted wrought-iron graveyard gate which whined pitiably on its loose, ramshackle hinges. They meandered down the winding stony walkway for a spell where it eventually petered out into a worn dirt foot path. Weeping willows scattered the landscape, casting blankets of shade over monument and headstone alike until they slowed to a stop under a particularly large Babylon. It’s crown of long, wispy, ground sweeping branches rustled in the soft wind and curtained them in an earthen room. Here, they were cut off from the rest of the world, as if stepping into another dimension. Here, they felt as if they were alone. Except they weren’t alone…

    Kara’s breath halted in her lungs.

    The child hand in hand between her and her sister toed her shoes in the grass patiently as they stared at the stone marker before them. Alex peered over to her left at the blonde whose face was sullen, yet almost unreadable. Her eyes drew together, wrinkling the lines on her forehead as if to ask, “are you alright?” to which she nodded affirmatively. The world around them smelled like home; the one that they had shared before.

    “Ruby, there’s someone we want you to meet.” Alex said, breaking the silence. Ruby’s deep brown eyes peered up at her mother, full of curiosity. Alex then kneeled onto the grass in front of her.

    “You know you have two mommies, and you are so, so lucky for that.” She said with a gentle squeeze of a hand. “I had one too… Her name was Eliza. Would you like to say hello?”

    Ruby contemplated silently for a moment before bobbing her chin up and down with a small “yes”. Alex pointed to the headstone in front of them and beckoned her to walk toward it with a small pat on her back. Kara meanwhile had kneeled and joined her sister to watch the scene before them unfold. Ruby hesitated and looked back with a confused expression, but Alex merely nodded that it was okay and urged her forward with a gestured toss of her brow.

    She took slow and inquisitive steps toward the marker and reached out a small hand to run along its smooth edge as she stood beside it. “Hello.”

    Ruby’s voice was shy, nearly a whisper. Her eyes looked back to both her mother and aunt for answers, who then waved for her to return and hugged her tightly as she stepped in between them once again.

    “Where is she?”

    Alex choked past a whimper at her daughter’s innocence and tried her best to maintain her composure. Her and her sister both knew that it was important to approach the subject of life and death with as much patience and compassion as possible, as they themselves were made aware of it around the same age as Ruby. They knew full well that the only thing that mattered was how you talk about it, and not necessarily when.

    “She’s there, baby. We come here to remember her.”

    Kara and Alex released their hold of her, careful to not overwhelm her by touch when already her sense of smell, sight and sound were hard at work. They could see the wheels turning behind dark brown eyes as Ruby tried to understand.

    “Ruby—” Kara said gently at her. “You already know what happens to horses and goats when they get old, right?”

    Ruby paused and made a shy pout with her lips, then nodded again.

    It was common practice in the south for families to introduce their children to the cycle of life and death at a young age, especially those who lived in farming and ranching communities, simply because safeguarding children from either viewing or becoming acquainted with the act of dying on a farm was a near-impossible feat. It was better to make sure that they understand it as an inevitability and to talk about it at length, lest they develop an irrational fear of it. They knew Ruby to be an exceptionally bright child and quick to retain all knowledge, which comforted them both in preparation for this talk. Sam and Alex had spent weeks deciding whether or not to move forward with this same generational practice, which their combined decision ultimately landed Alex here in the company of her sister, kneeling in front of an etched gravestone that read the name Eliza Danvers.

    The almost-five-year-old wheeled her head back around at the stone and with wide eyes, whipped her head back again at her mother. A pout began to grow on her round and beautiful face and her eyes misted with tears, turning them a shade of light pink around their dark colored irises. By the look on her face, she suddenly understood that people also died, and not just livestock and pets.

    Alex knew that more than likely her daughter’s very next thought would be of losing her, as was her initial reaction with her own mother. Prior to their visit to the cemetery, Alex and Kara had discussed whether to also tell Ruby that they shared a different mother, biologically, but decided to table that discussion for another time. All that mattered was that Eliza raised and loved them both together, equally.

    A single dewy tear rolled over the curve of Ruby’s honey brown cheek. She didn’t seem scared or panicked, but merely stricken with a sadness. Which was good, as it was the most appropriate and natural reaction she could have during that moment.

    “Are you going to go away?” Ruby asked her mother with a slight waver in her voice.

    “Yes.” Alex answered, finally taking her by the shoulders again and pulling her into her chest to squeeze her tight. “But not for a long, long time. I promise.”

    Kara felt like a pressure cooker about to burst. It came in a strong wave from the depths of her soul and wanted to spill out of her in sobs, but with every ounce of strength that she possessed she managed to hold it back and swallow it whole. She would cry later.

    Ruby craned her head away from her mother’s shoulder and twisted it hard to look back at the marker again. She struggled to keep her eyes from it now. With a wipe of her tear-streaked cheek, she pried herself from Alex’s embrace and stepped back toward it only to cross her legs and sit directly in front of it. She scooted in the grass as close as she could to its face until her knees touched the cold stone.

    Alex and Kara both had visited countless times over the years and had witnessed the wild grass grow over their mother’s burial site, covering it in a lush and springy carpet. They remained reverent on their knees and watched as Ruby stuck a little pointer finger into the crevices of each letter on the stone as she traced them, one by one. She was getting to know her grandmother, if only by name right now. Many, many stories were sure to come.

    “E-L-I-Z-A…” Ruby recited as her finger followed the lettered carvings in the headstone. When she traced the pattern of letters that came next, she gasped excitedly.

    “That’s my name!” She looked back around at them, wearing a smile this time. Kara beamed one of her own back at her in response. The blonde’s heart lifted, and she was full of happiness again. This child truly was a healing balm for any wound.

    She is so wonderful…

    “It’s one of them, good job!” Alex cheered quietly.

    Kara sniffled and dragged a finger under an eyelid to wipe away the moisture that escaped there. “Ruby honey, can you spell your name for me? Because I plumb forgot—”

    Ruby cackled. “You did not!”

    Kara held her hands up in the air to gesture a half-hearted “oh well”, and Ruby turned back toward the headstone in front of her with a playful huff.

    “R-U-B-Y…” She said each letter in rapid succession, quickly and by heart, and then continued on at a slower and more deliberate pace, “A-R-I-A-S…” Ruby then took her finger again and placed it in the carved divot of the ‘D’ set in the stone in front of her, tracing each letter again as she went. “D-A-N-V-E-R-S.”

    Finished, she turned at her hips with her palms in the grass and smiled from ear to ear proudly. “Ruby Arias-Danvers.”

    “Good job!” Alex and Kara proclaimed in unison. They shared a laugh and crawled the few feet forward on the ground to crowd around the grave marker with her.


    Under the willow’s canopy the air was cooler, and with the added gentle breeze and the softness of the grass beneath them, the three of them sat comfortably for a little over an hour talking about Eliza and sharing stories of when Kara and Alex were young. Ruby begged for one after the other, soaking up tales about horses and adventures until she was either satisfied with her imagination of the past, or her eagerness to visit Hank the Tool Man won out.

    Ruby adored Hank immensely. She loved his gruffness and the deep of his voice in her ear as she hugged his neck. It was that and the fact that there was always candy waiting for her whenever she visited. He doted upon her shamelessly, just as he did with both older Danvers women when they were her age.

    She was also excited to run amok with Streaky’s kittens, who were now full grown and looking very much like the intimidating half-bobcat feline oddity that was their mother. Pointy-eared and beautiful, they danced in and out of Ruby’s legs, pawing at her to squat and pet them. It was time for a snack and a nap when Ruby began to quietly cry once she was told that the cats were to stay there with Hank, because that was their home. It was as if the nearly five-year-old needed reminding of this on every visit, because each time she seemed to believe that she could pull a different answer from her mother. One that involved a car ride home with a yowling cat, who would have an admittedly difficult time adjusting to a stricter, more domesticated lifestyle. They were shop cats, and even though they were gentle and patient with Ruby’s pulling and clumsy handling, they were still country-hardened, rough around the edge wild animals who were easily able to fend for themselves without human intervention.

    “Somebody’s tired.” Kara chuckled as she lifted a pouting Ruby into her arms. Her niece dropped her tired head upon her shoulder and heaved a dramatic sigh, calming some then.

    “God you’re so much stronger than I am, she’s almost too big for me to carry her like that now.” Alex exasperated playfully with her arms crossed over her chest. Kara grinned when Hank covered a proud laugh with his hand, quickly turning it into a very obvious fake cough. He was many things, but subtle didn’t happen to be one of them. Kara had that in common with him.

    “Be good now and come see me again soon. These old bones will still be here.” He said with a smile as they gathered their things.

    Alex quickly closed the distance between them and wrapped her arms around his large frame. “I love you so much. Thank you for everything.” She whispered at the side of his face, making him squeeze her just a little bit tighter.

    She kissed him lightly on his cheek as she always did whenever they said goodbye, and Kara stepped around to do the same. Hank then cradled his hand under Ruby’s head where it lolled to the side and swiped gently through her hair with his thumb. He remained silent in his affection, wearing only a proud smile on his face. Not long after that he bid them farewell with a wave of his arm as they pulled away from the curb in Alex’s car and headed toward home.

    The drive was quiet as Ruby slumbered in her car seat in the back. Kara liked it when she could sit in comfortable silence with her sister, but after an hour or so she found herself giggling at a passing memory of when her and Alex were younger.

    “What’s so funny?” Alex inquired.

    Kara sighed and adjusted in her seat. “I was just thinking about the bees in the apple orchard and that time we had to jump in the creek.”

    “Oh my God- I was stung so many times. That was your fault, if I recall.”

    A quiet gasp broke out of the blonde. “It was not!”

    “You literally kicked the nest out of the tree, which landed right on top of my head.”

    “It fell, I didn’t kick it.” Kara then sucked the air through her teeth with a cringed expression on her face. “Maybe just be glad you weren’t lookin’ up with your mouth hangin’ wide open.”

    Alex merely scrunched her forehead and made a slight scowl at her sister.

    “At least it was my bright idea to run for water.” Kara added.

    “It was also your bright idea to go back the next weekend, climb the same tree, fall out of it and break your collarbone.”

    The blonde rolled her lips into her mouth with a shade of embarrassment.

    “Mama was mad as hell.” Alex continued with a laugh. “I got you home, but I was the one got yelled at.” Her laugh suddenly dwindled into a sad hum.

    The blonde turned in her seat to stare at the side of her sister’s face as she drove. She knew a lot of Alex’s childhood was spent in service of keeping her safe from others, as well as herself. Her sister had given up a lot to be by her side as often as possible, for which she wasn’t sure if she’d ever really taken the time to thank her for. Kara guessed that Alex already knew how grateful she was to her, but she chose now to go ahead and verbalize it. Knowing that articulating words into full sentences was not her strongest skill, she’d attempt to say them anyways.

    “I don’t think I’ve ever thanked you for going out of your way to protect me when we were kids. You missed out on a lot; I realize that now.”

    Alex turned her head to glance at her sister. “Kara, it’s fine—”

    “It’s not though. I mean, we’re alright- we turned out okay, but what about all the missed parties and opportunities just so you could stay home and play scrabble with me in my room? And taking me back and forth to school whenever you got your driver’s permit so I didn’t have to ride the bus… Constantly getting yourself into trouble with other kids and making sure I was always right next to you in church… Teaching me how to tie a necktie just because I wanted to wear one and keeping it a secret—"

    “You’re my sister.” Alex said with a shrug as she glanced over at her.

    “I know.” Kara replied. “I just wanted to tell you thank you.”

    “Well, you don’t have to. I love you.”

    Kara adjusted in her seat again. “I love you too.” She said after a small pause and then craned her head around to look in the backseat. Ruby was fast asleep still, both cheeks a rosy pink with her exhaustion and likely due to the day’s heat as well.

    A change in subject approached in Kara’s mind, not because sharing verbal affections and words of affirmations with her sister made her uncomfortable, but too long of a moment did tend to lean that way and she didn’t want it to be awkward. For her sister, a few years’ worth of speaking with a licensed therapist had gone a long way. For herself, well, that was still a road left untraveled, but it had been eyed with consideration from time to time.

    “Lena’s brother is in town.”

    Open mouth, insert foot…

    Alex’s forearm muscles sprang to attention when she squeezed the steering wheel tightly. “I’m sorry, what?”

    “Lex Luthor—"

    “The crazy one?”

    “The only one…”

    “Didn’t he go to prison for war crimes?”

    “Apparently he’s been released.”

    “Kara… what!” Alex shook her head in disbelief with her eyes wide as she stared a hole through the windshield of her car. Her mouth dropped open and snapped shut again several times, seemingly at a loss for words, or for anything other than the word “what” at this point.

    Of course, Kara knew her sister would want to know every detail now that what used to be a very real but very distant threat had somehow made its way closer to them, so she leaned further into her seat and took a deep breath before diving in. If a man who was convicted of killing people was now very likely to be in her niece’s life, however indirectly, then she knew Alex had every right to know everything about him. Or to the best of her knowledge, that is.

    “I’ll tell you everything, but you have to agree to put the detective aside, okay? Lena’s been through hell about it and just wants to live a normal life.”

    Alex’s temporomandibular joint on her jaw clicked as she gnashed her back teeth anxiously, then she took a long and contemplative pause before speaking again.

    “Are we safe?”

    Kara thought on that for a little longer than what Alex seemed to like. Her sister’s knuckles were a pale white where they wrapped around the steering wheel firmly. Kara obviously didn’t grow up with Lex Luthor. She really didn’t know much about him at all, and actually had nothing to go on except for what Lena had told her. That, and what she saw of him for less than five whole minutes at eight o’clock on a recent morning. He seemed mentally stable enough, and essentially non-violent. But as far as reformed… she couldn’t say.

    “If Lena thought it was okay to meet him at a public place while I’m out of town, I’d say that she felt like it was safe.”

    That appeared to be good enough for her sister. Alex exhaled loudly through her nose and nodded. “Okay. Let me have it.”

     

     

    —Earlier that day—

     

    Lena’s heels made a distinct -clack- every time they made contact with the hard concrete below her. She was pacing. The face on the clock tower that sat atop the highest eave of her favorite library read a half hour before noon. Her heels continued to tap a small path back and forth in front of a large fountain, which the sound of its running water did little to ease her anxiety like she had hoped.

    She was pacing because she was nervous. Nervous, anxious, a little bit queasy… and quite possibly scared?

    This has to be a dream…

    After days of struggling to decide whether or not to humor her half-brother’s innocent enough request to reconnect, she finally called him back that morning before Kara and Alex had set off toward their hometown with Ruby to visit their late mother. He was to meet her at the public library nearest their home, with an emphasis on the word ‘public’ to insist that she was not yet ready to be alone around him.

    “Bomber kills fourteen in church massacre…”
    “Lex Luthor; tech heir turned terrorist…”
    “Murderous monster makes mockery of mourning…”

    All of the newspaper headlines from a past she thought she had outrun began to swarm in her head, and suddenly she felt her chest begin to tighten. It was painful, all of it. The images of her brother scowling on the front page of several London newspapers as he sat in a courtroom during the highly publicized trial made the knot in her throat grow ever larger by the second. She could feel her entire body. Her aching ribs, the ringing in her ears… everything hurt. Her feet then came to a screeching halt, and she spun to sit on the edge of the fountain with a trembling hand to shield her eyes from the high noon sun. When she lowered it, it was as if the memory of Lex had leapt out of her troubled mind and had been given shape, because there he stood directly in front of her with a worried expression drawn onto the weary lines of his face.

    “Well?”

    His voice sent a chill through her.

    “Yes, just… tired.”

    Lex shoved his hands into the same suit pants he had worn when he showed up unannounced at her front door. He bowed his head. It was obvious that he was aware that his sister was lying. Who could blame her?

    “Thank you for coming…” His voice drowned out from the spray of the fountain at her back. “For a while there I didn’t think you’d ring back.”

    “I didn’t think I was going to either.” Lena admitted.

    Lex lifted his head to meet her eyes. “What changed yer mind?”

    His brogue was thick, and it reminded her of home. Lena straightened her back and sighed. A couple hand in hand walked past and entered the double doors of the large library just a short distance away. The thought of being inside that library, deep in its stacks at its furthest point with her nose shoved so far into a copy of Wuthering Heights that the rest of the world would just fall away was enticing, but much to her chagrin, she had reality to deal with at the moment.

    “I—” She shrugged. “I don’t know, actually.”

    He smiled at her again, and yet it unnerved her still. Lena shuffled her heels anxiously and crossed her ankles, hoping that she could appease him in whatever way that he needed and that he would be on his way.

    Lex reached a hand out to gesture at the fountain ledge beside her as if asking to sit, to which she nodded carefully, and then he stepped forward, unfastening a middle button on his suit as he sat down beside her. The sound of the running water at their backs still played in her ear, anchoring her somewhat to reality.

    “I can’t say enough how good it is to see ye.”

    “How about the light of day?” Lena shot back, bearing no hesitation in her words.

    Lex turned his chin toward her with a surprised look on his face. She had shocked them both with her malice; It was unlike her to speak with such a sharp tongue. Maybe she harbored more resentment for her older brother than she previously thought she did.

    Lena turned her face away and dropped her eyes to the water behind them. She was ashamed, but she felt the way that she felt and there was no helping that right now. Not without hours and hours of therapy, which she quickly considered before facing forward again.

    “I apologize, that was harsh.”

    “But deserved.” He replied calmly.

    She couldn’t bring herself to look at him again. She wanted so desperately to go home, or to at least be anywhere other than where she was. Even the mouth of an active volcano would suffice.

    “Lex, what is it you wanted to talk about?” She said painfully, eyes still downcast but doing her best to move the conversation forward

    “I did say that I wanted to see ye. The ‘talking’ I suppose was implied.” He squirmed momentarily. “Is mór dá aiféala atá orm, Lena.” The lilt of his Irish rang in her ears with a shame unlike anything she had ever heard. It was a beautiful language that she had long cherished and to hear it drip out of the mouth of a man who had hurt her so deeply only tarnished it in her mind. So much of her past had been taken from her, and now this? Who was she if she resented such an integral piece of her identity? What would be left of her in the end…?

    “I am sorry. For everything.” He continued.

    Lena bristled at that and twisted her hips to face him finally, drawing a knee up onto the fountain’s ledge with her to sit squarely in front of him.

    “You killed—” Her jaw clenched as she spoke through her teeth, “…fourteen people.”

    Lena watched the color drain from her brother’s face the further she leaned toward him. Her eyes were like daggers; piercing and unforgiving.

    “There were children in that church.”

    Lex looked as though he would be sick right there on the concrete at their feet. “Tá brón orm.” He said with a shaky voice. “But I traveled all this way to apologize to you.”

    Lena stilled with that; her heart panging so hard behind her chest that she feared it would stop completely.

    “To me?” She asked with her brow furrowed.

    He dipped his chin to nod a “yes” and then paused.

    “First—” She spoke firmly. “I want to understand how you’re even here.”

    Lex blinked as he adjusted to balance an ankle on an opposite knee. He seemed prepared for an explanation, which unsettled her further. “The long and short of it is… I was released. I appealed my conviction with new evidence supporting that I had no knowledge of the people who were inside that church and as a result, my sentence was commuted to time served.”

    “What new evidence?”

    He narrowed his eyes inquisitively at her, but in a way that was non-threatening. “Is that really what you want to know?”

    This was already becoming so tedious. Lena felt the urge to literally stand up and run away. Instead, she chose to remain seated and did her best to stifle her rising anger. “What do you want with me?”

    “I wanted to say that I’m sorry for abandoning you. I left you alone with them.”

    Them, of course, being Lionel and Lillian Luthor.

    “I was concerned with all the wrong tings and I regret leavin’. I couldn’t let go of home.” He paused to draw a deep breath through his nose and let it go slowly. “Dad, the old bastard God rest his soul, wouldn’t hear me about it. Drove me a bit barmy, it did.”

    Lena shifted uncomfortably at the mention of their neglectful and oftentimes abusive father.

    “It was like he couldn’t stand stepping on the same soil where—” His eyes shot to Lena’s as he stopped himself short of mentioning her biological mother. Lionel Luthor had hushed them all into silence about his dalliance with Lena’s mother, and when Lena was whisked away to London to live with them permanently after she had passed, they never spoke of her again.

    “I never blamed ye, though. Such a wee girl then, so you were. It wasn’t anyting anyone could do. I just… went to a dark place.”

    Lena felt like she could fall apart at her joints. It was all so overwhelming. A blast from her past in the form of her brother had just pummeled her with unlocked memories of her childhood, and not the good ones either. Their father was curt and rarely spoke a kind word, which had a way of making a child ravenous for approval. Lena however, had desired no such thing. The only thing she wanted was to crawl under a bed and to stay there forever. It had been Lex who had coaxed her out of her shell all those years ago.

    “You were my world, Lex. And you burned it down for what? Because Dadaí didn’t love you?”

    There was that unintentional, but seemingly unavoidable malice again. Her breath caught in her throat as she struggled to talk past the lump forming there. “When you were arrested, the craziest thing happened.”

    Lex blinked rapidly with his confusion.

    “All of the sudden, they started paying attention to me. Heavily.” Lena said pointedly, feeling as though there could be smoke coming from her nostrils. “Mother suddenly wanted the perfect daughter after ignoring me for near on a decade and Dad… Dad had me followed everywhere. Everywhere, Lex. He very quickly figured out that I’d no sooner marry a man than I would an ostrich, and he hit me. It took me two years to leave after that.”

    Lex looked as though he himself had been struck across the face, his shock appearing for the most part genuine. Maybe he hadn’t considered that their father would one day turn his violence toward her in his absence.

    “I have a life here. A good one. I can’t—”

    Before she could write him off into the annals of her history, he reached out and caught one of her waving hands as she spoke emphatically. “Wait…”

    Lena clapped her jaw shut, surprised that he had dared to touch her.

    “I just want a fair chance to be yer brother again. Let me at least try.”

    Reactively, she snatched her wrist back from his grip and held it protectively over her chest as if it had been burned. Floundering for a response, the first thing to come out of her mouth was, “I’ll think about it.”

    Seemingly satisfied, Lex smiled weakly and stood. They stared at each other for a moment before he bowed his head in a goodbye. “You know how to reach me then.”

    Lena’s brain sputtered for an explanation at what she had just half-heartedly agreed to. Which wasn’t actually anything, but it definitely hadn’t been a “no”. After Lex left, she stayed seated on that fountain’s edge and contemplated the events of her life which had brought her there. What had brought her to England, then to New York, and then to Texas… What would Kara say right now?

    “You’re right where you ought to be, darlin’.”

    Or something to that effect.

    Her wife always had a way of comforting her with just the right words, albeit they were typically few in number. Cars and farm equipment she could talk about at lightspeed. But expressing her emotions, especially when talking about herself, had a tendency to fire on far fewer pistons. Not for any lack of them though, she simply chose very carefully when and how to share them.

    Lena knew that she could only find comfort in Kara right now, but she would have to wait until that evening. For what it was worth, her part of the deal that she had struck with her brother had been upheld. She had come and did what she had said she would; She heard him out. It left her feeling untethered, and greatly in need of coming back down to earth. The small wish of it all just being a bad dream entered her thoughts again and she so badly wanted for that to be the truth. Soon, the ‘what ifs’ began to roll in. What if Lex Luthor hadn’t showed up at their door that morning? What if he had never left home to join up with a radical group of Irish Nationalists? What if he had never bombed that church and killed those people? What if he was still that same playfully goofy, uninhibited, and carefree teenaged boy that she had admired when they were younger? Would she even have met Kara?

    She realized that she probably would have never left home in the first place, but then figured that may or may not actually be true. Lillian Luthor was venomous in her dislike of her and may very well have continued making her every day a living hell if she had stayed. Whenever she wasn’t treating Lena as if she were invisible, that is. Her father most likely would have carried on placing impossible expectations upon her and denying her any chance at a life of her own. She was a Luthor, after all.

    It's likely that she would have never met Sam, or Kara for that matter. She wouldn’t know what true love was, had she not stumbled upon it by chance. Had it been fate? Was it some invisible, pre-ordained working that sacrificed the love of her brother so that she could find the love of her life? That seemed so cruel. None of it was fair. Losing her mother, and then the brother she grew to adore. But had she made up for what she had lost?

    Lena leaned forward onto her thighs with her elbows and cradled her head in her hands. She wanted to go home, to the loving sanctuary she shared with her wife. Everything would be alright then; she knew that much.

    It would just have to be enough for now.


    Chapter End Notes

    “Is mór dá aiféala atá orm” = I deeply regret it
    “Tá brón orm” = There is sorrow on me / I am sorry

    In Irish there are a few ways to express apology, all of which beautifully transcend the English "Sorry" — which in Ireland if you were to say, it would simply mean something close to "excuse me".

    Thanks for reading~

    Chapter 4: Olympian

    Chapter Summary

    Kara and Lena spend an evening together making all known gods green with envy. Kara receives an unexpected call before the night is over.

    Chapter Notes

    Welcome to a full chapter of smut. That is all, carry on.

    Chapter Four: Olympian

     

    The end of March brought thunderstorms and varying degrees of erratic temperatures which nearly ruined a full day of outdoor plans to visit the zoo with Ruby, but due to Kara’s quick thinking she managed to bring the zoo home to them.

    Each room in the massive house she shared with Lena had been imaginatively transformed into wild animal enclosures with the help of Ruby’s plethora of stuffed animals. Kara had designated different rooms by region: African, Australian, and South Asian species in three bedrooms, with the bathtub in Ruby’s room upstairs filled with floating dolphin and fish-shaped bath toys.     “Oh honey—” Kara said as she took a knee down to talk with her niece at her level. “They’ll be alright…” She paused for a moment when a concerning thought struck her. “Do you know what lonely feels like?”

    Ruby looked at Kara with big, brown doe eyes and blinked several times. “I want my Mama.”

    Kara’s heart plummeted at the innocent admission. Her sister Alex had chosen the name “Mama” to raise Ruby with in honor of the name they called their own mother, and Sam took a similar spelling but with a different intonation reserved for Latinx speaking families, “Mamá”. When Ruby addressed Sam directly, she was “Mami”.

    As a Vice Detective, Alex had taken two undercover jobs since the time Ruby had been born and had missed, in total, one year of her life already. Kara was vaguely aware of the strain this placed on her sister’s marriage, and she knew that Ruby was now getting old enough to experience the third-degree effects from said strain. If her sister and Sam were not doing well in their marriage, she didn’t know about it, but what she did know was that Ruby was starting to miss Alex. Maybe she understood the possibility of Alex taking another job which would mean more time spent away from her? Kara hadn’t heard anything about another UC investigation… at least Alex had not made any mention of one. Or maybe Ruby simply missed her mother over the course of a single weekend. Whatever it was, she would bend over backwards to make sure that Ruby felt seen, and cherished.

    “That’s where we’re goin’ baby, to Mama.” Kara reassured her and tucked a small bunch of her thick hair behind an ear. “And tell you what, you leave your Polly Pockets and your Barbie dolls here for when you come stay again next weekend and I promise to tuck them into bed every night until then so they don’t get lonely. Okay?”

    Ruby flashed a toothless smile at her, the beginnings of a new tooth already poking through her gumline. “Okay aunt Kara.”

    She climbed up the step ladder next to her favorite stool seat at the kitchen island where her dolls had been laid out and scooped them into her arms, making a mad dash back to the stairs to take them up to her room again.

    Kara tilted her head with another thought.

    “Hey Ruby, what about your Ken dolls? Aren’t you afraid they’ll get lonely too?” Kara called out to the four-year-old as she watched her take the steps of the stairs with her hands and knees, clumsily dropping a doll once or twice on her way up.

    “No! They’re having a tea party in the book room!”

    Kara chuckled to herself as she dragged the zipper back around Ruby’s backpack after shoving a couple of tangerines and a juice box into it.

    Should probably go and check in on this “tea party” before we leave… just to be safe.

     

     

    Noonan’s opened its doors at 6 am sharp and Kara was happy to have a fresh brewed cup of coffee that didn’t come out of a two-thousand-dollar espresso machine. While she appreciated her wife’s refined palette and her associated particularities concerning kitchen appliances, which most of them did lend a hand toward an efficiently made meal or drink, she sometimes just craved a simple cup of black coffee, two sugars. Ruby was just excited to have the first warm bagel of the day. Once finished with their breakfast, Kara had just begun to spread out a page of a coloring book and a box of crayons for her when the bell above the door of the store rang as it opened. Ruby busily scrawled away with a red crayon as a woman approached them. She was tall, with a distinct white streak in her hair and Kara recognized her well enough from their very brief and peculiar interaction at Eliza’s funeral years ago. That, and there was something eerily familiar about her that Kara just couldn’t put her finger on. This woman claimed to be her aunt. How come she didn’t remember her for other than the few seconds they spoke nearly five years ago?

    “Hello, Kara.” The woman said as she reached their table. “I’m Astra.”

    Kara dipped her chin in a friendly manner to return a non-verbal greeting before offering a kind word to the other woman. “It’s nice to meet you. This is my niece, Ruby.” She gestured downward with a hand. “Ruby, say hello please?”

    Ruby lifted her eyes away from her coloring book page for just as long as it took to mutter a shy “Hello” and then went back to her red scribbling.

    Astra seemed to force a crooked, close-lipped smile at her. Perhaps she wasn’t overly fond of children? Maybe it was just awkward. Hell, the whole situation was awkward.

    “Here, let’s take a seat at a booth.” Kara said as she stuck an arm out toward the row of booths along the wall toward the street. “Ruby, I’ll be right over here, two spaces away… okay? I’m watching you.”

    Ruby grinned and held her coloring book up between her fists excitedly. “Look, red!”

    Kara chuckled, bursting with pride. “So red! Great job, darlin’.”

    Happy with her aunt’s praises, Ruby went back to furiously scribbling away at the page. That poor red crayon didn’t stand a chance. Across from the table, Kara sat in the booth seat with her back to the front door to keep an eye on Ruby and Astra slid into the seat opposite her. There was an almost too long, awkward beat between them before the woman sitting across from her decided to diffuse the tension and begin speaking.

    “I know that you probably have a ton of questions—”

    “That’s putting it lightly.”

    Kara didn’t mean to sound stand-offish, but it had sort of slipped out that way. She wasn’t aware of any hard feelings toward an aunt that she had no memory of, but the thought did suddenly occur to her that she had lived all this time so far thinking that she had no other family other than her cousin, Clark, who regrettably lost his own father in the same fire that took both of her parents. He had been raised by his mother, whose mental health suffered infrequently from the devastation of losing her husband and remaining extended family so suddenly. Kara was not able to stay with her cousin because of this, and this was how she came to live on Danvers Ranch. The Danvers family had adopted her and accepted her as one of their own, and she had lived a good and mostly happy life because of their hospitality, as well as their love. Not to mention her sister Alex’s undying loyalty and strong sense of justice. She had been shielded from a lot, or so she learned as she grew older. Kara wasn’t sure what she would have done with the information of having another blood-relative, if she had discovered such any sooner of course.

    “My apologies, I’ll try not to get ahead of myself. And sorry for the early hour—” Kara began.

    “Not to worry, I’m still running on a completely different time zone; it will actually feel like time for bed in a few hours.” Astra replied coolly.

    “Oh, wow. Okay, well, Ruby’s mom- my sister Alex, she’ll be here in a little bit to pick her up…” Kara said as she stuck a thumb over at the four-year-old.

    Astra nodded. “Understood.” Then she straightened her back and adjusted slightly in her seat, leaning forward to rest her arms on the table and clasping her hands together tightly.

    “So, to begin, is there anything in particular that you want to ask me?”

    Kara chewed on the inside of her lip anxiously. She really did have so many questions.

    “Now that I know you exist, where the hell were you?”

    “Why did no one tell me you even existed in the first place?”

    “Or did I already know you and somehow forgot?”

    “Is that even possible?”

    “How did you get my phone number?”

    “Are you here to stay?”

    After a few anxious breaths, Kara chose the first one and reformulated it to fit a more polite tone.

    “Where have you been keeping yourself all these years?” The blonde asked nervously.

    Astra blinked and leaned back again, resting her shoulders into the back of the seat. “Zurich. Moscow. Kazakhstan. Romania, more recently… That’s actually where I’ve just arrived from.” Her expression was stony as she rattled off each part of the world where she’d ‘been keeping herself’.

    All Kara heard was “Far, far away from you.”

    “Did you know about me?” Kara asked without thought, which was completely unlike herself. Normally she was over-critical of herself in the way that she didn’t speak much unless she was comfortable in either her surroundings or company; The latter of which liked to joke that they could never shut her up. But just blurting things out was definitely not her speed. That was her first sign that these were choppy waters, and she should have to navigate the rest of this conversation carefully.

    “I did.” Astra blinked. Her expression for the most part was blank still. In fact, it unsettled Kara to no end how this woman sitting across from her seemed to have one expression permanently fixed upon her face, which was best described as cold, and unfeeling.

    An intrusive thought of leaping upward with her hands held out in claw shape with a loud “Boo!” managed to worm its way inside of her mind, to which she quickly stuffed back down and shook her head as if a bell had been rung behind her ears. Kara merely blinked rapidly with her mouth held slightly agape as if to say “I don’t understand…”

    Astra grew tense in her shoulders despite her unchanging facial expression and leaned forward again. “My relationship with your mother was… strained.”

    Kara just continued to sit and blink away her disbelief.

    “I left shortly after you were born.”

    The blonde paused with that and then allowed herself another straight-to-the-point question, “Did you ever wonder about me or want to get to know me?”

    Astra’s seemingly unfeeling expression shifted then, which Kara was unprepared for, and it jarred her slightly. Kara was skilled at her age in the art of reading human emotion, having been subjected to an environment for many years where verbal communication of this magnitude was sparse, but passive aggressiveness reigned supreme. She had learned as a child to gauge her interactions with other humans by how softly or how hard they shut cabinet doors, or how quickly their brow changed shape when certain subjects of conversation were broached. But now for the first time in her entire life, she could not make heads or tails of anything. It was as if the look Astra was giving her held too many distinguishable markers for too many different emotions. This woman was a maelstrom.

    “I did.” Astra admitted a second time after another long pause. Her words seemed to bite with a sharp bitterness, but again they simply did not match her face.

    Am I supposed to feel terrified right now? Because I do…

    What is wrong with me…?

    Kara thought as she glanced down at the watch face on the inside of her wrist where it read a quarter ‘til, and she turned her head to glance at Ruby again who was now inspecting her smattering of broken crayons with a vengeance. She’d be bored again soon…

    “My work keeps me very engaged.” Astra added quickly, possibly in an effort to alleviate some of her own guilt… though Kara wasn’t sure if the woman staring at her from across the table was capable of that particular emotion. She had only just met her, after all. But shouldn’t all humans be capable of feeling guilt though? She thought.

    Thankful for the segue into Astra’s work, for which Kara had many questions, she opened her mouth to approach the subject but was interrupted by the ring of the bell above the front door behind her.

    “Mama!” Ruby shrieked from her table.

    Kara’s eyes widened.

    Shit...

    It's not that Kara had planned to keep her meeting with Astra a secret from Alex… No, of course not. But why did she suddenly feel ashamed? Kara floundered subconsciously to appease her growing guilt… It had been very last minute, and she had been busy entertaining her niece all weekend. The blonde stood abruptly to pace over to Ruby’s now empty table and began to sweep the scattered crayons messily back into their box. It took about as long for her to shove everything back into Ruby’s backpack and zip it closed for Alex to sidle up next to her at the table.

    “Hey! You’re early.” Kara proclaimed nervously at her sister.

    “Everything alright? You look… pale.” Alex asked.

    Kara knew better by now than to try and outsmart a decorated detective who also happened to be her sister. Even as a child there wasn’t anything she could get past her. But the discovery of the woman still sitting in the booth just a few tables away was imminent, and she knew that it was ultimately better to get ahead of it instead of trying to work around it.

    Kara shook her head and swiped a wisp of blonde hair out of her face hurriedly with the back of a hand as she gestured toward Astra. “Yeah! Yeah. I uh, was just talking with someone—”

    “Who?” Alex leaned to look past Kara’s shoulder at the booth and suddenly dropped her phone and car keys from her hands. They clattered loudly against the floor, making them both jump slightly. The blood drained from Alex’s face in seconds as she clutched a fist in her polo shirt just beneath its last button. It appeared as though her sister was trying to catch her heart from beating violently outside of her chest.

    Kara reached out to steady her with a hand at each shoulder. “Alex?”

    “Kara…” Alex gulped hard and blinked several times. “That’s—”

    “Her name is Astra… She says she’s my aunt.”

    Alex huffed a quiet but astonished laugh between them. “Aunt? For God’s sake, I thought she was…” She stopped herself before going any further and shook the thought from her head. Kara, however, was not about to let her sister walk away from that one.

    “Thought she was who?”

    Alex focused her eyes on her sister’s baby blues and bore into them hard with a furrowed brow; the whole of her visibly shaken.

    “Kara, she’s the spitting image of your mom.”

    Every hair on Kara’s body stood on end with that. Her rising confusion astounded her in a way that she could very well have traveled outside of her body for a moment without even knowing.

    Wait a minute, what does my mom look like?

    How do I not remember what my mom looks like...?

    To be fair, Kara knew that she had been very young when her world was turned upside down, and the few remaining pictures she had of her biological parents were lost to the years after her first home was foreclosed upon. The rest of her family’s estate had been thrown into probate and then gathered into a small monetary trust for her. She had eventually been able to buy back their house from the bank when she turned eighteen, but everything within had been stripped clean of furniture, picture frame and memory alike.

    Ruby began to tug on one of Alex’s belt loops with one hand from where she stood at her hip and held in her other her mother’s keys and cell phone. Alex leaned down and kissed her on the top of her head with a whispered ‘thank you’ and ‘please wait a moment’.

    “She was at Mama’s funeral service.” Kara continued.

    Her sister shifted her eyes over to the woman again who was glaring at her phone and typing with her thumbs. Alex’s line of sight bounced back and forth between Astra and Kara for a few moments longer before straightening her back again. She then shook her head with a sort of befuddlement as if to ask, “What the hell?” which was precisely the next thing to come out of her mouth.

    “What the hell?”

    “Did you not see her there? We spoke for like three seconds and she left but—”

    “No!” Alex widened her eyes at the level of her voice and then covered her mouth with a hand to suppress her slight embarrassment. “I mean, maybe?” She squinted and peered around Kara a third time.

    “She called over the weekend. It was really sudden- she just flew into town and wanted to meet—”

    Alex hacked another small, surprised chuff of a laugh which brought her sister’s apologetic rambling to a screeching halt. “Yeah, I guess after a few decades of being literally anywhere else, because that makes total sense.”

    Kara’s forehead pulled together, wrinkling the lines that sat in between her eyes. “What does that have to do with anything?”

    Even though she had asked the question, Kara knew that it had everything to do with everything. It meant everything. It was everything… for right now, at least. She just wasn’t ready to admit it yet. Alex’s demeanor shifted immediately to backpedal.

    “I just mean… like, she could pop in for Mama’s funeral but wants to finally chat after almost five years? Come on, Kara.” The volume of her voice was still just low enough to reach the blonde who continued to stand close to her front, but the emphatic nature of her words hit Kara like a freight train. The pure audacity of her sister… But then, who else would she be if not were not audacious? And bold, and kind, and protective to a fault?

    Kara’s hands went to her hips then as she loosed an exasperated sigh. “Not everything is an investigation, Alex.”

    “I’m not saying it is…” It was Alex’s turn for her brow to pinch together defensively.

    Ruby tugged at her belt loop again, giving Alex the opportunity to abandon the suddenly awkward, unintentionally hurtful conversation. She kneeled to smile openly and excitedly at her daughter then.

    “Ready?!”

    “Yeah!”

    When Alex stood, Kara shoved Ruby’s backpack into her sister’s arms before they were even fully open to receive it.

    “Hey…!”

    “She ate half a toasted bagel with a fruit bowl and has clean clothes in the big zipper.” Kara muttered, pointing a finger at the backpack.

    “Kara—”

    Before she could finish, the blonde turned on her heels and walked at a brisk pace back toward the booth where the woman with the white streak in her hair sat.

    In Alex’s past, little things like that would spur her into a full-blown argument in front of anyone unfortunate enough to be in the near vicinity, but she had grown from all of that. She wasn’t without her ups and downs, though. Alex clenched her teeth and swallowed her frustration as Ruby pulled her by a hand toward the door.

    In that same moment Kara had so many mixed emotions bubbling up inside of her that she didn’t know whether she wanted to scream, spit, or sob. She was upset that her sister took any opportunity to be suspicious and had been unfortunately not as delicate about the situation as Kara would have liked… She was mad at the fact that it even upset her, because deep down she shared the same sentiments about this newcomer. But if she couldn’t admit them, then nobody else could. The fucking nerve. She was also sad that she was now mad at her sister. The torrent of her emotions struck the walls of her heart like a monsoon where it lashed away at the poor, pitiful muscle with the stinging rain of invisible tears.

    But as always, on the outside Kara Luthor-Danvers appeared thoroughly unbothered, eyes forward, and without a shred of discomfort. This was her curse. How could anyone possibly begin to understand how she felt when she worked so hard to conceal the fact that she did. Feel, that is, which she did so tremendously. Lena had been the only person in her entire life besides her adopted father Jeremiah to unravel the braided rope of anguish that dropped anchor against her soul. She knew she was safe to bare it to her wife without fear of judgement… But for the first time again in years, she wanted to harbor these feelings close to shore and keep them to herself.

    Astra didn’t stay much longer after Alex had left and actually appeared as though she was in a hurry to depart. Kara still had many questions, but the other woman was insistent on leaving as she had to get to her previously arranged meeting she claimed was what initially brought her so close to Austin. Kara, deflated and left feeling like a used napkin, sat alone in the booth and dripped a single tear onto the lid of her coffee. But just the one.


    Over the course of the following week Kara found herself utterly bitter and impatient with things that didn’t work out the way she wished. The acrimony she felt for her sister panged in her heart, along with whatever else she felt within herself at any given moment such as sorrow, resentment, desolation, hopelessness…  All of which revolved around a fixed, fallback emotion, one that she hated. Anger. She wished it would just leave her the hell alone, but it was persistent in its domination over her. Never before had Kara thrown a wrench in her life, but on a regular Wednesday afternoon and for no apparent reason other than accidentally snapping three transmission pan bolts in a row, she hurled a five sixteenths across her shop where it landed loudly against some tool cabinet drawers with a loud -clang-.

    Aware of her momentary loss of control and lapse of judgement, Kara endeavored to go the rest of the week without losing any more patience but found it much more difficult than she expected. Little things set her off, not so much in a tirade of anger but inwardly so, where there were many battles fought. One thing in her mind led to another, which led to another, which somehow threw her back to long-forgotten childhood memories of times when adults were dismissive of her, or other children were cruel to her. The difference now being that they pissed her the fuck off. The effort it took to get herself unstuck from those barreling trains of thought was exhausting and by the weekend she found herself waking up in the morning and begging the universe for her to not be so easily moved to anger anymore. Living like that hurt and made her soul feel tired.

    Friday evening the chime of their front door echoed past the foyer and into the living room area where Kara sat with an ankle crossed at her knee. Her thumb tapped away repeatedly on the tv remote in search of a program either about cute, fluffy animals or something animated and kid friendly, but she’d resolve to settle for whichever came first. Thankful for the bell of the front door, she paused her button mashing to toss the remote down onto a couch cushion and stomped over to the entryway to answer it. A flash of dark brown hair and a squeal shot through the door just as soon as she pulled it open.

    “PO-TAYTOEEES!”

    Kara winced at the shrill scream as the streak of dark hair continued its high-pitched giggling and squealing around the corner and ran into the kitchen where a surprised greeting from Lena rang back through the walls of the house. Sam was revealed standing in the open doorway now, shaking her head into the palm of her hand with an embarrassed, but equally proud grin.

    “We… had an early lunch. I think she’s hungry.”

    “No kidding?” Kara joked lightly and turned her head to shout over her shoulder. “Hey babe? What are we having for dinner?”

    Two voices in unison echoed back from the kitchen. “Potatoes!”

    Sam and Kara laughed as Lena appeared from around the corner with a bouncing Ruby tethered to her by a hand and just like that, Kara’s anger and bitterness was gone. Sam smiled and reached out to grab a hug from around Lena’s shoulders.

    “Well?” The raven-haired woman asked, her usual way of asking her best friend how she was doing.

    Sam rolled her eyes playfully. “Just glad it’s the weekend. Who knew editors could nag more than wives can?”

    “I heard that.” Alex teased from the driveway, apparently still seated in the driver’s seat of her BMW.

    “Is that Alex? Tell her to come up for a craic and a hug—”

    “She um—” Sam’s expression shifted slightly. “We’ve actually got dinner reservations, just here to drop and run unfortunately!”

    Lena and Sam shared a split-second, knowing sort of glance at each other as if to silently transmit a message without detection; A skill they had perfected in college. Lena then knew that something was awry between the sisters Danvers but would shelve her curiosity for after Sam had left.

    Sam leaned down and gestured toward her daughter with her arms. “Ruby, one more hug before I see you on Monday?”

    The four-year-old practically leapt into her mother’s frame but only stayed there for maybe a full second before bouncing away again on her toes.

    “Can we watch the meerkat show?” Ruby asked up at Kara eagerly.

    Kara smiled and nodded. “Sure, you’re gonna have to show me which channel- but we’re eating dinner first, okay?”

    “Yes! Potatoes!”

    Kara laughed and rested her hands on her hips, seemingly satisfied. Lena shifted and leaned a hip against the door frame in admiration of her wife whenever she stood like this. It was one of her favorites on the mile-long list of things the blonde did with her body that made her want to spontaneously combust.

    “I’m sure we’re having more than just potatoes, Rubes.”

    “She nearly had me convinced to change the menu. The wee’un loves her spuds.” Lena said with a shrug and flashed a smile down toward Ruby, who nodded with an air of exaggeration and nearly stumbled while standing still, much like an uncoordinated and overexcited four-year-old would.

    With a chuckle and a wave, Lena and Kara bid farewell to Sam and welcomed Ruby back into their home for the weekend. Lena had prepared a meal of sausage, roasted potato and carrot with stewed cabbage and a loaf of bread with pats of butter to spread. After they ate, they each climbed into their seats on the large sectional couch in front of the tv and before long, little Ruby was fast asleep. Normally, she liked to snuggle in between Kara and Lena, but tonight she chose to drape herself dramatically in an upside-down fashion across Lena’s lap, which she had been eventually readjusted to correct the flow of blood to not go directly to her head.

    Lena leaned her head against the back of the couch and stared at the side of Kara’s face, who was still intently watching the program about meerkats and their apparent matriarchy. She admired her stony, carved angles from the sharp line of her jaw up to her brow, and down to the slope of her nose. She was beautiful at every angle, and with every expression that her face held. Lena sometimes liked to just watch Kara while she concentrated, because that expression was usually shared with the one that she made whenever she was struck with a determination, or a strong urge to complete a task that perplexed her. The way that her eyes narrowed, and her lips pursed outwardly made Lena’s stomach feel like it was full of excited butterflies desperately beating their wings to escape. The small, adorable lines that bunched in the middle of Kara’s forehead just above her nose simultaneously made her want to run into a puddle on the floor in front of the couch. Her wife was unintentionally, and remarkably gorgeous. But Lena knew this already. She would never tire of being reminded of it, though.

    Lena lifted a hand after a few long and beloved moments of staring at the blonde to smooth the backs of her knuckles along her deliciously smooth jawline. Kara then turned her head to look at her, and spread her lips into a warm smile.

    “Can I ask you something?” Lena whispered.

    Kara nuzzled her face fully into Lena’s palm as a thumb swiped lovingly back and forth there, and after a moment she nodded.

    “Are you and Alex alright?”

    Kara sat silently, her crestfallen eyes giving her away. It was all the confirmation Lena needed.  

    “Was it about Astra?”

    Kara’s temporalis muscle just above her ear rose repeatedly with the clenching of her jaw. A nerve had been struck. Lena gave her the moments after to decide whether or not she wanted to talk about it, to which Kara eventually obliged after a few half-sighed attempts to locate the words.

    “It’s like she automatically jumped to accusing her- Astra of… I don’t know. She just seemed…” Kara’s words fizzled out into a throaty grumble as she shook her head tiredly.

    “Concerned?” Lena offered carefully.

    Kara turned to look at her again, this time with a look of realization. Then she tucked her chin half-heartedly in agreement. “Maybe. It all happened so fast, and I didn’t really get a chance to explain—”

    “You didn’t tell her?” Lena immediately regretted having interrupted her. It was rare, but her surprise had escaped her.

    Kara’s jaw and temporalis began to churn away again, faster now. The blonde stood suddenly, seemingly desperate to remove herself from the way she was feeling at that moment.

    “Let me take Ruby up to bed.” Kara said, gesturing her arms down at the child in Lena’s lap.

    Lena nearly managed to hide her frown as she wiggled to sit up straight and helped a still sleeping Ruby into her wife’s arms. Once hoisted, Lena stood before Kara could turn toward the stairs.

    “Wait...”

    Kara’s eyes could not conceal the misty sheen of tears that rested within them. Lena leaned forward and kissed her on the chin once, and then again at the corner of her mouth. Hovering her face close to Kara’s, she stood there and drank in the blonde’s shallow breaths.

    “Everything’s fine. I love you. Come to bed.” Kara whispered against her top lip.

    Lena couldn’t help but to be worried, knowing her wife’s track record concerning abrupt change and how she had a tendency to retreat inwardly which was oftentimes to her own detriment. Lena worried, as would any loving wife or partner.

    They sank into each other beneath the sheets of their bed not long after, letting their relief flow into one another. Their tactile bond always had a mitigating effect on any emotional or mental turbulence and was often a respite they sought at the end of a long or distressing day. Those moments of disrobing and climbing into each other’s arms never failed to alleviate any stress they had. The mere act of resting her limbs within Kara’s and feeling her warm skin against her own was enough to quell Lena’s burgeoning dread and eventually sent her off into a slumber.

    She’d worry tomorrow. Right now, everything was okay. Just as Kara said.

    Chapter End Notes

    Chapter 6: Goodness, Personified

    Chapter Summary

    A strange encounter leaves Lena feeling anxious, but Kara knows just how to fix it.

    Chapter Notes

    Just saying again for the record that time in this story moves much like that of Lone Star Lover. We'll hop from weeks to months in some areas, with each chapter being a glimpse into both Kara and Lena's lives as things happen. You will see different shades of these characters at different times, some casting more darkness than others, but if you were missing that goofy, endearing, "I will lasso the moon for you" side of our Kara- this chapter may do the trick. NSFW warning for its second half.

    ...why am I even warning y'all at this point? Enjoy the dash of seggsiness (with a pinch of fluff).

    Chapter Six: Goodness, Personified

     

    The air was thick with a stagnant and sticky heat when Lena stepped out into the garage at nine o’clock on a Tuesday morning. Her nostrils quickly filled with a damp, rotted earth smell which screwed her face up with an expression that could have been easily read as mild disgust. The smell wafted heavily toward her from the direction of the lawn mower that was pushed into a corner of the garage; the offensive odor emanating from it more than likely a result of the grass clippings stuck to the underside of its blade well which probably hadn’t dried out yet due to the humidity. Lena tapped her top lip with a curious fingertip as she investigated a bit further before climbing into her car. After their first year of living there, it had only taken one Mexican hat dance with a rather large rat snake in search of water for Lena to be wary of “critters” in this part of their house. Satisfied, Lena spun in her Valentino heels back toward her car and opened her driver’s side door. On hot mornings like this it was like stepping into an oven, which usually earned the walls of the two-car garage a few colorful grumbles about Texas weather. At least it offered shade from the sun, which often without it made the steering wheel too hot to touch for more than a few seconds at a time.

    “But you can bake a sheet of cookies in it if you just set it on the dash and wait fifteen minutes.”  

    Lena giggled at a passing memory of her first summer with Kara as she sat in front of the blasting a/c. While she waited as the cool air pushed into the front seats of her Mercedes, she reminisced about her wife’s endearing way of spinning anything negative on its head with something fun. It was something that had drawn her to Kara from the moment they had met.     Chapter 7: Book Spine and Human Bone

    Chapter Summary

    A five year old Ruby celebrates her birthday with a horse riding lesson. Lena receives a request that quickly turns sour.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Seven: Book Spine and Human Bone

     

    “That’s good, baby girl, but hold ‘em like this—” Kara said calmly as she reached up and adjusted the reins in Ruby’s hands to fit properly. It took a fair amount of time to explain how they were meant to loop inward between her ring and pinky and then back out pinched between her thumb and forefingers, but the now five-year-old caught on quickly. Ruby sat in Ducky’s saddle patiently as Kara then showed her how to slacken the reins by rolling her fingers away from her palms.

    “See, that way they go in and out if you need them to. Long or short. But just hold ‘em up right there and he knows the rest, okay?”

    Ruby nodded. She loved Kara’s horse more than any animal in the world, but this was the first time she had been placed on top of him. Sam had taken the day to visit the stables and witness her daughter’s first ride and was a proud mother indeed, though she barely managed to hide a laugh when she saw the size of Ruby’s eyes once the five-year-old had climbed into the saddle and realized how high up off the ground she was.

    “Okay, just like I showed you.” Kara instructed.

    Ruby focused her line of sight back on Ducky’s withers and after a long moment of what appeared to be hesitation, she clicked her tongue and tapped her heels into his sides where they dangled too high to fit into the stirrups. The gelding simply tossed his head in protest.

    “Try just squeezing with your legs, baby, don’t kick him- that’ll give him a start and send him into a gallop.”

    “What’s a gallop?” Ruby asked innocently.

    The blonde smiled at her endlessly curious niece and threw a thumb over her shoulder toward a nearby track. “You remember earlier when I rode him down the lane and you saw how fast we were going?”

    “Really fast!”

    Kara nodded once with her chin. “That’s a gallop.”

    Ruby mouthed an ‘oh’ and then squared her hips in the saddle, focusing her attention back on the mountain of moving muscle and coarse hair beneath her. She then squeezed with as much strength she had in her little legs, but Ducky still refused to move.

    Come on, Duck…

    Kara knew that he wasn’t giving Ruby a hard time simply because he felt like it. No, he wasn’t that kind of horse. He was gentle and possessed miles of patience, especially for children. He did however seem to appear to want Ruby to give him the proper signals. Kara thought it was endearing in a way. He didn’t seem to be cutting the child any slack perhaps because he recognized her as a miniature version of Kara, or Alex, and seemed to hold her to the same standards as he would either of them.

    “Okay now try pushing him forward with your hips. Remember… like you’re swinging, don’t hop.”

    Ruby quickly added a forward lurch with her hips when she squeezed with her legs again. This time when Ducky’s head tossed upward again, he stepped into a walk.

    “Good! See, he wants you to tell him what to do.”

    Ruby grinned from ear to ear as her lower half dipped from side to side with Ducky’s slow, meandering pace. He had given lessons before to other children about Ruby’s age but seemed to be more mindful of her in particular. Lucky Duckling had known her since before she could walk, after all. As they passed a few stable workers, Ruby dropped the rein in her right hand and waved at them excitedly.

    “Hi! It’s my birthday!”

    The young women unloading a trailers’ worth of canvas feed bags and stall bedding stopped to laugh and wave back, offering her a ‘happy birthday’ in return when Ducky suddenly began to curve right.

    “Don’t drop his reins, Ruby, pick it back up- he thinks you’re turning him.”

    Ruby immediately scooped up the leather and fixed it back into her hand in the proper fashion after quietly muttering an “Oops”.

    “Remember, give him open space with either rein and he’ll turn into it. Tuck in your arms and hold them like an ‘L’, just like I showed you.” The blonde reminded.

    Kara corrected Ducky’s veering direction with a pull of her fingers tucked into the cheekpiece of his bridle. Doing so with any other horse would have resulted in pushback or attitude of some sort, but she knew him to be the most easy-going horse she had ever worked with. Not a lot ruffled his feathers, except when it came to Kara. Ducky had quickly gained a reputation as being “overly protective” of his rider when he refused to let any other stallion, gelding or otherwise, come near her after arriving there five years ago. So much so that he would obnoxiously position himself between the other horses if they were to even notice her.

    “Can we gallop?” Ruby asked plainly.

    Kara shook her head as they continued walking. “Absolutely not.”

    Ruby shot her a downward pout.

    “We can try a trot if you want, but any more than that and your Mamá will tan my hide.”

    “Walk, trot, canter, gallop… walk, trot, canter, gallop—” The five-year-old rattled off the order of horse gaits she had come to learn by age three. Such was her way of amusing herself if any lessons did not fit her rapidly paced intelligence. Kara had come to recognize this behavior and chose to engage her with new knowledge.

    “Is ‘trot’ fast?” Ruby’s voice seemed a bit apprehensive now.

    “No, just a little bouncy.”

    The five-year old’s wide grin quickly returned to her face. “Okay!”

Ruby screeched her excitement when she recognized Lena’s car and was nearly vibrating in her saddle by the time the raven-haired woman made it to the gate of the corral. Ducky’s ears flitted back and forth like excited radars with her squealing, but remained perfectly calm.

    “Auntie Lena, look!” Ruby shouted with a wave. “I trotted him!”

    “Did you, now?!” Lena dropped her chin with an impressed look as they passed by for another lap.

    “Hello there, wee birthday girl!” Are you having fun?” The raven-haired woman asked with a cheerful mask up at Ruby in the saddle.

    “Hug my neck, and I’ll see you next weekend.”
