After a quick embrace, Kara reached up and scooped Ruby out of the saddle to place her and her new leather riding boots onto the dirt. The five-year-old took her mother’s hand with another pout and then headed toward the air-conditioned building. Concerned now, Kara quickly stepped up onto the same rungs of the corral to come face to face with her wife.

    “What’s wrong?”

    Lena sighed and let her forehead fall onto Kara’s strong, sun-kissed shoulder.

    “It was Lex. He said it was an emergency but wouldn’t say what exactly.” She admitted with a small sadness to her voice.

    “It’s okay. Go.” Kara replied.

    Lena lifted her face from Kara’s warm shoulder and gazed into her eyes. “Are you sure?”

    The blonde simply nodded with a small smile. “Go.”

    Kara reassured her as best as she could and gently tucked her wife’s face into the palms of her hands. “We’ll be fine. You saw Ruby on a horse, and that’s all she really wanted.” She then paused for a moment and squinted with a slight tilt of her head. “…And she’s five, she’ll get over it.”

    “I still feel bad.”

    Kara shook her head and pursed her lips. “You’re fine. I promise.”

    Before Lena could protest, Kara pulled her face close and kissed her softly. Lena instinctually leaned further into her, emitting a small noise of relief through her nose. They kissed tenderly a few more times and then stepped down on either side of the corral.

    “I’ll see you at home?” Lena asked as she fished her car keys out of her small handbag.

    Kara wrapped her hand around the crown of a Stetson that hung on a nearby post and fit it back onto her head, tilting its brim forward into a nod. Lena smiled at the blonde’s attractive, old-country style of non-verbal communication and then turned to walk with a quick pace toward her car. Kara knew that it would take maybe less than ten minutes for Ruby to become preoccupied with something else, and sure enough when she stepped into the front door of the building near the stables, she found Sam sitting with her head in her hands as she listened to her five-year-old daughter ramble off different names of horse breeds with lightning speed. If not for the table being in the way, Sam’s jaw would have been on the floor. Kara had never been prouder.

     

     

    Half an hour later, Lena turned her key in the ignition of her car to its off position and stepped back out into the heat. Only moments before, she had fired off a quick text message to Kara and heavily contemplated turning the key again and driving away.

    Why shouldn’t I?

    Lena grumbled lowly to herself and began a slow walk down a river rock and shrubbery lined pathway toward the library.

    Because I wouldn’t be able to live with myself if I did, that’s why.

    She knew the guilt of disappearing on her brother after he had so frantically requested to see her would eat away at her until naught but bone was left, so really, she wasn’t left with any choice. Her somewhat spiteful reasonings that he had been the first to disappear on her did little to assuage her decision. She eventually turned a corner to find him pacing agitatedly back and forth near the outdoor fountain near the library’s front doors. He swiped his palms at his forehead one at a time to keep the beads of sweat that perspired there from rolling into his eyes.  

    Lena was suddenly glad to be wearing flats instead of heels and began to feel very uneasy as she approached him.

    “Lex, what’s going on?”

    The disheveled man turned and softened the pained features of his face a bit when he laid his eyes upon her. Lena could see where the sweat from his brow ran through the wrinkled worry-lines of his face, which were now exaggerated more than ever before.

    “That favor…” He clasped his hands together flat in front of him, as if in prayer. “I need it.”

    “Okay.” Lena responded without missing a beat. She had no idea what came over her, but her instinct to help her brother at least calm himself a little bit won out. “What is it?”

    “I need a loan from ye, deirfiúr.”

    Lena tossed her eyebrows in surprise. Although if she were being honest with herself, she wasn’t all that surprised. She did after all have the unnerving feeling that this was coming. Just hearing him say it out loud, much like she said she would have preferred him to, still ended up catching her off-guard somehow. It surely didn’t help with his worrisome behavior; the pacing, the sweating, and the wringing of his hands…

    “We can… talk about that, sure. How much would you be needing?”

    “One hundred and twenty.” He replied blankly.

    Lena waited for the other shoe to drop, assuming it was there. She then lifted a prominent eyebrow as if to urge him to just come out with it.

    “Thousand.”

    There it is.

    “Lex, what in the name of St. Matthew do you need with one hundred and twenty thousand dollars?” Lena asked exasperatedly.

    Her brother merely continued wringing his hands together nervously and appeared to be almost wholly unconcerned with trying to conceal his anxiety by this point.

    “It’s better if you don’t know.” He answered after a long pause.

    Lena shook her head furiously. “See, that is where you lose me—”

    “Baby sister, please—” Lex begged as he took a couple of hurried steps toward her, to which she backed away from him reactively with her hands held out in caution.

    “Now wait just a minute…” Lena began. “You don’t get to just traipse back into my life with the promise of reconnection and then ask me for one hundred and twenty thousand dollars. I want to help you, but if you can’t tell me what it’s for, then the answer is no. That’s a lot of money, Lex.”

    The man suddenly stopped his incessant, anxious movements then and his demeanor shifted so drastically that it set a chill into her bones. That intense feeling to dart and run had come back tenfold.

    “Aye, but you can drop three quarter of a million on a car shop for yer mot? Tell me, how many tyres d’ya need to plug to pay some’tin like that back?”

    With that, it was impossible for Lena to shield the hurt look on her face. How did he even know about the shop, or the money that she had put up for it to get on its feet? That hurt inside of her quickly morphed into a defensive position.

    “She is my wife—”

    “Is mise do dheartháir!”

    His voice was just below a shout but instead of appearing scared or startled, Lena simply exhaled a frustrated breath through her nose and reached with her hands to clutch at each of her elbows.

    “Lex, let’s not—”

    “Or does she pay it back another way?”

    “Is minic a bhris béal duine a shrón, and you would do well to remember that when you speak about Kara in my presence from now on if you ever wish to see me again.”

The smell of tobacco and sweat clouded her nostrils as she glared into her brother’s eyes, who was staring back at her just as intensely. He was an immovable force, unwavering in his stance. Finally, he took a step back and spit onto the ground next to where they stood. There was a long and uncomfortable pause between them before he opened his mouth to speak again.

    “He was vicious after he brought you home. D’ya know that?”

    Lex’s words struck her as if she were a deer in headlights. There could be only one person in the world of whom he was speaking.

    “He said you’d never carry the full Luthor name, but you had it anyway, didn’t you? Blood of my blood.” He spit again, this time with less precision. “Dad said you were a mistake, and yet you were there anyway, weren’t you?”

    “You don’t mean that…” She pleaded, trapping a sob in her chest. The pain there radiated outward toward her limbs, and her knees began to judder helplessly.

    “Oh I do, though. You, with your fancy car and your fancy job and your nice house, all prim and perfect like always. ”

    “Stop, please—"

    “Don’t you worry about me, baby sister. I’ll be just fine without’ye. If you’re really just livin’ big front and slope back, don’t worry, I’ll be away now.” He snarled, and then turned on his heels to step into a long stride away from the rushing water of the fountain.

    Despite her shattered heart, Lena felt something unexpected… she wanted to explode. All of the pain and hurt she felt funneled into a raw, and unexplored feeling that she wanted to set loose upon the world. And most of all, upon him.

    “What, so that’s it? You’re just going to vanish again?” Lena called out to him exasperatedly, but not without a small, unmistakable twinge of panic in her voice.

    He rounded the tall hedge at the path to the library’s entrance and disappeared into the distance, not even bothering to slow his pace or show any sign of remorse whatsoever.

    “Lex!”

    The nape of her neck tingled with a burning feeling just beneath her skin and her heart began to thunder so hard inside of her ribs that it hurt. She winced when she felt her fingertips digging small, half-moon shaped cuts deep into the flesh of her palms.

    How could a person just do that, like it’s nothing? Like I’m nothing?

    Her chest began to heave up and down with heavy, distraught breaths.

    Am I nothing?!

    She wanted to scream, cry, and then vomit. But first… she had to get home. There she’d be safe to disassemble her broken pieces and lay them out carefully. Kara would be home soon to put her back together, that much she was sure of.

     

     

    The gold miners dishpan that sat on the skinny table of their entryway rang out with the sound of keys being tossed into it. The shallow, tin pan had been a gift from Lena for their first wedding anniversary and commemorated the wealth of love they possessed for each other. It had also come with a story of Irish immigrants who flocked to Texas in the 1850’s during the height of the gold rush. Kara admired its deep sentimental and historical value, and had placed it strategically in their home where it greeted her whenever she came home every day.

    “Babe?” Kara called out into the expanse of the seemingly empty house.

    The blonde peered into seating area that lay before the tall, glass paned walls that turned on their central axes into their large back yard. She knew her wife sometimes liked to read at dusk on the lantern lit deck just beyond, but she was nowhere to be found. She then silently scratched the back of her slightly sunburned neck as she turned on her feet to continue in her search.

Lena’s car had been parked outside, so she knew she had to be home. A feeling of uneasiness began to come over her, as if the universe or somewhere beyond were telling her that something was very, very wrong. Half-way up the stairs, Kara stopped the movement of her feet to hold herself still and listen to the small, pitiful noises coming from somewhere just past the landing above her. With her hand on the railing, she launched herself forward. Quiet, broken sobs came from the end of the hall upstairs, and Kara’s head turned on a swivel toward the small library that lived across from their bedroom. It was a place of joy for her wife, who often found solace in the hundreds of tomes that were collected there over the years. But tonight, instead of the spectacularly happy, bookish woman who habitually leaned into the thousands of pages that slumbered peacefully upon its shelves, it held a crumpled, weeping mess of a thing curled helplessly into one of its round armchairs.

    “Lena…” Kara exclaimed in surprise as she lunged toward her. Dark hair curtained the woman’s face, hiding her from the world as she cried into her hands. A discarded book at her feet looked up with its worn, hard cover and held a title that read “The Lost Princess”, which Kara recognized as one of her wife’s favorites. She also knew it to be a story which Lena read to self-soothe during times of woe or worry. She had a book for every feeling, and Kara knew them all.

    “What’s wrong- Lena, tell me what’s happened…” The blonde urged her as she bent forward, heart racing.

    Lena shook her head in between pitiful sobs and then leaned out of the chair to wrap her arms around Kara’s shoulders, falling with all of her weight into her. Kara then took a knee to the floor. That intense, protective part of her nature began to do battle with the part of her that wanted to be scared, which, those two aspects of Kara’s personality typically went hand in hand. One fed the other, and so forth. She’d throttle the thing that hurt the person she loved, and nobody would be able to stop her. Lena clung to the blonde limply and tucked her face into the crook of her neck to mask her persistent weeping, for her chest could not cease its caving in no matter how hard she appeared to try.

    “You’re scaring me… are you hurt?” Kara begged.

    The raven-haired woman managed to shake her head side to side where it was safely nestled into Kara’s neck. Kara then breathed a silent sigh of relief through her nose. Instead of purging any more information from her wife until she was good and ready to do so, Kara then folded each of Lena’s arms into her chest and pulled her down to the floor with her, slipping her out of the leather of the armchair like a glove from a hand.

    Finally, some sniffles escaped from beneath her chin. Kara reached forward and pulled Lena’s legs into her lap, wrapping her arms completely around her from shoulder to waist. She made her body a sturdy resting place for her, one that Lena knew she was safe to tether herself to. Should the house fall to rack and ruin, they would still be there. Together.

    Kara hummed empathetically in her chest for a moment. Now that her initial panic had subsided, she realized that it was her wife’s estranged, criminal half-brother who was to blame for this. This, the woman she loved more than anything in the world crying haggardly amongst her books. The blonde then felt an instant and sickening desire to put her fist through the middle of his face. She didn’t like the sudden and unexpected image of violence which flashed across her eyes, and she chose to put it from her mind immediately.

    “It feels like I’ve lost him all over again.” Lena confessed past another aftershock of a sob. “What do I do with the way this feels, Kara? I don’t want it.”

    Kara cooed her quietly, running her fingers through straight, silky hair. Her instinct rose again to fix things, but if there was an answer to that question, she didn’t have it. Though, she desperately wanted to find the person that did. She instead took solace in the fact that one of the biggest reasons why the two of them fit so well together was that in every way that she was reserved and unspoken about her own feelings, Lena was not. Her wife had no difficulty sharing how she felt whenever she needed. Her fears, her anxieties… it was something that made the blonde feel safe in her presence. And for Lena, simply knowing that she could express the things that sometimes clawed their way out of her without Kara bolting in the other direction was a constant source of comfort for her. They held each other up like this; an equal force of give and take. Their equilibrium was in the way that they ran to each other. She would let Lena pour her hurt and her strife into her for as long as she needed, because she knew that while her wife may not be able to weather this storm alone… they could together.

    Chapter End Notes

    Translations:

    "Macushla" = a phonetic English spelling of "Mo chuisle" which literally means "My pulse/vein/heart"
    "Deirfiúr" = Sister
    "Mot" = Slang for "Girlfriend"
    "Is mise do dheartháir" = "I am your brother"
    "Is minic a bhris béal duine a shrón" = "Many a time a man's mouth broke his nose"
    "Big front and slope back" = Slang for appearing to live with money, but having none.

    Edited the name of Lena’s book title to “The Lost Princess” as a hat tip to Lex’s pet name for her (as per the CW tv series canon).

    Chapter 8: Mild Discomfort

    Chapter Summary

    Lena busies her mind (and her hands) to avoid an emotional toll. Kara visits with Astra again. An attempt to bridge a gap between sisters is made.

    Chapter Notes

    And so begins the emotional metamorphosis herein.

    Chapter Eight: Mild Discomfort

Every spare moment she had she dedicated to emailing, scheduling, reviewing, and anything else she could find, sometimes leaving her newly hired assistant to float around the office in search of things to keep herself busy. Lena had spent more time in her office downtown than she had at home lately, and Kara had begun to notice.

    With one foot out of the door and into the garage, something caught her by the wrist and swiftly pulled her back onto the tile of the kitchen, spinning her on the points of her heels like a top from a string.

    “Woah now, you weren’t thinkin’ of leaving without saying goodbye, were you?” Kara asked with a devilish grin as she pulled Lena tightly around her waist and began to peck small kisses on the underside of her sharp jaw. Lena smiled and dropped her leather bookbag on the floor gently with a quiet -flop-.

    “Mmm, how could I?” Lena replied with a pleasant tone to her voice to mask her guilt. If she was being honest, she had forgotten that Kara was still home. Lena had been so eager to start her day and to drown herself in campaign proposals and numbers that she was very much in the process of leaving the house without a word to her wife. However unintentional it may have been, she still felt sorry for it. She knew Kara would of course forgive her and would more than likely insist that it was nothing to fuss over to begin with, but still it left her with a dull, pestiferous pain in her chest.

Damp, golden tresses fell to the tops of strong shoulders and left behind tiny water droplets on the smooth, sun kissed skin that lived there. Some pretty harsh words had been exchanged with her sister that were just the tip of the ‘extended family tree and its accompanying issues’ iceberg, as well as how bothered she had been by the brutal not once, but twice abandonment that her wife had suffered via her criminally insane half-brother-in-law… Things had definitely taken a strange turn as of late, and right now she felt almost nothing.

     

    That was what worried her.

    Chapter End Notes

    I've decided on Tuesday and Saturday updates from here on out, so check back this weekend for Chapter 9. (The metaphorical shit will be hitting the proverbial fan, so to speak.) Buckle up!

    Chapter 9: Hell Itself

    Chapter Summary

    Kara makes a stressful decision at work. Lena's office is thrown into chaos.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Nine: Hell Itself

     

    August brought some of the hottest days they had so far that year with temperatures upwards of one hundred degrees for over a week at a time. A heat advisory from the national weather service scrolled across the bottom of the mounted tv screen in the lobby of Danvers Rescue & Repair that morning as Kara watched and sipped her iced coffee. It was a drink she didn’t care for that much for how sweet it was, but it was far too hot to drink regular coffee and she needed the caffeine. The watch face on the inside of her wrist read fifteen past ten o’clock, and she glanced out the window into the parking lot to find that Nia still hadn’t shown up for work.

    The barely caffeinated blonde grumbled to herself as she walked past the front desk where Brainy sat with his head in his hands, sulking at the computer screen in front of him.

    “Good morning.” She said after a quick sip of her sickeningly sweet, syrupy coffee drink.

    “What’s good about it…” He muttered under his breath, not so much a question as it was a statement of fact.

    Kara reared her head back with surprise and in an almost comical gesture, spun around on one boot heel to face him.

    “What’s eating you?” She asked. She cared, of course, but was altogether doubtful that he would offer up what it was that had him so downtrodden. He looked as though his best friend had run off with his girl, his truck broke down, his dog ran away, or any number of opening lines from popular country songs.

    Brainy sat up straight in his rolling chair and put on the best smile he could muster, releasing a long and slow exhale through his pointed nose. He then shook his head and tightened his close-lipped smile further before answering. “Nothing that can’t be fixed.”

    Kara nodded, satisfied. “I like that.” She said with a snap of her fingers into a point. “Let me know if you need anything.”

    The young man turned in his chair with another pained smile and tapped away at the keyboard with one hand as he reached with the other for the phone to begin confirming appointments for the day. With that, it didn’t take long for Kara to put two and two together… Nia was late and Brainy was sad. Both of which were firsts for either of them, as far as work was concerned.

    Just then the sound of a car door slammed out in the parking lot and Kara turned to see Nia walking a slow, somewhat awkward pace toward the first bay door with a bag slung partly over her shoulder. She seemed to struggle with a thermos in one hand while the other clumsily adjusted a big pair of gaudy sunglasses on her heart shaped face. The blonde heaved a relieved sigh and moved through the lobby door into the shop to greet the younger woman as she hobbled in.

    “Hey, everything alright?” Kara asked with a calm voice, wheeling to a stop on the balls of her feet. She possessed a wealth of patience on most days, but as a business owner, she did every once in a while have to assert her role as ‘boss’, which was pretty much the only thing about the job that she wasn’t overly fond of.

    “Yeah- yeah. Sorry, I know I’m late.” Nia replied, lazily dropping her bag and thermos onto a cabinet. Kara tilted her head with an air of curiosity when the drowsy looking technician didn’t bother to remove the large pair of sunglasses from her face. She was, after all, inside the shop and the sun was no longer in her eyes. 

    “That’s fine.” Kara began. “You’re just going to be a little backed up for most of the day… they’re your flag hours. But are you feelin’ okay? You look a lil’ wobb—”

    Just as the words left her mouth, Nia stumbled over her own feet and nearly went to her knees as she reached for the lift controls.

    “Woah—” Kara exclaimed as she lunged forward quickly and caught her by an elbow. “I’ll take that as a no...”

    Nia shook her head, tossing the small, beaded braids that were tied across the side of her head that matched her grunge, almost punk style of accessorizing. Nia Nal dripped in ripped denim, chain and studded leather, making her appear standoffish in nature… but for those that knew her, knew better. A light blue, light pink and white Trans Pride pin always donned the pocket flap of her work shirts. Her style of dress spoke volumes of her personality; She was defiant, though not always to a fault. Kara had a mind to convince Nia to remove the amalgam of “Don’t mess with me” bracelets and rings Nia wore when she had first hired her, which one close call with a timing chain was apparently all it took for the young rocker technician to acquiesce and agree not to wear them while on the clock.

    “I’m just a little hungover, I’ll eat something and drink lots of water.”

    “Hungover? Try still drunk.” Kara quipped. This was the last thing she needed today. Though frustrated, the blonde did her best to proceed calmly and with respect for her employees having their own lives with their own set of problems. “I need you to take the day and sleep it off.”

    Nia dropped her mouth open and pulled her sunglasses down over her nose as she looked at Kara, bewildered. Dark circles hung below her bloodshot eyes.

    “What? Wait, I need these jobs today…”

    Kara hated that she had to do this. She wanted her employees to like her, which made her feel silly at times and was something that often plagued her mind, but regardless of that, she wanted them to feel comfortable around her and to enjoy their jobs. Most of all, she wanted them to be safe. She just couldn’t have Nia work like this. With all of the heavy machinery and pressurized gases that were an essential part of regular vehicle maintenance, not to mention the risk involved of breaking parts or causing damage to either a customer’s car or to shop equipment, it was in the young technician’s best interest that she just go home as ordered.

    “I can’t let you work like this.” Kara continued with a shake of her head.

    “I’ll be fine, I promise—" Nia protested desperately.

    “I’m sorry.”

    The younger woman voiced her disappointment with a frustrated groan and wheeled around to swipe at her bag and thermos.

    “Thanks a lot.”

    Kara immediately furrowed her brow into a defensive position.

    Now wait a minute…

    “What are you mad at me for?” The blonde started with a harshness to her voice. “You’re the one who decided to come to work still drunk as a skunk- you think I’m just gonna let you crush yourself under a seven-and-a-half-ton truck lift? Get outta here and take care of yourself.”

    Nia blinked at her in shock. Kara had never been stern like that before with any of her techs. She had never had cause to before, as they were all reasonably well adjusted and hardworking people that she had come to call friends of hers. Nia raised her hands in surrender and bowed her head in deference.

    “I’m sorry, you’re right. Thank you…”

    What is it with everyone I care about laying into me lately…?

    I’m just doing my damn best here.

    It was either too early still or too hot out for Kara to calm down completely, so instead of sticking around to exchange any more words, she stepped back inside of the lobby toward the front office and hollered across its floor over at Brainy.

    “Brain, do me a favor and drive Nia back home for the day.”

    He popped his head up with a petrified look on his face as the color ran from it, raising a hand to insert his own protest when Kara cut him off. She really didn’t want to hear it right now.

    “Just, please… do as I ask. And whatever the hell kind of mess you two got goin’ on, fix it and then come right back. I’m gonna teach you how to empty an oil pan today, you’ll get paid for the labor.”

    Clearly defeated, Brainy nodded politely and then grabbed his set of keys before he walked into the shop to collect his girlfriend. Kara stood there in the lobby and shifted her weight onto her right leg as she pinched the bridge of her nose with a long sigh. It was going to be a long day.


     

    “Okay love- no, no it’s fine.” Lena said into her cell phone as she stood in the middle of her office with a hand palming anxiously at one of her elbows. She had called to see if she could spend her lunch with Kara like they had been doing for a few weeks in a row now, but was sad to learn that her wife would need to work through lunch and would also likely be home late due to sending Nia home for the day.

    The raven-haired woman frowned as she listened to Kara’s guilty rambling of an apology. It wasn’t the fact that she was disappointed with not being able to stop by and see her, she wished instead that she could reach through the phone and hug her. She didn’t like Kara to unnecessarily place blame upon herself; a bad habit that had lessened over the years, but still lingered in the shadows. Old habits die hard, and when they’re actually trauma responses, they can seem almost impossible to get rid of. Still, Lena reassured her as best as she could that it was fine and that she’d see her at home later that night.

    A small tap on her office door to the left caught her attention just then. Eve stood there with one arm elbowing the door open and tucked into the crook of the other was a plain brown, haphazardly taped cardboard box. Lena gestured at her to enter with a toss of her chin as she ended her call with Kara.

    “Okay. I love you too.” Lena finished in a hushed voice and then tapped the red end call button on her cell phone screen.

    Eve strode over quickly and placed the box on Lena’s desk with a small -clunk-. Lena crooked her head curiously at the package, not knowing whether or not if she was expecting any special deliveries that day. Eve simply shrugged back at her, just as confused.

    “Someone left it at the information desk on the ground floor- it just says ‘Luthor Worldwide’ on it in marker there…” The petite blonde pointed out.

    “Strange. Was there a letter attached?” Lena asked, picking up the box in both hands and turning it sideways to read its blank sides. It had been messily taped together and indeed seemed to only read the name of her company with no street or return address.

    Eve shook her head. “No. The desk just called and said it was there.”

    The raven-haired woman turned it once more onto its opposite side, and upon doing so something inside rolled awkwardly with its weight and landed against the side of the box with a solid -thunk-. That was when the faint ticking noise began.

    Lena froze.

    “What’s—”

    “Stop- shh, stop…” Lena interrupted Eve with a shaky finger, and lifted the package closer to listen. The ticking noise grew louder as she hovered an ear over the top of the box. Her initial reaction was to panic, as logic quickly dictated that strange packages with no return addresses were not supposed to make ticking sounds.

    “Listen to me very carefully…” Lena said as she placed the box back onto the desk cautiously. “We need to clear the floor and alert the rest of the building to evacuate. Now.”

    The shorter blonde woman nodded in agreement, offering no complaint or question about her orders. It was clear by the look on Lena’s face that something was terribly, terribly wrong.

    “Run.”

     

     

    The impact drill buzzed loudly in Kara’s hands as she tightened the lug nuts on the last wheel of the car currently on lift three. Thankfully, they had stocked this car’s front set of brake pads and hadn’t needed to make the customer wait on parts. What Kara thought was going to be a long and arduous day had seemed to run rather smoothly. She ran an efficient shop, which was something she was consistently proud of. With the last nut snug to her satisfaction, she released the trigger on the drill and heard the sound of her name being called out from the office at the end of the long shop.

    “Kara! Phone!”

    The blonde swiped her brow with a red shop towel before turning to toss it onto a cabinet behind the lift.

    “Be there in a sec!”

    She was due for a break anyway as she had been running full throttle since that morning. Kara staggered into the lobby tiredly, pulling her latex diamond-grip gloves from her hands one at a time. Brainy sat in his rolling chair behind the front desk with a wild, almost terrified look on his face.

    “You have to go…” He mumbled away from the phone, almost incoherently.

    Kara quirked her eyebrows at him anxiously. Something in the pit of her stomach panged, making her instantly feel very uncomfortable.

    “What is it? Who is that?” She asked nervously.

    “It’s your sister…” He said past an efforted gulp and stuck the phone in his hand out at her. The sound of Alex shouting through the phone was clear as day even before she brought it to rest against her ear.

    “Kara! You need to head toward downtown right now.”

    “Alex? What’s wrong…?” Kara begged, officially scared.

    “Just get here to Lena’s office building and hurry.”

    “Wait a minute—"

    “Come on, Kara!”

    “Stop!” Kara shouted. “Somebody needs to tell me what’s going on right now!” The few people sitting at the opposite end of the lobby turned their heads toward the sound of her voice.

    Brainy tapped his hand on Kara’s elbow then and pointed with his other at the tv. “I think it’s that…”

    The breaking news bulletin flashed across the screen with the words ‘active bomb threat’ and live helicopter footage of Lena’s building. Suddenly, every organ inside of her wanted to plummet to the floor as bile rose to the back of her throat. Kara then shifted her attention back to her sister on the other end of the phone.

    “Where’s Lena?!”

    Alex’s voice came through again. “Just get here as fast as you can.”

    Kara’s body jumped into action. Every second she wasted after that felt like the end of the world. She tossed the phone back at Brainy who caught it clumsily against his chest, and by the time she had jogged back to her office to grab her keys and was running through the lobby again towards her truck outside, he was standing in the center of the large room announcing that they were closing early and that they would reopen as soon as they could.

    Once in the driver’s seat of her pickup, Kara threw the shift lever hard into reverse, flinging gravel in the add-on parking lot in front of the shop before pulling the lever downward into drive. She was determined to get there as fast as she could burn the rubber on her tires once they hit city pavement.

     

     

    Firetrucks and other emergency vehicles blocked an almost a four-block radius of the Downtown district of Austin, which Kara soon realized that she wouldn’t be able to make it any further in her truck past Congress and Ninth. She didn’t know how long it took her to get there from the east side of the city where her shop was located, but she wouldn’t have been surprised if she had made record time.

    Uniformed officers were waving her away as her truck approached the intersection and she tried her best to lean out of her driver’s window to tell them that she needed to get through, but they quickly turned and ignored her. They did have a job to do, but Kara needed to get past them, and she wasn’t about to wait around and ask for their permission. A quick turn of her steering wheel pointed her tires toward a curb as she rolled alongside it and then cut the engine.

    She resigned to make it the rest of the way to Seventh street on foot. But how was the question. While the police were distracted with their directing of passing traffic, Kara saw that she could either run completely around the block the other way to avoid them… or run right past them. She weighed her options quietly as she minded the -pop- of her driver’s door hinge, shutting it softly with her back.

    Taking a few seconds longer to ponder how to proceed, Kara decided to throw caution to the wind. Running around would take too long. She needed to be there now.

    With their backs still turned to her, Kara bolted into a sprint on the sidewalk and twisted her shoulders to split in between the two officers who yelled for her to stop immediately. Speed and agility were on her side. A small twinge of guilt urged her to shout back over her shoulder at them as she continued running. “Sorry!”

    Her boots thundered on the uneven pavement of the sidewalk as she quickly passed street after street. Downtown Austin was a tight fit and a four-block stretch while sprinting took a surprisingly short time to traverse, especially when there was no vehicle traffic. At one point she passed another group of uniforms who didn’t pay her any mind at all. In an effort to keep her forward momentum, she hooked her hand around a lamp post to swing left onto Guadalupe Street and continued to run as fast as her legs would take her over grate, brick and pavement alike.

    Two more blocks… keep going…

    All of the sudden the combined sounds of shouting, police radio, and emergency bull horns hit her like a Mack truck.

    West Seventh Street was absolute chaos.

    Kara slowed to a stop to catch her breath momentarily when she caught sight of the front of Lena’s office building. Unable to see anything else past the armored trucks and ambulances, she moved her feet forward again and finally saw the shimmer of reddish-brown hair in between two police cruisers that were parked in the middle of the street. The blonde broke into a run again and had just ducked under a strip of yellow police tape when a pair of hands grabbed her by the shoulders, bringing her to an abrupt halt and nearly off of her feet entirely.

    “Hey, you can’t go that way- get back behind the line!” A voice shouted behind her as she struggled to rip herself free.

    “Alex- Alex!” Kara yelled for her sister as loud as she could over the cacophony of competing harsh noises that surrounded them. “Let go!” She struggled again against the hands that held her back and managed to slip free, but for only a second before they caught her again and pulled hard, bending one of her arms behind her back forcefully.

    “Stop!” She shouted with a pained grunt as her arm bent further. “My wife’s in there- let go! Alex!”

    Her screaming had been so loud that Alex was able to hear it just then, and she whipped her head around toward her. With wide, panicked eyes, Alex waved her hands to get the officer’s attention. “Hey! Let her through, she’s with me!”

    Kara took her arm back with a wince as soon as it was released and broke into a short run again, weaving in and out of the large crowd of both civilians and law enforcement. Alex stopped her with a hand on her shoulder as soon as she reached her.

    “Damn you got here fast…”

    “Where’s Lena?” Kara said in between gasps as she doubled over with her hands on her knees.

    “She hasn’t come out yet—”

    “What?!”

    “Kara listen—” Her sister braced both of her shoulders now and squeezed. “The elevators couldn’t take the stress of ten floors of people trying to all escape at the same time… they’re stuck. One car is trapped on the third floor and the other on the sixth.”

    The blonde’s chest began to rise and fall quickly, much more than that of just having run several city blocks at top speed; It smacked of an impending panic attack. Alex leaned further down to speak more clearly and squeezed Kara at her shoulders again to ground her.

    “I haven’t seen her yet, but we’ve still got people coming down out of the stairwells. I need you to be here if- when she comes out, but I need you to stay calm to do that. Okay? Look at me—”

    Kara craned her neck further to lift her gaze and lock eyes with her sister. Her heart wanted to burst with how hard it was beating inside of her chest and her ribs were on fire, not to mention how sick to her stomach she felt at that moment.

    “Can you do that?” Alex asked with a sobering grit.

    The youngest Danvers nodded and swallowed past the painful knot that all but arrested her throat. “What happened?” Kara asked past a dry cough that she hacked into one of her curled fists.

    Alex’s hands fell to her thighs, smacking them exasperatedly as she shook her head. “All we know is that someone called in a bomb threat and then people panicked and started climbing over each other, it’s fuckin’ nuts out here—"

    “Danvers! Bomb squad’s here but they can’t get through!” A voice yelled out to her from a few cars over.

    “Shit- I have to go…” Alex said as she turned to run toward the direction in which the voice came from.

    “Wait!” Kara shouted after her, unsure of what to do next.

    Alex stopped and quickly yelled back to her, “Keep your eyes on the outside stairwell door in the east alley, there’s another on the other side of the building towards Eighth!”. Then she disappeared into the crowd in seconds.

    Kara began to move frantically through the hundreds of people in the street closer toward the front of the building. It took time to shoulder her way past the frenzied swarm of pedestrians moving in the opposite direction. It was like she was a salmon desperately trying to swim upstream, against a strong opposing current. Once at the final barricade in front of the building, she could hear several fire alarms churning their ear-splitting alert over and over again from the inside. A constant flow of terrified people poured out of the alley where Alex said the emergency stairwell door was located. She must have screamed Lena’s name a hundred times with her eyes trained on the alley, afraid to blink even once in case she missed her.

    Minutes crawled like hours.

    There wasn’t a single drop of shade anywhere on the street, and Kara was pressed too far toward the front of the crowd against the barricade to move back now, or anywhere for that matter. People would soon start dropping like flies from heat exhaustion, which would be an entirely separate danger apart from a suspected bomb. She was stuck there, with the only place to go being forward over the barricade, which at this point was beyond tempting to disregard. Kara was just about to put a leg across when she saw Alex again to her right ushering through about half a dozen men in large, padded gear. A yellow Labrador was leashed to the person standing in front of the group and Alex was busily yelling over the clamor of the crowd to speak into the side of their helmet. Moments later, another group wearing Fire & Rescue uniforms quickly descended upon her. It was as if everyone needed her attention all at once. Kara squinted under a hand shielding her eyes from the blaring sun and could see that Alex was overwhelmed but was no doubt doing the best that she could with what she had. Once there was a break in communication, Alex finally caught sight of the blonde again and jogged over to stand on the other side of the barricade.

    “What’s going on?” Kara hollered.

    “We can’t get the ‘okay’ from the bomb squad chief to send in the K-9 team until they know that the building is clear, but we know that it’s not because we still have a live feed of the cameras inside the stalled elevators…” Alex took a quick breath and pulled at the collar of her shirt. “…And we can’t get them out because the Fire Department can’t move in until the bomb threat is called off, but we need the damn dog for that!” She replied angrily, throwing her hands in the air.

    “And apparently, I’m the only one on-site right now. I can’t reach my sergeant or our unit chief and everyone’s asking me what to do…” Alex rambled with her distress, her chest heaving up and down as she turned back and forth on her feet.

    “Alex, you’re doing great, just breathe…”

    “Kara, I can’t do this!”

    “Yes, you can! I need you to.” The blonde insisted, moving her hands over her sister’s ears to calm her from the noisy chaos around them. Kara leaned her forehead over the barricade and rested it on her sister’s, whose brow was slick with sweat. “Relax and think.”

    If her sister was about to panic, then there was absolutely no hope for herself. She’d share what little sanity she still possessed if it meant keeping her sister about her wits. They stood there for a moment, tuning themselves out from the pandemonium that surrounded them until Alex jumped with a thought and turned her head toward a nearby crowd of Fire & Rescue workers who were huddled over the hood of a car.

    “Hey! Do y’all still have that tablet with the building blueprints? Bring it here!” She ordered, beckoning them to come closer with a wave of her hand.

    “The fire department has pdf’s of every building at or over ten floors in the city…” Alex said as they rushed to hand her the device. She continued to explain as the men huddled to stand in front of her now, “If we can move past the people coming down the stairs up to the fourth and seventh floors, we can crack those elevator doors open and drop down onto the cars to pull them out. The package is on the west end of the building and the elevators are down the middle here…” She stuck her forefinger at each point of entry as she tapped back and forth through the floors. She just had to convince the Fire Chief that this was the only feasible plan they had without wasting any more time, and Alex knew that he was just as likely to shut her down to keep his men safe. She had to try.

    “Bring me your chief and get suited up, we have to make this work to get the explosives dog inside.”

    The men hollered loudly in agreement and pounded their chests and helmets, revving themselves up to get the job done. After another moment to run through the plan once more with the Fire Chief, he nodded his approval and the men hurried off toward the alley to begin their ascent up the stairs and into the building.

    “Two of you take the pneumatic jaws up the stairs, I don’t want one of you trying to be a tough guy and end up taking it by yourself. Get to the fourth floor and start with car one on the south side, and then move to car two.” Alex called out after them.

    “How many people have come out? I still don’t see Lena…” Kara said with a broken voice.

    The youngest Danvers woman wanted to vomit, but that would have taken precious time away from watching the alley where people continued to exit. Alex slicked her hair back with both of her hands as she drew in a long breath through her nose.

    “The blueprints say that each floor has enough square footage to hold less than two hundred people and there’s ten floors total… that puts capacity at two thousand bodies and there’s nowhere for them to go down here.” Alex wheeled around to look at all of the people behind the barricade. It looked as if they had been packed in tightly like sardines. “These streets are too narrow for this many people…” Her voice trailed off with her despair.

    While her sister’s words echoed inside of her head, it was all Kara could do to keep from jumping over the barricade and into the building. She knew that Lena should have come out by now. The sickening feeling in the pit of her stomach that told her something was very, very wrong only grew worse with each agonizing second. Kara stood with her heart in her throat, watching and listening to her sister talk into her radio for several more minutes until she saw her bounce on her heels and pump a fist once into the air. Alex had a smile on her face this time as she turned to speak to her again.

    “Okay- the Sparkies are following the fire department in with the dog in now, they’ll go floor by floor—"

    All of the sudden, a deafening -boom- came from above their heads. An explosion had blown the thick tempered glass outward from the building in sections at the fifth floor.

    Exactly where Lena’s office was.

    “No!” Kara shouted as her body shot forward. In one swift action, she leapt over the barricade with one hand but was caught by her sister with the other and dragged back over it, kicking and screaming. “No! No, let me go!” Her screams began to break with devastated sobs. “Lena!”

    “Look out!” A nearby voice shouted just seconds before a large piece of spider webbed glass crashed onto the ground dangerously close to them. Several pieces of building debris and glass segments began to smack the roofs of the vehicles on the street as they fell from the gaping hole that was once the wall of Lena’s office. Instinctively, Alex pulled Kara hard to duck behind a fire engine until the sound of pelting rubble and smashing glass ceased. The blonde shook as her knees buckled and sank down the side of the truck to land clumsily onto her folded legs. That creeping, existential dread had just come face to face with reality. The love of her life and her reason for living; gone in seconds.

    Sharp, pointed ends of auto glass that littered the ground beneath her bit into the palms of her hands like tiny little needles. The air from her lungs refused to return and her ears rang with a piercing, high-pitched tone from the strength of the blast, but she didn’t care. All of it paled in comparison to the hot, twisting knife that burrowed its way deeper into her heart.

    Gone?

    Gone.

    Gone...

    Kara felt nothing and everything all at once. Where the skin on the inside of her hands were cut, she felt no pain; only that the wounds were there. Her body could still feel things, but nothing hurt more than her heart. A minute longer felt like a lifetime, and what she thought was her own trembling was actually the force of her sister shaking her by the arms.

    “Kara, look…!”

    Blinking through her tears, the blonde lifted her eyes toward a small number of people running in her direction from across the barricade. Dark, raven colored hair in a high pony swished in the distance. Kara squinted further, more hesitant than she had ever been in her life to dare hope.

    Lena…?

    Kara was on her feet in seconds. No human being nor wooden barricade stood a single chance of keeping her away now. With a lunge, a vault and a short sprint, Kara crashed into Lena and lifted her off of her feet with a broken cry.

    “Oh my God! Oh my God…” The blonde repeated, squeezing her tightly. It surely would take an act of God to separate them now, because Kara wanted to hold her like that for the rest of her life.

    Forget eating or sleeping. Just this, forever.

    “Kara! You’re here…” Lena said past her frantic weeping.

    If Kara were able to wrap her arms around her twice, even three times, she would have. She simply couldn’t feel her enough. After thinking that she had just lost her forever, she wanted to take in the feeling of every breath against the side of her face, her smell, her touch, everything.  Eventually, the blonde lowered her to her feet and kissed her several times across her face before answering.

    “Hell itself would have to swallow me up and spit me back out to keep me away from you…” Kara admitted as she inspected her wife from head to toe. “Are you hurt?”

    “I’m alright- we got stuck in the stairs…” Lena said as she turned her head toward a group of firemen who were busy roping off a larger area for remnants of falling debris. “They knocked down the door—” She stopped suddenly to face the blonde again and smiled, lifting her weepy eyes. “I am so glad to see you…”

    Kara hugged her wife closely again, tucking her head under her chin. “I was so worried about you.” The blonde admitted with a relieved exhale and one more tight squeeze.

    That feeling of absolute terror and impending doom had officially landed at the top of her list of ‘things I wouldn’t wish even on my worst enemy’, which as far as enemies were concerned, she had none, but if she did, she wouldn’t want even them to endure the nearly hour-long dread she had just experienced. Her mind had absolutely run away with itself, and it had devastated her. She had envisioned Lena being trapped in an elevator and slowly running out of oxygen or standing too close to the explosion… all manner of nightmarish scenarios had flooded her mind in such a short period of time that she was sure to have nightmares for weeks. All Kara cared about now was holding Lena and feeling her breathe against her chest. She was alive, and they were safe.

     

     

    Under a now empty awning across the street, Kara and Lena took cover in the one spot of shade that generously graced the earth and waited alongside Alex until it was safe. The blonde had stubbornly dug her heels in at her sister’s orders to go; There was no way she was going to leave without her. It took two hours for the streets to empty and another four for the bomb K-9 and her team to ascend all ten floors in an effort to clear the building of any more explosives, which there were none. Police, EMS, and Fire & Rescue applauded as the bomb technicians, or ‘Sparkies’, exited the front of the building with their thumbs up to signal the end of any imminent threat. Soon, the streetlamps overhead flickered on as dusk quickly approached. Kara was standing with her arm around Lena, thankful as ever for the worst day of her life to finally be over with when they heard shouting from a short distance behind them.

    “Alex!”

    All three of them turned around to see Sam running in between the lingering maze of police cruisers that were still parked lopsidedly onto street curbs. The brunette cried hoarsely into Alex’s neck when she landed against her. She struggled to explain through her tears that she had been unable to move any closer until now and had almost been detained at one point when she tried to put up a fight and violate the police line.

    Kara had never seen her sister look more terrified and relieved at the same time. She was just glad that they were all together now, and unharmed. When Sam lifted her eyes from Alex’s shoulder and saw Lena, she burst into sobs again, shuffling from one pair of arms into another.

    “Are you okay?!” Sam cried. Lena nodded with her chin fervently as she held her tightly. The brunette sighed and continued, “I heard the boom all the way from Tenth street, they said it was your office!”

    Lena consoled her with gentle pats on her upper back and then made room to welcome both Alex and Kara on either side of them as they all hugged each other into a tight circle.

    Kara was also unfamiliar with this level of emotion from Sam, only ever having seen her cry similarly to this when they first met in Alex’s hospital room several years ago. Her sister had saved Sam’s life and nearly lost her own in the process, which had been hard on everyone. Suddenly, Kara realized with how distraught she had been that she wasn’t broken after all. She could feel. Despite the past several weeks of emotional turbulence not exactly affecting her in the way that she had expected, or at least in the way that it should have, the thought of losing Lena forever had been like flipping her brain upside down in a vat of acid. Knowing that kind of danger had been so close to the woman she loved? God help the person responsible; She’d tear their head from their shoulders with her bare hands.

    In the middle of their huddle, Sam looked up at the gaping hole in the side of the building where Lena’s office once was. “¡Dios mío! Why would someone do that?!” She exclaimed before clapping her hands over her mouth. With that, each of them parted from one another and gawked silently upward at the site of the explosion.

    “I gotta ask you some questions now, Lena, since you’re still here.” Alex said quietly, reaching for a spiral notepad in her back pocket.

    “Right now?” Kara asked with disbelief.

    Alex turned her head toward her sister and replied with a blank stare. “Yes, now.”

    Kara folded her arms and sighed. She supposed her sister was just trying to do her job. If they wanted to catch the person who sent a bomb in a box to her wife’s job, then she knew she needed to give Alex space to work.

    “Okay, sure, whatever you need.” Lena said with a nod, wiping her tear-streaked cheeks with the backs of her hands.

    Alex clicked her ball point pen and scribbled at the top of a page. “Alright, I’ll just get preliminary questions out of the way so everyone can go home, but I’ll have to talk to you some more soon, as well as anyone who works on your floor and has access to your office.”

    “Okay, but that would be… pretty much anyone who works for me.” Lena admitted with a shade of contrition in her voice.

    Alex raised her brow with that and by the look on Lena’s face, quickly understood that she was not exaggerating as she had silently hoped.

    “Okay.” Alex began, taking a momentary pause to pull in a long, calming breath and held it there for a second longer before letting it go.

    “Do you have any reason to believe that anyone would wish to cause you injury or death?”

    Kara bristled visibly at the stark question and her sister gave her a knowing, sideways glance.

    “No… I don’t think so.” Lena answered with a small shake of her head.

    “Are you aware if any of your clients are upset with you or your company?”

    Lena shook her head again. “No… we haven’t had any major failings at this branch.”

    “Anywhere else?” Alex probed.

    Lena paused with that for a second. “A couple of years ago my New York office was served a frivolous suit for false advertisement, but the case was thrown out.”

    “Okay.” Alex scribbled again. “I’ll have to talk to you more about that, is there a way that you can get your hands on those litigation documents?”

    “Yes, I can have my New York lead Jess fax them to you.”

    Alex nodded and then made extended eye contact with her again. Kara noticed this as a tactic that her sister used when interrogating suspects of a crime. Maintaining eye contact to read subtle micro expressions or the darting of eyes allowed her to gauge a statement’s validity, or truthfulness. Most of the time, her long periods of intense eye contact unnerved many of her suspects into making costly mistakes, which typically led to their arrests. It was one of several expert qualities that made her a damn good detective. Still, Kara didn’t exactly like the fact that she was employing this skill on her wife.

    “Do you have any reason to believe that anyone who works in the same office building would wish to cause you injury or death? Maybe someone you pass by daily, or take the elevator with often?” Alex continued.

    “No.”

    “Any odd phone calls?”

    “No.”

    “No strange messages or voicemails even on your work phone?”

    “No…” Lena blinked rapidly with a thought and shifted her weight to one leg as she reached up to rub at her elbow.

    That gave her away. Kara saw the wheels behind her sister’s eyes begin to turn at full tilt now.

    “Are you sure? Even if you think it was nothing, anything out of the ordinary at all… I need to know about it.”

    Lena paused and then turned a palm upward in the air to convey uncertainty. “There were some flowers left in my office recently with a card. It didn’t say who they were from.”

    Alex tossed her chin as if to urge her to continue. “How recently are we talking?”

    Lena turned her head to look at Kara. “I don’t know a few weeks, maybe a month?”

    “You knew about this?” Alex stopped and turned to Kara with an accusing tone.

    “Yet but—” Kara began.

    Lena quickly jumped to her defense. “It seemed so small and innocent…”

    “…And I didn’t think to bother you with it.” Kara added.

    Alex shook her head with a grumble as she scribbled furiously into her notepad. “Yeah, well, you should have.”

    “Alex—”

    “You should have, Kara.”

    Taken aback, the blonde screwed her eyebrows together tightly and pursed her lips into a frown. “Well, maybe I don’t need you investigating every miniscule detail about my life.” Her words carried much more venom than she had anticipated. Tensions were high, and she was exhausted.

    Alex narrowed her eyes at her and spoke through her teeth now. “It is my damn job to investigate things. Especially if it involves my sister.”

    Room, meet elephant.

    Err… street? Whatever. Fuck this.

    “You cannot be serious… this is not still about Astra, is it?” Kara proclaimed, unamused.

    Sam and Lena both gave each other a sheepish look as if they had just walked in on a private conversation.

    “It’s about you not tellin’ me shit! Like always!” Alex fired back, throwing her hands into the air agitatedly.

    “Babe…” Sam tried to chime in then, but her wife practically had steam coming out of her ears as she continued to yell over her.

    “Somebody tried to kill your wife today and you want to get your back up about how I have to do my job to find out who, and how… and why!”

    “Babe!” Sam shouted finally, attempting to de-escalate the situation before it could get out of hand and words that neither of them meant could be said.

    Alex’s breath halted in her throat. “I—” She tried again. “You—” Then failed, choosing instead to give up for now. She growled quietly through a clenched jaw and slapped the cover of her notepad shut in her hand, stuffing it back into her pocket with a vengeance.

    “Like I said, I’ll have more questions soon. I need to check in with my sergeant and talk to the Spark- the bomb techs some more first.” Alex muttered with her eyes on the ground beneath them. “Go home.”

    Sam looped a hand through one of her arms as they stepped away and Kara and Lena began their walk north toward Ninth street where she had abandoned her truck. By the time they pulled into the driveway of their house, it was dark outside.




    Kara cut the engine and leaned her head back to rest on the top of the bench seat, letting her hands lay flat tiredly on the tops of her thighs. The moon had just begun its ascent into the night’s sky and provided the faintest amount of cool, bluish light which gracefully illuminated Lena’s profile.

    “Worst. Day. Ever.”

    “Tell me about it.” Lena chuckled exhaustedly.

    The blonde unfastened her seat belt and turned on her hips to face her wife on the other end of the truck’s cab. “Do me a favor?”

    Lena hummed as she lolled her head to the side to look over at her. “Hmm?”

    “Don’t ever scare me like that again. I don’t think my heart could take it…” Kara said as she reached out and tucked stray wisps of dark hair behind an ear. “I’m too young to have chest pains.”

    “Oh baby…” Lena cooed and lifted a hand to caress the side of Kara’s face. “I’m so sorry. I’m glad you were there, though. It really helped to be with you as soon as I made it out.”

    Kara nuzzled her face further into the palm of Lena’s hand. “Me too.”

    The blonde realized all too late that this was what her sister had given her; The chance to be there as soon as Lena was safe. It was the right thing to do, of course, but Kara knew that Alex would always look out for her, just like she always had. She began to think more about their recent arguing and how it only put distance between them. But damn was it so easy to do… and why? Why did some things have to be such a battle?

    It was knowing that while her sister didn’t always have the best way of showing it, a lot of the things she said and did were for her benefit, and to keep her, as well as the people she loved, safe. Kara knew then that she owed Alex a huge apology, and that it would be best not to wait for her sister to be the one to wave a white flag again. That would be on her this time.

    Chapter End Notes
