    Chapter 10: I'll Always Need You

    Chapter Summary

    Torn between family and career, Alex finds herself knee deep in one investigation just to stumble into another.

    Chapter Notes

    This chapter is entirely Alex POV and contains some rather pertinent information that is integral to the rest of the story. Read it, skip it, up to you. (You may want to read it, though.) The story is also finished, so I already know how everything plays out. Thank you to everyone who offered a comment over the weekend on the last chapter- I had some doubts that people were enjoying the story because of the low hit count and the sound of crickets for weeks... but I've decided to keep posting. If there's at least one person who wants to read it, that's fine by me. Turns out there are quite a few, and I appreciate the reassurance. Enjoy some Detective Danvers.

    Chapter Ten: I’ll Always Need You

       

    Alex stood hunched over with her elbows on a countertop in the kitchen and her face cradled wearily into her hands. Her eyes blinked heavily as the coffee machine in front of her steamed and dripped its hot contents into the pot. After a wide yawn, her ears popped and the sound of patient footsteps behind her grew as they drew closer. Hands snaked around her waist from behind, and a warm body laid across her back gently. A cheek pressed against her shoulder blade with a soft sigh.

    “What time did you get in last night…?” Sam asked shortly after a yawn of her own.

    Alex stood up straight and turned in between the counter and her wife, wrapping her arms around her as well. “Late. I’m sorry if I woke you.”

    “Don’t be—” The brunette hummed gently and leaned in to kiss her at the corner of her mouth. “Nothing tops feeling you crawl into bed in the middle of the night…”

    Alex tossed her eyebrows. “Nothing, huh?” She teased her with curious fingertips under the hem of her faded rock concert tee, causing the slightly taller woman to bite her lower lip in a mad effort to trap a moan.

    “Mama!” A tiny, excited voice echoed out of the hallway and bolted toward them in the kitchen.

    Ruby crashed clumsily into her mothers’ legs and clawed with her hands and knees in an attempt to scale them like trees. Alex laughed.

    “C’mere, my baby girl!” She said as she bent downward with Sam, pulling the five-year-old up and onto their hips. Ruby clung to them tightly with her arms and legs. Picking her up was still an easy task when there was someone else there to share the load, and at her age now it meant more to Ruby as a treat; an exception to be close and affectionate apart from the regular hug or squeeze. Holding both of her mothers at the same time was always something that brought her an immense amount of joy.

    “How are you?” Alex piped at her happily.

    “I missed you!” Ruby exclaimed in return without a second thought.

    Although it didn’t necessarily answer her question in the way Alex had asked, she took it as a sign that her daughter was happy to see her. Close to a week had passed of just barely missing Ruby by either bedtime or day care. Alex hadn’t seemed to catch a moment with either of them and when she had they were small, and fleeting.

    “I missed you too, sweetheart. Are you excited about school?”

    “Yes!”

    “I’m glad.” Alex leaned forward and kissed the tip of her nose. “Okay, go and get ready.”

    “Are you going to take me?” Ruby asked, her eyes big and bright much like that of a small doe.

    “…No, baby, I’m sorry. I have to go right back to work, but Mamá will stay there with you until you feel okay about it.”

    The five-year old’s face turned downward into a disappointed pout. When Sam matched their daughter’s downcast expression, Alex thought she might have been hurt by Ruby’s indifference of having one mother along and not the other… but she soon realized that it was just a child’s inability to mask her sadness that both of her mothers wouldn’t be there. Again.

    Ruby wriggled her hips silently to signal that she wanted to be let down now out of their arms, and when her little feet met the cold floor of the kitchen, she plodded them in a sulking sort of walk around the corner and back down the hallway toward her room.

    Sam leaned against Alex and sighed once she was out of earshot. “She’s glad to see you, don’t worry.”

    Alex felt a sharp, unmistakable pain in the middle of her chest. She had been working so often and with such long hours that it was normal for her to either come home after Ruby had been put down for bed or was gone again on an urgent call before she had time to greet her in the mornings. Her job as a Vice Division Detective was demanding, and not to mention dangerous. She knew Sam worried, but her wife also claimed that she understood, despite their few arguments over the years while acclimating to the nature of Alex’s job. Admittedly, it had taken some getting used to at first, but thus far there had been nothing that they couldn’t get through together.

    “I know.” Alex replied with a tone of guilt. “It’s just that the department is spread so thin right now—”

    “You’d tell me if it was us, right?” Sam asked in a quiet voice.

    Alex blinked repeatedly, shocked. “What? What are you saying… that I’d rather be at my job than here with you and Ruby?”

    Sam shot her eyes downward toward the floor and shook her head. “Forget I said it, I’m sorry—”

    Alex knew her wife had every reason and every right to doubt. She simply hadn’t been there as much as she should have been. Anyone would worry.

    “No- Sam, I don’t want you to be sorry, and I certainly don’t want you to feel like that…” Alex spoke clearly with a serious tone. “Listen… look at me.” She begged, picking Sam’s chin up by a finger.

    “You know how much I love my job, and that it’s the only thing I’ve ever wanted to do, and how hard I worked to get here… but way before that I knew I wanted to be a mom. I knew that I wanted to love somebody and to be loved in return. There isn’t a single thing on earth that compares to being your family. You both matter more to me. You always will.”

    Sam’s chin quivered slightly where it sat above Alex’s finger and the lines on her forehead drew together as she fought back tears. “We just miss you.”

    “I know. I am so, so sorry for that.” Alex whispered as she pulled Sam close. “I love you and Ruby more than anything. I need you to believe that.”

    Sam’s head rocked up and down in a nod onto her shoulder. “I do.”

    They stayed like that for several minutes until the brunette pulled away with a look of realization upon her face. “Is it quiet?”

    Alex, recognizing the question, stilled for a moment and listened before answering. “Yep.”

    “Ruby! Are you getting ready?” Sam called out across the house.

    “No, I’m playing!” The small, honest voice rang back cheerily.

    Alex burst into a laugh when Sam shook her head into the palm of her hand.

    “I’ll tell ya right now, that girl would not hold up well in an interrogation.”

    Sam began to laugh with her. “She’s too innocent. I wonder where she gets that from?”

    Alex shrugged playfully and tossed her eyes upward, then she leaned in and kissed Sam firmly on her lips, catching the other woman off guard. A gentle exhaled breath through Sam’s nose fell across Alex’s cheekbone as she pulled her tighter into her embrace. Alex let go after a long moment and then looked Sam directly into her eyes as they fluttered back open. “Remember. Always. It will always be you.”

    Sam smiled. Alex then turned her head toward the opening of the kitchen and shouted down the long hallway of the house. “Ruby, honey, bring me your backpack- I’m going to school with you.”

    A loud shriek leapt from the five-year-old’s bedroom.

    “Yay!”

     

     

    As it turns out, Alex was glad that she had chosen to go with Sam to see their daughter off on her first day of kindergarten. She had realized almost too late that these are the kinds of days that you only see one of. Watching Ruby introduce herself to the other children struck Alex with all manner of questions about her future… Would never missing another important day of her daughter’s life mean hanging up her badge? Would Sam have her choose between the two? Would it really be much of a choice to begin with? Alex had said it would always be them, and she meant every word.

    Ruby was at first apprehensive when she realized that neither of her mothers would be staying there with her for the day, but after some gentle prodding from Sam and a bit of bribery from Alex, the five-year-old skipped off toward a corner of the large room where a globe stand sat by itself in front of several pinned posters on the walls behind it.

    “There, she’s found the maps. She’ll be fine.” Alex reassured Sam quietly and placed a hand at the small of her back.

    Sam sniffled and turned her face away from one of the teaching staff who had stood with them until Ruby had felt comfortable enough to cease her clinging. “Excuse me, I’ll just go and say goodbye one more ti—”

    “Hey…” Alex said as she caught her by the hand. “Everything’s going to be alright. It’s just a few hours. You go over there now and remind her that we’re leaving, and we’ll need the jaws of life to pry her off your leg.”

    The brunette flitted her eyes back and forth between her wife and her daughter a few more times and then nodded her chin in agreement.

    “Okay, but what am I supposed to do all day now?” She teased, gesturing her hands in the air with a playful flourish.

    Alex flashed her a devilish grin. “Oh, I don’t know, you can write another chapter of your book and tell me whether or not my character makes it out of her super-secret mission alright or not…”

    Sam laughed as they walked hand-in-hand through the door of the classroom. “I can neither confirm nor deny—”

    “Oh come on!”

     

     

    Later that day, heads turned away from their desktop computer monitors toward the elevator doors as Alex swept into the bullpen hurriedly. She had been halfway to the precinct when she realized that she had a meeting with the bomb squad liaison to go over what was recovered from Lena’s office and the surrounding debris field earlier that week and was expected to be there, but she was incredibly late.

    Alex cursed under her breath as she hooked a right into the conference room where she took all of her meetings and nearly came nose to nose with a short, beady eyed man with a familiar angled face.

    “Winn!” Alex laughed.

    “Alex!” He mocked with small upward gestures of his hands and a half-hearted roll of his eyes. “I was just about to come looking for you, did I get here early?”

    She shook her head and rested her hands on her hips. “No, I got here late. It was my kid’s first day of kindergarten.”

    Winn Schott beamed a smile back at her. “Oh wow, she’s already in kindergarten? Time flies.”

    “Tell me about it.” Alex replied. “Glad you’re here though. And I’m glad they sent you, or else I’d have some serious apologizing to do.”

    Winn dropped his mouth open in a feigned display of hurt, resting a palm high on his chest. “Well in that case, I can just drive back to Dallas and send one of the rookies instead.” He teased with his nose pointed upward.

    “I mean- I’m glad they sent the best.” She corrected.

    He nodded with his satisfaction.

    Still the genius peacock.

    “How long’s it been, a year?” Alex asked as they each pulled out a chair at the oval table that took up the middle of the room.

    “Two.” He answered. “Fireworks stand off I-35 a couple Fourth of July’s back.”

    Alex tilted her head back with a laugh. “Oh yeah! That one was fun.”

    “You chased me with a bottle rocket.” Winn deadpanned back at her.

    “Nobody saw! If only you had run like that on the training course when we met.”

    Winn had been her longest friend and the first kind soul to venture past her angry, punchy walls when she had first moved to Austin. They stayed in touch over the years and visited when they could, but each had a career now that monopolized most of their time. He was still rolling his eyes and shaking his head as she grinned from ear to ear at him. She had missed their in-person camaraderie, as phone calls and texts just didn’t quite have the same effect.

    “Okay!” She proclaimed, clapping her hands together. “What have you got for me?”

    He lifted his brow in surprise and then nodded, more than likely remembering that they were there for interdepartmental work and not to catch up on life, unfortunately.

    “Alright- so basically, your bomb maker is a pro. But he’s broke.”

    Alex quirked her head to the side with her confusion. “How do you figure?”

    Winn smiled proudly and held a finger up to suspend her questioning, then he leaned down and pulled a clear evidence bag from the duffle at his feet. Its contents clunked awkwardly inside the bag once he set it upon the table.

    “It’s all in the materials. See these bits?” He said, pointing a short forefinger toward what looked like uninteresting hunks of charred junk. “Polyvinyl chloride.”

    Alex narrowed her eyes directly at him, unamused. “And for those who don’t get their jollies from making things go boom?”

    He laughed. “PVC piping. Your bomber assembled a perfectly sized and well-constructed pipe bomb with a time delayed trigger, but most often you see these made with casings like carbon fiber, or aluminum, which have a good amount of flex to them and can pressurize well with a high breaking point. Both are relatively cheap, but PVC even compared to aluminum is pennies on the dollar.”

    He then rattled the bag to move its contents around and flattened it over some smaller pieces. “Plus, see these gears?”

    Alex leaned forward on the edge of her seat to look more closely. “Mhm.”

    “They’re clock gears, but they’re made of cheap plastic instead of metal. Probably from a dollar store analog wall clock- you know the ones that run off a double A battery? Speaking of, I found the positive end of said battery embedded in what was left of a desk.”

    “So…” Alex screwed her eyes shut and rubbed at her temples. “Is that why it went off after an hour?”

    Winn nodded. “Look at the bigger gears here—” He pointed again. “There are only enough teeth to complete a pass of sixty minutes, but I suspect it took longer for it to detonate because of what they were made of. It’s likely the plastic gears bent, causing the teeth to misalign and catch once engaged, essentially delaying the inevitable. Metal gears wouldn’t do that.”

    Alex sat back and folded her arms in thought.

    Note to self: Winn is sort of terrifying.

    But in an endearing, nerdy kind of way?

    “In theory, and in practice, it was a well put together device because of how it was built. But its components are a dead giveaway. I would look for someone who is flat broke or down on their luck, with possible scars from fingertip to elbow. Maybe near the face or neck too. Somebody who makes bombs like this, like it was their job, has made mistakes before. They all do to get this good. But we didn’t pull any prints and found no trace DNA, so this is where we turn things over to y’all.”

    Alex sighed. It was more than what they had to go on before now. A lot more, in fact. They had the beginnings of a profile, and all behavioral forensic roads led to Lex Luthor. She made a mental note then to try and discover any scars on the man once the legs of her investigation brought her face to face with him.

    Just a friendly chat with a killer.

    That’ll be fun.

    “Anything else?” She asked with a long breath.

    Winn’s expression darkened then. He seemed almost frightened, which Alex noticed immediately. “What is it?”

    He blinked several times and then with a frown, leaned back down and dug a hand into the duffle bag on the floor between his feet to retrieve another evidence bag, tossing it on the table. Its contents chimed together with a metallic clanging. Alex sat forward and hovered over it, inspecting it closely.

    Holy mother of God…

    “Are th-those… nails?”

    Winn nodded carefully. “Nails, straight edged razors, and metal jacks filed into sharp points like tiny caltrops.” He paused for a moment and cleared his throat, and then sat up straight to command his currently fleeting nerves. “This explosive device was crafted with the intention of obliterating its target. Your bomb maker hates someone. A lot.”

    A chill ran up Alex’s spine.

    Sure sounds a lot like him…

    The difficulty with knowing that Lex had attempted to kill his estranged half-sister with a bomb was that she needed to prove it. Alex knew it in her bones from the moment the call had come over her radio and directed her toward Lena’s office building, but a jury would need to know it too. Beyond a reasonable doubt. Eliminating all other suspects had been her first intention, and as more evidence pointed toward Lex it filled her with a twisted, sickening sense of accomplishment. It really was a curse to be so perceptive and suspicious. The more she proved herself right, the more she dreaded the reality of it.

    Alex suddenly wished that she had stopped and eaten breakfast instead of the sixteen-ounce energy drink she guzzled on the way to the station. With a shaky hand, she reached out and pushed the bag full of shrapnel toward the edge of the table.

    “Okay, take it away- I’ve seen enough.”

    Winn quickly slid the evidence off of the table and dropped it back between his feet. Now that they were done with the ominous and incredibly horrifying improvised explosive device and its accompanying details, he leaned back into his chair and folded his arms across his chest, loosening his jaw a bit in an attempt to relax.

    “I’ll take everything downstairs for you, I’ve—”

    “No, Winn, that’s alright. Chain of evidence and all that- I got it.” Alex stated matter of factly.

    He adjusted in his seat and then leaned to the side to dig a laptop out of a backpack that he had placed in a chair next to his own. “In other news…” He paused for a moment and hugged the laptop against his stomach.

    “You remember a few weeks back when you said that under no circumstance whatsoever should I run a background check on a woman in her early-to-mid fifties, who was approximately five foot four to five foot six, who may or may not have recently entered the country by international flight and has a super weird white streak in her hair but looks slightly, if not almost identical to one Kar—”

    “Winn! Shush!” Alex jumped and whipped her head around at the shut door of the conference room. “Jesus H Christ, Schott…”

    Intrigued, he lifted his chin with an air of confidence. “Those are certainly one of my names.”

    “You’re going to get me in trouble.”

    “Then it’s a good thing that I looked her up and not you, so you can relax there a little bit, squirrely.”

    “I am not squirrely.” She harped at him quietly through her teeth.

    “Do you want to look at what I found or not?” He asked with one eyebrow raised.

    Alex sat and thought for a second. Technically, she hadn’t been the one to open that can of worms. And she had explicitly said not for him to go digging… which of course he had more than likely mistaken for a non-committal request or permission of all things. Their style of communication did border on the sarcastic at most times. She scrunched her nose and furrowed her brow for a second longer and then released a held breath.

    “Okay fine, show me.”

    Winn smiled and opened the laptop on the table, turning it at its side to face its screen at her. “So, your mystery lady is well traveled. Passport stamps in the UK, Russia, so on and so forth… but what’s more interesting is what I didn’t find.”

    Alex stared daggers at him again, a frequent gesture she made when she was really trying to say, “Get to the point”.

    “You said she was born here, and I found a birth certificate, but that’s about it. No job history, no domestic bank accounts, no credit history, no DMV records, nothing. That is to say up until very recently when she checked into a hotel here in Austin under an alias, which I double checked with the hotel’s CCTV just to be sure.”

    “You didn’t by chance get the hotel’s permission to take a look at their footage, did you?”

    “That’s beside the point—” He said, waving a hand dismissively, which really meant he was running away from having to admit that he had hacked into a closed-circuit camera system illegally.

    “But here’s where things get really interesting…” He said in a hushed voice as he tapped the directional keys on the laptop to scroll to the next page.

    The screen loaded with long rows of numbers that appeared like transactions, and as soon as Alex’s eyes floated to the top right corner and read the words ‘Grand Cayman National Bank’ she gasped and clapped both of her hands over her mouth. Her eyes darted back to Winn, who was smiling with the full use of his teeth in a ‘don’t hurt me’ sort of expression.

    “Please, please, tell me we are not spying on the Cayman’s private banking system with the use of Austin City PD’s internet and IP address…” She asked hesitantly. Her skin had flushed so hot that she could break a sweat at any second.

    “Don’t be silly, they’re just screen grabs.” He admitted with a playful lilt to his voice.

    “Lord have mercy I could strangle you right now, Winn Schott.”

    He laughed and then closed the laptop quickly. “I’ll take that as a compliment. Or police brutality…”

    She leaned across and swatted at his knee, making him yelp and jump reactively. They laughed for a moment and then shared a long, relieved sigh. Satisfied, he reached into his pocket and pulled out a thumb drive, holding it out toward her.

    “You know I can’t plug that in here, right?”

    He pulled his lips into a thin line and nodded with his eyes closed. “Mhm.”

    She took the drive from him and then stuffed it into her shirt pocket. “Thanks, bud.”

    “Don’t thank me yet.” He said as he leaned sideways again to reach into the backpack beside him and pulled out a dark red folder filled with a stack of papers at least a half inch thick.

    Puzzled, Alex took the folder as it was handed to her and opened it to take a look inside. It was printed pages of Astra’s bank account. Suddenly, she slapped the folder shut and stuck it back out at him.

    “Winn, I think this is a bad idea, I’ve changed my mind—”

    “Look…” He began as he pushed the folder back toward her slowly. “With as much as you’ve chatted my ear off about this woman every time we’ve talked on the phone lately, and I mean every time we’ve talked, I think you need this. Maybe this will give you some closure, and you can stop obsessing abou—”

    “I do not obsess.”

    He stared blankly at her, knowing full well that ‘obsess’ should have been her middle name.

    “Just… do yourself a favor and get it out of your system. And then burn literally all of that. Including the flash drive. Seriously.”

    Alex heaved another sigh and dropped the folder into her lap. Maybe he was right. Maybe she just needed to give in and let herself take one good, hard look at this woman who appeared almost overnight in her sister’s life right at the brink of a series of very strange, and dangerous events. Maybe it was nothing after all, and Kara was right to be frustrated with her. Hell, she was frustrated with herself.

     

     

    That evening after work, whether it was the incessant, throbbing feeling of guilt that gnawed at the back of her brain which brought her to Kara’s front door, or if it was the fact that she was still so relieved that her sister was safe and had been desperate to talk to her for weeks… she couldn’t be sure.

    She knew one thing, though. She needed a hug.

    Her knuckles rapped against the wood of the front door and moments later, it pulled open.

    “Oh, Alex, hi.” Lena greeted her with a smile. “Did you have more questions?”

    Oh.

    Alex grinned with a shade of embarrassment and ran a hand through her hair. “Well, yes, but… not tonight. Is Kara home?”

    She knew that she was, her truck was parked just in the driveway, but this was already proving to be more difficult than she had expected.

    “Sure, let me get her for you. Come inside?”

    Alex nodded with a polite thank you as she stepped over the threshold and habitually tucked the hem of her leather jacket over her service pistol where it was holstered at her hip. Lena quickly exited the foyer and then there was silence. Moments later, familiar footsteps drew closer. She knew Kara to walk with a bit of a stomp ever since they were children; a constant course of irritation for their mother who would shout up the stairs to ‘step lightly’, or especially when they would rough house, or run, or make any kind of grating noises that children typically made. The memory itself was not exactly a fond one, but it still made her smile.

    “Alex?”

    Kara’s jeans were tattered at the ends where the heel of her boots would usually bite when she walked, but she was barefoot, so they bunched under the arches of her feet clumsily. She wore a sleeveless shirt that was at least two sizes too big for her and had her hair pulled up into a messy bun. Alex realized that she had showed up later than she intended, because Kara looked like she was half-way into bed.

    “I can come back another day, if now’s not a good time—”

    “What are you talking about, come on…” The blonde urged her into the large seating area with a wave of her hand. “What’s going on?”

    Alex dropped her keys on the large glass tabletop in front of the couch as they sat down at each end. She then hugged her elbows anxiously, pulling a deep breath and holding it there. She was desperate to talk about what she had learned earlier that day, but she couldn’t. Not about the investigation involving the bombing, and definitely not about the unsanctioned, and incredibly illegal investigation into her sister’s biological aunt. She felt like she could burst, but instead of doing so with words, she chose to do so with her body.

    Kara squealed at her sister’s sudden lunging movement across the length of the couch and emitted an audible -oof- when Alex landed against her and squeezed.

    “Kara, I’m so sorry I’ve been such an asshole, and I don’t mean half the things I say, except for now, because I love you, and I’m sorry—”

    “Okay! Okay- okay… breathe for a second and let go before you squeeze me in half.” Kara managed to utter with her arms pinned to her sides.

    Alex jumped away quickly, releasing her. “Sorry…” She said, hovering her hands out over Kara’s arms in apology.

    “You know, that’s a word you’ve probably said more just now than… ever. What’s wrong?”

    Alex blinked and sat back on her knees. She felt somewhat deflated by her sister’s unintentional callousness, but she didn’t let it deter her from what she felt was more important now than ever to say out loud.

    “I just mean it. I feel horrible about us not getting along and I was…I don’t know.” Her voice puttered out into a silent breath.

    Kara quirked her head. “Jealous?”

    “I guess.” Alex pursed her lips in thought. “I don’t think I like that word, though.”

    “Then we don’t have to use it.” Kara responded matter-of-factly.

    Alex sighed. “Sort of feels like you don’t need me anymore—"

    “Alex, stop.” Kara reached over and wrapped a hand around her sister’s forearm, startling her for a split second. “I should be apologizing to you.”

    “W-what?”

    Kara sat up and adjusted a pillow behind her back against the arm of the couch and took a deep breath. “I haven’t exactly been very patient or understanding about things either… I know that you’re only trying to protect me. It’s what you’ve always done.” The blonde said, casting her eyes downward, ashamedly.

    “I should have called you- I mean I meant to…” Kara’s words trailed off into a grumble and then she drew her eyebrows together tight on her forehead, wrinkling the thin lines in between. “Why is this so hard?”

    Alex shrugged her shoulders and chuckled softly. “No idea. Maybe it’s because I love you this much, so it’s just as easy to be mad at you?”

    “That makes zero sense.” Kara said unenthusiastically, and then laughed quietly at herself.

    “Listen…” Alex folded her hands into her lap and waited until her sister’s eyes lifted again. “I want you to hear me when I say this; If I took a bullet for Sam, then what do you think I’d do for you?”

    Kara’s eyes opened wide. She sat speechless for a moment before she mustered the courage to answer. “I don’t know… a lot?”

    “Anything.” Alex echoed.

    The blonde’s cheeks flushed red, and her eyes began to glisten with tears. Alex clicked her tongue and cooed at her, opening her arms out wide in a gesture to receive her. Kara then dove forward and flattened her upper body into her sister’s where she squeezed her tightly around her shoulders.

    After a long moment and several big and obnoxious sighs, the blonde whispered faintly, just loudly enough for her sister to hear, “I’ll always need you.”

     

     

    The door to Ruby’s room creaked slightly on its hinges, opening into darkness. A red caped, superhero shaped night light on a small end table near the child-sized bed gleamed with a dull, crimson glow. Ruby was fast asleep with her cheeks red and puffy from exhaustion and lips pursed into a tired pout. Alex stood in the partially open doorway and admired the small, sleeping human that was in fact a biological miracle. Genetically, she resembled Alex since the procedure used to conceive her first began in a dish with one of her eggs. The resulting embryo was then implanted in Sam’s womb where baby Ruby grew to term and was birthed, biologically connecting them both to her.

    The simple fact that they had used a Colombian sperm donor profile that bore so much of a resemblance to Sam by shade of skin, thick grade of hair and even the shape of her face only added to Alex’s wonderment whenever she looked at their daughter. She looked so much like Sam. Especially when she slept.

    With a content smile, Alex leaned back out of the doorway and shut it with a small -click-. She was disappointed that she had missed coming home in time to tuck her in to bed again, but was glad to be at home, nonetheless. She knew that going with Sam to see Ruby off on her first day of kindergarten was an important milestone in the grand journey that would be her daughter’s life, and she had been there to see it. Alex stood silently in the hallway for a moment in disbelief that she had even considered missing it in the first place.

    What the hell am I doing…

    “You’re home—” A voice caused her to jump and turn toward the end of the hallway. Sam stood there, beaming a lovely smile back at her.

    Alex settled her hands back onto the tops of her hips. “Yeah, I still have something I need to look over actually…” She said as she began a brisk walk toward her wife. “But I wanted to do it here instead, where you are.”

    She leaned forward and placed a hand flat at the small of Sam’s back, pulling her roughly into the front of her body with a firm kiss. Muffled behind Sam’s lips was a small, surprised squeak, and then an audible, melting sort of sigh. The brunette softened, resting the backs of her arms on the tops of Alex’s shoulders. She wrapped them around the crown of her head, teasing her fingers softly into rich, mahogany hair. It had been so long since Alex had put her charm to good use like this, and she swore then and there to never let herself drift away from her affections ever again.

    After a few, long and intimate moments of languid, unhurried kissing, Sam pulled her lips away with a quiet -smack- and placed her cheek against Alex’s with a short and satisfied sigh.

    “I’ll make you a deal. You can bring work home as much as you want, as long as you do exactly that when you get here.” Sam teased with a tap of a finger on Alex’s lips.

    Alex’s shoulders bounced with a hushed laugh and then leaned forward to whisper at the shell of Sam’s ear. “Sounds reasonable.” The brunette shivered slightly as the breath from her words danced across her skin.

    Almost reluctantly, Alex took a step back to end their warm embrace and dug into the front pocket of her pants to fish for her keys. “I’ve got to grab some stuff from the car- join me on the couch?”

    Sam bounced forward on her toes with a smile and quickly pecked a kiss on Alex’s top lip. “Would love to.”

     

     

    The red folder in her hands was heavy and carried a foreboding feeling which was something that Alex tried to shake off, but simply couldn’t. She couldn’t explain the deep, unsettling feeling in the pit of her stomach as she thumbed through page after page of large withdrawals and deposits of Astra’s offshore bank account. Biting past her shame for even deigning to look and for giving in to her curiosity, Alex balked at some of the dollar amounts that bounced in and out of the account over the course of years. She looked over decades worth of transactions in the hundreds of thousands of dollars, sometimes as frequent as five to six times a year. Then there seemed to be months with little to no activity, appearing to be random in nature at first, but soon realized that they followed up each round of large deposits like clockwork. Financial activity like this mimicked that of drug cartels as they moved around, laying low for months before moving product, and then packing up and moving again. Her heart fluttered with her anxiety the more that she continued to read.

    Alex felt a dip in the cushion next to her on the couch as she looked up at a tumbler glass with two fingers of amber colored whiskey, and behind it lay the soft, smiling eyes of her wife. She was like a breath of fresh air. Sam handed her the glass and sat comfortably with her own as she snuggled closer to Alex, pulling a knitted blanket over their legs and into their laps.

    “Thank you.” Alex chimed at her happily.

    “You’re welcome.” Sam replied, smoothing a hand up and down Alex’s thigh on top of the blanket. “I’m glad you’re here.”

    “Me too.”

    Alex focused tiredly back on the papers in front of her and as if looking at them with fresh eyes, a specific row of dates of deposits had jumped out at her. From the early 90’s to early 2000’s, there were a flood of several hundred-thousand-dollar deposits with withdrawals not nearly in half of those amounts peppered in between. In between her investigation of the bombing, Alex had found no other source of work for the mysterious woman. No college degree or collegiate history for that matter, and no mention or affiliation with any Fortune Five Hundred companies or wealthy businesses abroad either. It was as if the woman was a ghost who trafficked in an unknown industry. Even drug cartels and entities in the private military sector had paper trails. Astra’s financial footprint was just that, a footprint that seemingly came from nowhere, and led to who knows. Alex continued scanning each date as a horribly ominous, and deeply unnerving feeling washed over her.

    I must be crazy… these dates have to be a coincidence…

    Alex sat up abruptly, tilting Sam’s head off her shoulder. The brunette twisted around and looked at Alex with wide eyes.

    “Is something wrong…?”

    Alex hastily continued dragging her eyes over the page. Her heart rate increased, and she could feel the tingle of small hairs at the nape of her neck begin to raise.

    April 1991.

    July 1991.

    April 1993.

    August 1996.

    July 1997…

    “Alex…” Sam’s voice was but a faraway echo that bounced in the now empty chamber that was her mind. It was as if the world began to fall away beneath her. Alex began to breathe heavily as she gripped the paper tightly, crumpling it slightly.

    Presidential Intelligence Advisor John Tower was killed in a suspicious plane crash in 1991…

    That July, a British reporter for the Financial Times was found murdered in his hotel bathtub.

    Alex’s not-so-small obsession with matters of political intrigue and conspiracy theories had finally become of use. A ‘hobby’ she called it. Countless hours she spent deep-diving into the world of espionage and financial corruption at a national and world leader level, which up until now had been a source of playful mockery by others. Alex, on the other hand, knew that somewhere hidden in all of it had to be a grain of truth. Now it seems that she had found one.

    Chris Hani was assassinated in April of 1993.

    The President of Somalia in 1996.

    Mary Mahoney in July of 1997…

     

    …Oh, my God.

     

    “Babe, you’re not breathing…” Sam reached out and shook her by an elbow.

    Alex realized that she had indeed been holding her breath and gasped. She then flashed her eyes back and forth between Sam and the page her hands.

    “I’ve gotta go back to the station…” Alex muttered, visibly shaken now.

    Sam balked at her. “What, now?”

    “I’m sorry—”

    “Wait, it’s late…”

    Alex stood quickly and tucked the folder under an arm as she reached back down for her phone and her car keys.

    “Alex—”

    “I’m sorry. I’ll be back as soon as I can.”

    “What is going on?” Sam asked as she sat on her knees with her eyes flitting anxiously up and down over Alex.

    “I—” Alex swallowed and paused for a moment. “I can’t say.”

    “Then don’t leave like this…” The brunette begged upwardly at her.

    Alex’s heart broke. She’d be damned if she ever found a bigger rock and an even harder place to be stuck between. She could tell her, but that would mean Sam would have to keep a secret from Lena and that wasn’t a burden that she wanted to place on anyone other than herself. Alex couldn’t be sure yet, but what she held in her hands could possibly implicate Kara’s biological aunt in a number of international murders. That’s why finding out was important, and she couldn’t breathe a word of it to anyone. Not yet.

    Alex hung her head in despair for a moment longer before making yet another incredibly hard decision that only aided in worsening the schism between them. She had been forced to make too many of those over the years and wanted nothing more in the world than to find a way out of it.

    “I’m sorry.” Alex responded again in a broken voice.

    “Alex—”

    “I have to, Sam.”

    “You don’t have to do anything! Tell me what’s wrong, please.”

    With a small shake of her head and a frown, Alex turned her hands upward in the air to convey silently that she simply couldn’t, and turned to leave.

    “Alex…"

    "Alex!”

    Chapter End Notes

    Chapter 11: Your Beautiful Pieces

    Chapter Summary

    A fatigued Kara faces down an old enemy in a moment of panic. An ultimatum is given, whereupon a decision is made.

    Chapter Notes

    CW: Panic attacks, themes of violence.
    Some NSFW toward the end of this chapter.
    (Pssst… I’ve updated and added some more tags)

    Chapter Eleven: Your Beautiful Pieces

       

    Bare limbed trees scattered the landscape surrounding Danvers Rescue & Repair. Their branch tips bent in a westerly breeze as if tickling an overcast sky. It was windy for an October morning as fallen leaves of burnt orange and gold swirled in sweeping, circular gusts throughout the parking lot. The trickling water of Boggy Creek just behind the shop seemed to move with a bit more haste as well.

    Yellowed patches of grass around the lot were still damp with overnight dew and filled the area with a lingering, tangy scent. Kara stretched into the gentle, perfumed wind, having arrived an hour early to work with a resolute and unyielding need to busy her hands.

    For weeks after the bombing, Kara found herself lunging awake in the middle of the night drenched in sweat and unaware of her surroundings. Some nights were more frantic than others, the worst of which often settled a deep refusal within the blonde to even attempt go back to sleep. Lena would plead with her on occasion, insisting, “You have to sleep eventually…” but Kara simply couldn’t muster the courage at times.

    It made her feel weak, and cowardly. Oftentimes, what transpired in her dreams was worse than the fatigue she experienced during her waking hours. The lack of energy and awareness were sacrifices that Kara was willing to make. Though, she knew her stubbornness would soon come to heel when she eventually passed out or hurt herself at work. For now, it was all she could do to just be careful.

    As she crossed the parking lot, the glass on the front door of the main building looked a bit more transparent this morning than it usually did. Either her eyes were still hazy from her exhaustion, or Brainy had cleaned it so well that it appeared as though to not even be there. When she stepped closer, it suddenly dawned on her.

    It wasn’t there. The door had been shattered.

    Despite her bewilderment, Kara decided to duck under the push bar of the door and step through where pieces of glass crunched loudly under her boots. For how tired she was, her anxiety took much longer to catch up to her initial shock than she expected. Her confusion mixed with her rising dread was, still for the moment, a calm and fluttering feeling behind her ribs.

    Did we get robbed?

    When the thought finally struck her, so did a second wind of energy. Kara’s legs shot forward as she bolted into a run across the lobby and past the front desk toward her back office. When she reached it, she saw that it had been hammered with something big around its knob. With her heart now beating much harder, Kara reached out with a hand and found that it was still locked. The door itself had been a good investment, it seemed.

    Its hard metal was dented and dimpled in many places where it looked like it had been pounded against with something heavy. Someone had tried with all of their strength to breach the threshold of her private office but had failed. There was no sign of what large implement was used to do so either. She spun to see the drawers at Brainy’s desk had all been pulled from their tracks and their contents dumped onto the floor.

    Kara quickly began to assess the value of as many things as she could in her head as she stumbled around the mess, trying to figure out what exactly someone had been in search of, if they hadn’t been able to break into her back office. Motor oil she purchased by the drum was a lucrative payday if stolen, but she’d like to see someone carry off a trailer bed full of fifty-five-gallon metal drums in a hurry without either spilling one or hurting themselves in the process.

    Oh shit, the freon…

    The shop kept a stock of R134a, an a/c refrigerant used for many different applications other than vehicle air conditioning, such as household refrigerators, commercial ice makers, central a/c systems, cold storage facilities and more. It wasn’t cheap either. Kara typically took advantage of sales for thirty-pound cylinders and bought in bulk when she could. She wouldn’t have been surprised if they were all gone.

    Fumbling her keys in her hands to unlock the door that led into the shop from the lobby, she paused for a split second to drag in a deep breath and calm her nerves, allowing the key to slide into the lock cylinder of the door without a fight. Her boots echoed off the high ceiling in the shop as she ran down its length past rows of tool cabinets, and eventually swung a left into the parts cage where they kept the freon and zero gas, as well as the gauges for each which were also considered high dollar equipment. To her relief, everything seemed to still be stacked in their respective places and were all for the most part accounted for. With a heavy sigh, she wheeled backwards on her feet as she rubbed her thumbs into the corners of her eyes.

    I can’t deal with this right now…

    Suddenly, something heavy met her back and a loud clanging noise rang out into the shop, making her clap her hands over her ears instinctively. Kara shouted and ducked as the clanging grew quicker in succession while maintaining its same ear-splitting volume. She swung with her elbows wildly as she screamed until her vision cleared, and she could see a bare twenty-inch alloy wheel circling on its side, the noise from which screeched like glass marbles circling a stone drain. It was deafening, and her panic mixed with her exhaustion, combined with the incredibly loud and startling noise, sent her into a full-blown panic attack.

    Desperate, choking gasps brought her to her knees. Kara couldn’t tell if it was the noise from the wheel against the poured concrete of the shop floor or if she was still screaming, but her ears throbbed. Up was down, air was molasses, and if she was on her back or her stomach, she didn’t know. All she knew was that it seemed to go on forever.

    Something shook her all of a sudden, turning her. A pair of hands gripped Kara under her arms and dragged her away from the cage as she fought to keep her own over her ears. Images began to flash vividly behind her eyelids in quick succession:

    The foggy picture of a distressed gambler hat descending a flight of stairs, the broad, tapered horns of a longhorn bull, a shotgun, swinging… A tiny, flailing body pulled by its hair… 

    No…

    No! Leave me alone!

    A far away voice called out to her. “Kara! Stop!”

    It was useless. Kara’s complete and unbridled panic sat in the driver’s seat now. Upon necessity, fear morphed into anger. Left with no other option, it seemed to be the only thing she had left in the world to ensure her survival. Nothing felt more right than making him hurt. 

    “Kara!”

    She was on her back, and someone was on top of her. They certainly had a face, but who it belonged to was apparently still too much to ask. Suddenly, she was upright again, and the person who had been above her was now pinned beneath her knees. Kara’s hands gripped tightly around a throat and her thumbs pushed hard into what felt like an Adam’s apple. A strange sense of familiarity flooded her senses as the seconds dragged on. A pair of black eyes that sat beneath thick, dark eyebrows blinked frantically at her, begging. The skin around them quickly began to turn blue. With her peripheral vision fizzling back into view, Kara noticed shaky palms facing upward at her and slowly realized that they were held in surrender. Her eyes flitted back to the face below her, and that’s when she saw him.

    “Brainy…?” Her voice rasped as she recognized him.

    Kara’s hands were still tight around his neck, but he made no attempt to grab at them, as if it were futile to even try. Kara was strong, and he must have realized that his only chance at escaping her was to visibly show her that he was not a threat and to simply hope for the best.

    With a surprised gasp, she released him and then shoved herself off the top of him. Brainy choked so hard for air that he nearly vomited and took a long time to begin breathing on his own without feeling as though he could pass out at any moment. Kara perched herself clumsily on her heels as she watched in shock while the young man fought for air. During the struggle, her body and her mind had melded into one all-encompassing, imperative measure; To make the thing that scared her stop. But that thing was a person, and that person had been Brainy.

    “B-Brainy, oh my God—”

    Now that Kara resided once again inside of her body and was in control once more, waves of torment washed over her in a cascade of realizations. She damn well could have killed him, had he not had the sense to not try and overpower her. He labored to breathe still, clutching at his throat as he hacked and coughed, but he was conscious enough to extend a hand and gesture a weak wave that he was alright. Purple faced and red eyed, Brainy leaned up and sat on his knees as he continued to reach out to Kara in an attempt to calm her. It was just like him to do that, to worry about someone else first over himself.

    “I am so sorry…” Kara lamented helplessly. The blonde felt as though there was no amount of apology for what she had just done, even if she had done so unconsciously.

    Finally able to swallow and draw in breath that didn’t catch halfway and cause him to choke, Brainy sat rubbing his neck and shaking his head. “Are you okay?”

    Kara sputtered for a split-second. She shook her head in disbelief at the young man sitting on his legs across from her. “I just had my hands around your neck…”

    Kara lifted her reddened, aching hands and glowered at them sullenly. What could create, she realized, could also destroy. Never before had she been so disappointed and ashamed of herself as a human being. The heavy, roiling feeling of her shame quickly filled her to the brim, and her face burst forth with a contorted and pained expression as she began to weep.

    Brainy, being the empathetic and kind soul that she knew him to be, leaned forward on his knees and reached out for her again. Kara jolted unexpectedly, and with that he sat back slowly.

    “Hey… it’s alright. Everything is okay—”

    “No it’s not!” Kara shouted as she began to spiral again.

    What do I do if I can’t trust myself?

    What if it had been Lena?

    “Kara—” He paused to swallow again painfully. “You need to hum now …”

    She lifted her gaze from her trembling hands and stared at him, petrified and confused.

    “Hum a song that makes you feel safe.”

    Kara squinted her eyes and crooked her head at him as her chest barreled up and down with her hyperventilating.

    “Trust me.”

    She knew that she could. Trust him, that is. However, humming a song while she hurtled toward another panic attack… she wasn’t so sure of. How in the world could she streamline a single thought in the form of a song past the M. C. Escher painting that was the inside of her head? If it meant making all of this stop, then she had to at least try.

    Kara closed her eyes.

    Think of a song, think of a song…

    The menagerie of her mind that was upside down staircases and twisted, high arches pulled away and shifted on top of one another, folding and dismantling themselves until finally something came through. It was silhouetted, and beautiful. What appeared hadn’t been a song, but a memory of her wife singing to her. Its melodic tune was familiar and radiated with an enveloping warmth, and the buzzing rush of blood behind her eardrums began to slowly subside. She knew the song well, as it was one that Lena sang often when content, or joyful.


    “One morning a young man was walking his way,

    When he saw a fair maid - come along the highway,

    He said to this fair maid, ‘If you do not mind -

    I will walk with you on this fine morning’.”


    Kara’s chest vibrated with her low humming as she followed the tune in her head. It required partly held breath, to hum, and she soon realized that her nerves began to settle now that she had taken back control of her breathing.


    “The young maid she told all of her sorrowful tale,

    How the landlord was wicked - and forced her away.

    And she had just left on that very same morn -  

    Then the young man he turned, and he told her…”

    "Won't you come with me now?

    And I will look after you always.

    Oh, won't you come with me now?

    And I will look after you always."

Alex stepped out into the grass and beckoned them to come over with large swoops of her arms into the air. Ruby nearly burst into a run but stopped herself short to crane her head up at Sam as if to ask first. Sam nodded, and away she went.

    “Remind me to thank Kara for teaching her that. It’s been wonderful inside the house.” Sam teased at Lena with a playful elbow into her side.

    Lena laughed with a happy shrug of her shoulders. “What can I say? She is a woman of many talents.”

    “OH-kay.” Sam replied dramatically, clearly choosing to shelve the many inappropriate comebacks to that phrase for a later date.

    They crossed the grass in a comfortable walk while Alex spun Ruby around in the air joyously. Lena admired how they looked exactly like a Hallmark movie. It was witnessing little moments like that which allowed the not-entirely unwelcome, yet still intrusive thought of doing the same with a child of her own to enter her mind. Warm thoughts like those usually left her as quickly as they came about in the first place, though they never failed to leave a smile on her face.

    When Lena reached them, Kara leaned forward and kissed her on her cheek with a small “Hey darlin’—" meant only for her ears. It made Lena’s heart flutter so easily the way that Kara could speak to her as if she were the only woman in the world. She took a moment to just stare at her, silently appreciating her.

    So charming… and mine, all mine.

    “Are you ready for candy?!” The blonde exclaimed toward the five-year-old currently attempting to scale her like the tall side of a cliff. Ruby squealed happily when Kara bent down and scooped her around her middle before standing straight again and tossing her over her shoulder like a bag of oats.

    “Okay, see y’all later!” Kara joked as she turned around to leave with a kicking and giggling Ruby perched on her shoulder.

    Alex, who had been preoccupied with smattering a layer of kisses over Sam’s scrunched and smiling face, quickly turned and threw her arms in the air with a flourish. “Well, what are we supposed to do now that you’ve taken the child?”

    Sam chimed in, hot on her heels. “Yeah, we’re too old to trick-or-treat.”

    “No eres vieja, Mami!” Ruby interjected and then screeched when Kara reached up and tickled the inside of her knee.

    “What this one said- but also, there’s an apple bobbing contest on the other side of the park that I wanna get to before the line gets long. Plenty of time for candy, right kid?” Kara said as she jogged backwards gently bouncing Ruby on her shoulder, who screeched incoherently with her excitement.

    “Wait a minute, where did you see that? I wanna try!” Alex leapt forward on her feet eagerly, then stopped on her toes to turn back around and give Sam one last kiss before jogging to catch up with Kara and Ruby, who were nearly across the knoll on the other side now.

    Sam and Lena shared a stunned and silly look with one another before breaking out into laughter. Lena wiped at the corner of her eye with a slender finger and clung to Sam’s arm as she caught her breath and cleared her throat.

    “So apparently there are three weeuns now instead of just the one.”

    The brunette sniffled happily and nodded, looping her arm into Lena’s as they walked. “God help us.”

     

     

    October had been a sweet breath of relief from the heat of the summer, and crowds like the one on this particular afternoon at Ruby’s favorite park seemed to move with a less frenetic and ‘buzzing’ sort of energy. People seemed to undulate in a calm and moseying sort of manner from bench to table, and so on. When people gathered outdoors during peak summer days, tensions were usually high to begin with. But not today. A cool and subtle wind tossed dried leaves with their deep browns and yellows around in little cyclones that dissipated after a few seconds. Skinny, naked branches of trees loomed overhead as the blended Danvers family walked a meandering pace along rows of wooden benches. The afternoon had waned, and the sun began to set lower in the sky, bathing the landscape of the park in a warm, orange glow.

    Ruby hid a yawn behind her elbow as she struggled to hoist the handle of her nearly full trick-or-treat bag. She was hand in hand with Kara, stumbling here and there over her own tired feet. Kara looked down and frowned at her in an adoring way, much like one would at a puppy who couldn’t hold its head up for being so sleepy.

    “Are you ready to head home?” She asked.

    Ruby immediately shook her head and fixed her face to appear more alert, though her heavy eyelids continued to give her away. “No.”

    “It was the half a turkey leg. That did her in.” Alex admitted as she walked closely behind them with her arm around Sam’s waist.

    “For a while there I thought she was going to eat the whole thing, bless her.” Lena chimed in on the other side of Ruby. The five-year-old had made a game of swinging in between her and Kara until she had begun to yawn just a little while before.

    Alex laughed behind them quietly. “Pretty sure she would have if ‘hollow leg’ over here hadn’t helped herself to the rest of it.”

    “Hey- if it’s turkey legs, sausage on a stick or funnel cake, I don’t mess around.” Kara retorted as she displayed her fingers proudly one at a time to list her favorite fair and carnival foods.

    Just then something caught Kara’s eye and snapped her head around on her shoulders quickly. The oval crown of a gambler hat bounced into view from a nearby crowd, and every ounce of Kara’s blood ran cold. A pungent scent of cigar smoke and mildewed leather sent her reeling.


    It can’t be…

    He can’t be here.


    Lena noticed the wild look in Kara’s eye as if she had just seen a ghost. “Hey—” She whispered over Ruby who continued to lumber forward ungracefully between them. “Are you alright…?”

    Kara managed to blink and shot an unmasked, terrified look at Lena before pulling herself together and coming to her senses. “Yeah—” The blonde gulped. “I just thought I saw something.”

    “Can I have my candy now?” Ruby asked over her shoulder back toward her mothers just then, offering Kara a route of escape from the air of concern that had slowly begun to surround her.

    Kara gave a final nod of her chin to convey to her wife that she was alright, which seemed to satisfy Lena enough to not press the issue any further. Lena then threw her attention back toward Sam, who was eyeing Ruby’s full candy bag with a contemplative expression.

    Sam hummed for a moment in thought. “Okay, but just a couple of pieces. Auntie Lena’s going to help you.”

    Ruby lifted the heavy bag upward toward the raven-haired woman, who then offered her a Reese’s peanut butter cup from it in return.

    “A Cadburys does sound cracker right now. May I have one, Ruby?” Lena asked as she pointed animatedly into the bag with a finger. Ruby busily hummed an “Mhmm” after having shoved the entire Reese’s cup into her mouth and chewed to her heart’s content.

    Kara hid a proud chuckle behind her tightly closed lips. Like herself, her niece had a healthy appreciation for all things sweet, especially chocolate. She watched as Lena dug around into the canvas trick-or-treat bag and fished out a small chocolate bar with a bright purple wrapper. She then watched as Lena paused for a moment, pulling her brow together tightly in consternation with the bar of chocolate between her teeth and dove her hand back into the bag.

    A crisp piece of notebook paper appeared in Lena’s hand, and she began to unfold it carefully as her steps began to slow, causing Sam and Alex behind her to match her pace and ultimately come to a stop.

    “What’s that?” Kara asked. Ruby tugged at Kara’s hand with her curiosity, wanting to see for herself.

    Lena opened her mouth and released the chocolate bar from between her teeth, letting it drop back into the bag. Her face was pale, and her chin quivered as she spoke.

    “Ar scáth a chéile a mhaireann na daoine.”

    The blonde tilted her head with her confusion, as she still did not have a complete grasp of the Irish language. Lena was too stunned to speak for a moment, which prompted concern from Alex who stood just a few steps behind her.

    “Was that in Ruby’s bag?”

    “Lena, what does that mean?”

    The Danvers sisters had spoken over each other as they attempted to discern the reason for Lena’s state of shock, the frightening pallor of her skin instantly setting a deep and thrumming anxiety within Kara. A heavy pause filled the space between them until Lena broke her silence and spoke once more.

    “It literally means ‘We live in each other’s shadows’—” Lena answered with a wild look in her eye and began to turn her head back and forth frantically to look at the people around them.

    “This…” Lena waved the paper emphatically as her eyes continued to dart from one place to the next. “I’ve heard it differently before… and so have you.” She said, looking at Kara again.

    “What do you mean—”

    “Under the shelter of each other, people survive.”

    The phrase was eerily familiar to the blonde, and when it struck her as the same string of words that had been left on the ominous note delivered with the bouquet of flowers to her wife’s office some months before… she froze. Out of the corner of her eye, Kara caught sight of a small red dot travelling over her sister’s arm. If she had blinked, she would have missed it. It jumped from Alex to Lena in a flash and moved higher up Lena’s wrist toward the paper clutched in her hand, eventually coming to a sudden stop there over the messily written words.

    What the hell…?

    Kara thought she couldn’t possibly be seeing what she was seeing right now. It had to be a glare from one of the lamp posts next to them. A trick of light. Another second passed as everyone stood silent and still, but it was when Lena moved her hand with the note that Kara saw the crimson dot move along as if to follow it. Kara barely had time to utter a single word before what happened next. 

    “Alex—”

    A small -thwip- noise flew past their ears, tossing everyone’s hair with an invisible sweeping wind as the paper in Lena’s hand buffeted, appearing to now have a hole through the very middle of it. Alex, recognizing the imminent threat, screamed out into the open.

    “Gun!”

    Bodies darted and pitched forward, throwing themselves to the ground and toward each other. The park erupted in an instant.

    Quick on her feet, Alex ducked and spun to grab at Sam and threw themselves toward Ruby. As if sharing the same mind in that split second, Kara lunged toward Lena to shield her with her body.

    People everywhere began to run and knock each other down in their desperation to flee any open space. Panicked screams filled Kara’s ears as she tried not tripping over her own boots, but after two full strides of a running gait with Lena at her side she came down hard against the sidewalk, barely catching herself before throwing her chin into the pavement. Lena yelled and turned back to pull her by the wrist behind the closest park bench. Getting up and continuing to run was no longer an option, they needed to take cover now. The sound of another bullet hit the furthest end of a wooden slat on the bench then with a hollow -thock-.

    “Alex!” Kara shouted over the back of the bench as another -thock- splintered its wood. “Alex!!”

    Someone yelled for her to get down, but it was no use trying to figure out where anything was coming from in that moment.

    Pointed fingertips pressed into Kara’s biceps so fiercely that if not for the amount of adrenaline currently coursing through her veins, it almost certainly would have been painful. Kara scanned their surroundings for additional cover, doubting very much that the thin wooden planks of the bench which currently shielded them would provide much stopping force of a laser scoped rifle round. Everything around them was too open, and they would have to run several yards to reach the next available structure that could effectively protect them, which was either a metal wheel barrel filled with pumpkins or the park restrooms. The former being much too small to cover them both and the latter being the furthest away.

    Think- goddammit, think!

    “Kara! Where are you!” The sound of Alex’s voice rang out into the air.

    “Here!” Kara shouted back, popping her head up to look over the back of the bench again instinctively to locate her sister.

    “Is Lena alright?” Alex yelled again.

    Lena then lifted a shaking hand upward and waved. “I’m here—”

    -thock-

    A scream shot out of the smaller woman as she yanked her hand back toward her body. Kara reactively dove forward and took it from her, splaying her small palm and fingers wide to look for a wound. Lena’s hand was still intact, as was the rest of her. Kara heaved a breath of relief and tucked her wife’s head downward and into her chest, who began to cry openly into the front of her shirt. The sound of Lena’s shuddered sobs brought Kara’s blood to a boil and with it rose a sickening and somewhat familiar feeling: Anger.

    “Kara- you have to move! You can’t stay there!”

    “Dammit to hell Alex- I’m working on it!”

    Kara turned her head back and forth around them a second time and spied a stack of long, rectangular hay bales set up in a stadium-styled seating arrangement. It occurred to her that if she could reach them, she could use one as a shield and walk both herself and Lena to safety. Wherever that was. Kara just had to hope that one, she could reach one of the bales and grab it before getting shot and two, that whoever was firing at them was not cruel enough to shoot at her feet. Deciding that she would chance it, she turned her focus back down toward her wife, who had calmed some but still rattled from limb to limb. 

    “I’ve got a pl—”

    “I’m going to fecking kill him.” Lena said through gritted teeth.

    The blonde stopped, letting her jaw hang open momentarily with her surprise but then shook herself back onto her train of thought and continued. “Listen- I’m gonna run and grab one of those hay bales over there and walk us outta here, okay?”

    “I’ll go with you—”

    “No, stay here. Only one of us needs to be able hold it and forgive me for being blunt darlin’, but you wouldn’t get very far with it. They’re heavier than they look.”

    “I remember.” Lena replied quietly. “Please be careful.”

    “I will.”

    Kara bent forward and kissed Lena on the lips with a held breath, as if she were committing it to memory. This kiss, should it be her last, would be one that told her wife just how much she loved her, leaving no room for doubt. A long moment turned into two and then was cut short by a small hand that pushed gently at her chest to part them. Lena gazed up into her eyes, silently conveying her trust to which Kara replied with a quick, affirming nod of her chin. She straightened slightly then and turned her head to shout back in the direction of her sister, keeping herself concealed still behind the back of the bench.

    “Please tell me you’ve got people on the way…!”

    Alex hollered back quickly. “I’ve got uniforms from three counties converging on us- what are you about to do?”

    “Can you fire back at this jackass?” Kara asked loudly, ignoring her sister’s question altogether.

    “No, Kara, I cannot just discharge my service weapon at an unknown target that I cannot see!”

    “Do you have it at least—”

    “Of course I do!”

    Now that everyone in their area of the park had vacated during the panic, Kara could hear the sounds of small cries in her sister’s direction. It had to have been Ruby. Kara was suddenly very small again and cowering in the corner of her room back on Danvers Ranch.

    Five year olds are not meant to feel this kind of fear…

    She wanted to freeze. Everything in her body screamed at her in that moment to stop. Kara thought to herself,

    Could I live with myself if I did nothing?

    Kara sighed.

    No. I already did that once.

    Never again.

    She bent forward one more time to kiss Lena on her cheek with a whisper. “I love you.”

    Without another word, Kara twisted and broke into a run toward the hay bales, the only thought in her head being of her hands making contact with its scratchy surface. If she could just reach one, she knew she could lift it quickly enough to keep herself safe. Dirt kicked up from the heels of her boots as she bolted across the last few feet of distance between her and her soon to be shield, and a second later she was plunging her fingers beneath its tight, braided wire and heaving it upward to cover the length of her body.

    “I’ve got it!”

    “Jesus Christ- are you nuts?!” Alex shouted.

    Maybe. Probably. Definitely.

    Kara was still unable to see exactly where her sister had taken cover but knew that wherever it was, she could apparently see her. She began to take careful steps back toward the bench where her wife looked as though she was about to run out from behind it to meet her.

    “Wait, Lena- let me get closer…” Kara warned.

    Lena moved on her hands and knees as close to the end of the bench as she could without revealing any part of her body and paused there.

    -thock- -thock- -thock-

    Kara jumped, this time seeing the actual rounds hit the other end of the bench, which served to piss her off more than it scared her.

    They’re only shooting at Lena… why?

    Once she made it, she gestured with her chin in a short jerk for Lena to come stand with her behind the cover of the hay bale. It grew heavier the longer Kara held it, as she was more used to throwing them and not necessarily walking with them for extended periods of time. Once Lena shot upward and tucked herself flush against Kara’s frame, they began to move away from the bench taking small steps toward the restrooms.

    Finally, the sounds of sirens sounded off in the distance.

    “Calvary’s here- just hold on a little while longer!” Alex shouted.

    After several minutes of painstakingly slow steps, they made it safely behind the cinderblocked structure. Exhausted, Kara released the hay bale with an audible grunt and immediately took a knee to catch her breath. Lena dove to catch her by the shoulders and wrapped her arms around her neck with a relieved cry. The blonde winced, pulling air sharply between her teeth when she reached out to do the same. Two long, deep cuts transected the flesh of her palms from where she had gripped the baling wire. Lena looked down and took them gingerly into her own hands, holding them upward to lessen their bleeding.

    “Why is it always your hands, a mhuirnín…”

    Kara leaned in and kissed her forehead. “We’re gonna be alright—"

    Out of nowhere, heavy footsteps set upon them quickly and Lena threw her body in front of Kara protectively without a single thought. A figure in black, bulky tactical S.W.A.T. gear slowed to a stop in front of them.

    “You two come with me. Stay exactly on my six o’ clock and do everything that I tell you, when I tell you.” The voice of the man inside the padded suit wasted no time in waiting for a reply and motioned with a wave for them to get up and follow him quickly.

    “Extraction team south- two civilians inside perimeter, go for gate- over.” He chirped robotically into his radio, which then blipped a short tone and a static voice called back. “Greenlight to proceed- over.”

     

     

    Everything after that was a blur. Kara’s heightened awareness and sense of danger slowly dissipated as the minutes dragged on, especially once Alex, Sam and Ruby were safe by her side once more. Still, that lingering and incessant itching feeling that attacked the back of her brain between every other thought still left her feeling apprehensive as hell. She began to wonder if that tiny, yet foreboding hint of ‘doom’ would ever leave. Would she be stuck like this always? With that little voice in the back of her head trying its best to convince her that something was very, very wrong… would things ever go back to normal? Would she ever be able to fully relax again? Always waiting for the other shoe to drop sounded exhausting.

    Please God, make it stop.

    “Kara—”

    It was better when I felt nothing at all…

    “Kara.”

    The blonde’s head snapped upward with her eyes wide and full of awareness again. Lena stood there in her space, assessing her up and down from jittery fingers to clenched jaw.

    “Are you alright?”

    Kara nodded with a tuck of her chin and a click of her tongue. “I’m good.”

    Charm would have to suffice where reassurance was weak, because right now Kara was a mess. Regardless of that, the blonde felt that she needed to keep up the appearance that she was a rock, solid and immovable. She knew that Ruby was near, which prompted an immeasurable need to keep calm and otherwise sane in front of her. Her niece was too young to learn that adults lose their shit too. That would come later, hopefully much.

    “Alex said we can go home now.”

    Kara took a step forward and ran the backs of her knuckles along one of Lena’s soft, alabaster forearms. “Okay.”

    “When we get there, can you do something for me?”

    “What’s that?” Kara asked.

    Lena sighed and spoke in a hushed voice only meant for her. “Can you just hold me?”

    Kara smiled. “Oh darlin’, that I can do.”

    Chapter End Notes

    This chapter was a bit fast and more action-packed... Check back Thursday for Ch.13. My notes from here on out will be lighter, and I believe I'll be sticking with the Tues & Thurs updates. Lena's Irish term of endearment "A mhuirnín" (A wur-neen) which means "Beloved” seems to have stuck. If you're familiar with the phrase "it gets worse before it gets better"... we are quickly approaching that "worse" part. But fear not, everything will become right again. There's also a familiar line here from the beginning of LSL, kudos if you can spot it.

    Translations:
    Irish/Slang:
    "Weeun" = small child/children (Wee one)
    "Cracker" = northern Irish slang for something that is very good, exciting or enjoyable
    "Ar scáth a chéile a mhaireann na daoine" = "Under the shelter of each other, people survive" or more literally "We live in each other's shadows" (The title of this story)
    Spanish:
    "Míja" = A term of endearment for small female children (Little girl, baby girl, etc.) Mostly used by adults who share a close relationship to said child.
    "Buena chica" = "Good girl"
    "Así que vamonos" = "So let's go"
    "Ay dios mío, qué susto" = "Oh my god, what a scare" or "You gave me a scare/that scared me"
    "No eres vieja" = "You are not old"

    Chapter 13: My Heart Is in You

    Chapter Summary

    Kara continues to persevere in spite of the emotional battle that rages within. As a last-ditch effort, Alex enlists the help of her wounded family and makes a shocking discovery.

    Chapter Notes

    TW: Blood

    Chapter Thirteen: My Heart Is in You

     

    “There she is- Alex!”

     Kara tossed one hand in the air to wave at her sister while the other clung desperately to Lena’s wrist. The visibly shaken blonde tethered herself to her wife, reluctant to release her for even a single moment.

    “Everyone okay?” Alex huffed between tired breaths from having jogged from the east entrance of the park with her armed escort. Sam and Ruby trailed along just a few steps behind. Kara’s heart lurched in her chest at the sight of Ruby’s twisted crying face. Sam was crouched and shielding her daughter’s eyes and ears against her chest and Alex seemed to look torn between wanting to flit back and forth between her wife and child and her sister.

    There were simply too many places to be all at once, and Kara’s head grew heavier.

    “All fine- but Kara’s hands…” Lena answered as she raised the blonde’s bloodied palms.

    “Shit- SHIT—”

     “They’re just sliced, I’m not shot.” Kara stopped her sister before she could panic. “I’m okay.”

    “What did you even- how is it always your hands?” Alex asked in disbelief as she craned her head down to look at the state of Kara’s wounds.

    “I said the same thing.” Lena admitted, tucking a length of her dark hair behind an ear as she nervously scanned their surroundings for maybe the fourth or fifth time. Kara couldn’t be sure.

    Everything was happening so fast, and Kara’s head grew heavier.

    She stood and watched her wife’s chest heave to and fro with deep breaths. They had run fast, but it seemed far from over. Soon there were more police officers outside of the park than there were civilians, who little by little began to come out from their places of hiding. It was a dull, but noisy, lingering panic.

    Too many sounds were breaking all at the same time, and Kara’s head grew heavier.

    A moment passed and Sam threw her arms around Lena just as Alex bent down to pick Ruby up. The five-year-old wasted no time in wrapping herself tightly around her mother’s frame. Every corner of Kara’s brain screamed for her body to move, to hug, to embrace, but she remained frozen in place.

    “The five of you—” A deep voice called out to them. “Stay put here and don’t move until someone tells you to move.”

    “Hey!” Alex yelled after him. “No one’s reporting back to me- what’s going on?”

    “Stay here, ma’am.” The S.W.A.T. officer replied with a hand held out in a ‘wait’ sort of gesture. Kara recognized him as the one that quickly took her and Lena out of the park.

    “Ma’am…?” Alex parroted to herself as she scrunched her face incredulously. She then gripped at the badge on her belt and gave it a tug. “Does this help?” With that the officer stalled on his feet and then came to a stop. “What’s happening?” She asked again.

    With an impatient exhaled breath, he called a quick reply back into the radio at his shoulder and then turned to face Alex directly.

    “We’re extrapolating bullet trajectories to determine if we have one or two shooters. Until then we need zero movement—”

    “It was one.” Lena chimed in with a shy but determined voice.

    Everyone’s head immediately turned on a swivel to look back at her.

    The raven-haired woman anxiously pawed at an elbow as she shuffled back and forth on her feet. She appeared to be nervous, but also angry in a way. “It was one person.” She said dryly.

    Alex shook her head, confused. “Lena- we can’t know th—”

    “It was Lex.” Lena insisted. “It was my brother.”

    “What makes you say that…?” Sam asked as she took Ruby from Alex’s arms.

    “The note—”

    “The note!” Alex exclaimed, cutting Lena off. “Where is it?!”

    Lena pulled the now crumpled piece of paper from a front pocket of her capri pants and just as she reached out to hand it over to Alex, she stopped suddenly to look directly at Kara.

    “Oh my God, Kara…” Lena said with a horrified intonation.

    The blonde tilted her chin inquisitively at her, not understanding her wife’s sudden look of surprise. Lena darted in front of her with her hands held out toward Kara’s torso.

    “You’re hurt—”

    “No, it’s just my hands, remember?” Kara said, displaying them outwardly in front of herself. She saw that they were covered in blood… but much, much more than what the long cuts in her palms had contributed. It streamed down to her elbows and felt warm, and thick.

    Voices began to yell loudly, and Kara’s head grew heavier.

    “Someone call an ambulance!” Alex shouted toward some uniforms near a parked police interceptor.

    “What do you mean—” Kara inquired innocently as she followed Lena’s eyeline down to her stomach. The color red drenched her light tan Henley down to its sewn hem. Suddenly, she felt very cold where the wetness that soaked her shirt felt very hot against her skin. A tangy, metallic taste spilled past her back teeth then and washed her tongue with a foulness.
