Quickly, her hands flew to the bottom of her shirt and pulled it upward to expose the flat of her stomach where a bullet hole bled profusely in time with the slowing beat of her heart. It spilled out of her in a rhythmic, almost calming tempo as she all of the sudden lost the feeling in her feet. Next to go was the feeling in her legs, the numbness growing there upward and upward further. It pushed its way up to the middle of her chest through the bottom of her lungs and seized them from working entirely. Her mouth opened, but she took in no breath. Startled, she tried again and failed. She was trapped inside of herself now, unable to speak or to breathe.

    Kara’s head grew heavier.

    Her vision began to tunnel rapidly and the chill in her bones grew deeper by the second until the only thing left was the sound of the far away voice shouting her name.

    “Kara!”

    Darkness finally fell over her eyes and the echoing voice came hurtling back, this time louder.

    “Kara!!”

    As if breaking the surface of water, Kara dragged in a ragged gasp and felt her lungs fill with air. She choked, then sputtered for a moment whether to exhale through her nose or out of her gaping mouth and then pulled in another.

    “You’re okay, breathe…”

    The same voice spoke to her, sounding somewhat less frightened now. Kara could feel her legs again, and then the bedsheets on the mattress that lay underneath them, soaked with sweat. She realized that she was no longer standing… but laying.

    “You’re safe.” The sound of Lena’s voice rang in her ears now, and Kara finally realized that they were alone.

    They were in bed, safely at home. The twinkling light of dawn broke over the treetops outside which poured gently in through their bedroom window. It illuminated a bright sheen of sweat over Kara’s ribs that extended below her bra as they banked up and down with her panicked breaths. She could see her skin. She could feel it too. Suddenly, Kara’s hands shot to her stomach and clawed in search of a wound that wasn’t even there.

    Thank fucking God…

    Kara let the back of her head fall again into her pillow and released a breath of relief through her flared nostrils.

    “That was a bad one.” Lena whispered, clinging her knees to her chest from where she sat at the other end of the bed.

    Kara shuffled to sit up further against the headboard and swiped a tuft of golden blonde hair out of her eyes from where it stuck to her damp forehead. She saw the scared look on her wife’s face, and her heart sank with it.

    “I’m sorry.”

    “Don’t be, it’s okay.” Lena reassured her quickly and then released her hands where they were clasped tightly around her shins. “Can I come closer now?”

    “Please.”

     

     

    Sleep came in fitful spells for the remainder of that morning, though Kara managed to still her mind well enough to not awake with another start as she had done during the early hours. Now, she lay with her knee tucked in between Lena’s, who continued to slumber deeply. Kara stared heavily at the dark circles beneath her wife’s eyes and felt her heart plummet toward her stomach again. Neither of them had gotten much in the way of rest as of late, what with Kara’s frantic and unpredictable sleeping patterns and Lena’s persistence to be at her side always, whether she was conscious or not. Kara had tried the couch once, to which Lena adamantly refused even the faintest notion of accepting. Strongly worded reassurances such as “Kara Danvers you will sleep in our bed and nowhere else” and “I trust you, so you should too” from her wife had pretty much put a cork in that discussion, stopping it entirely. Lena wouldn’t hear a word of it and if Kara was being honest with herself, which she found that she was more and more often, she was glad that Lena had stood her ground and made her to sleep in their bed each night. Not doing so would only give cause to bring distance between them, and feelings of resentment upon herself. And that helped no one.

    Kara’s eyes had been open long enough investigating the tiredness that lived on her wife’s face that she accepted the fact that she was awake now, and that there was no chance of any more sleep. Carefully, she rolled out of Lena’s embrace and sat up slowly on the bed to pull in a deep breath. Her neck ached, as did her shoulders and the space in between her shoulder blades. Everything hurt. The sun was bright and hung high overhead now which assaulted Kara’s eyes through the sheer curtains of their bedroom window. She wondered just how long she could make the morning last, if not indefinitely. There was nothing that she wanted on the other side of their bedroom door. Not today, anyways. Maybe not even tomorrow.

    Moments later the mattress behind her dipped with movement and Kara turned her head to see Lena scooting herself up with her hips toward the headboard to sit up. The raven-haired woman blinked sleepily as her eyes adjusted to the brightness of the room and pulled in a long yawn.

    “Well?” She asked, stretching slightly. She seemed to be just as stiff, and in all the same places.

    Kara merely nodded in her direction and clasped her hands into her lap. Lena paused, casting her eyes down to the quilt still covering her legs.

    “I suppose a nod’s as good as a wink to a blind horse.” Lena said under her breath, acknowledging her disappointment that Kara still didn’t feel much like talking. Sometimes she rather felt like begging. It was either that or cry quietly in parts of the house where her wife could not hear her.

    But Lena did not feel like crying today, so she tried again.

    “Don’t forget your appointment later…”

    Kara bristled slightly. “I know.”

    Lena tossed the covers away from her body and moved on her hands and knees across the bed to sit behind the blonde without touching her.

    “Can I ask how you’re getting on with it?”

    A long sigh fell out of Kara as she straightened her back and exhaled deeply toward the window. Then she turned on her hips and pulled a knee back onto the bed to sit face to face with Lena. Her tired eyes radiated with a familiar kindness as she stared her wife in the face.

    “Yes.”

    Lena tossed her shoulders and gave an inquisitive tilt of her chin. “So…?”

    The blonde paused and danced her eyes over the features of Lena’s face, taking her in slowly.

    “It’s uh- She says it’s a process.”

    Lena dropped her chin into a nod of her own then and seemed to accept the fact that Kara maybe just didn’t have enough to say about her therapy appointments yet. She had only been to a handful so far, anyway. But a process it was, and it had disheartened Kara at first when she had been told that it would take years to unpack all of the self-applied guilt and shame that she had layered onto herself throughout her life. It had almost made her step right back out of her therapist’s office on that very first visit, as it had immediately struck her as a monumental waste of time, but she had been talked back down with her hand still on the doorknob and eventually resolved to sit back down and complete her first session.

    The following sessions hadn’t felt as though there had been any ‘progress’ concerning her high level of stress and anxiety, not to mention her sleeplessness. It was only during her most recent appointment when somehow her therapist had managed to get her to admit the reason why she was not vocal about her own feelings.

    Words of affirmation were something that Kara had no difficulty with, but only when she offered them to the people she cared for most in the world. That, for whatever reason, came naturally. It was easy telling Lena what she admired about her, and how much she loved her. When it came to herself however, those same feelings simply had no room to exist, and it had everything to do with the years of witnessing the people she looked up to either physically recoiling from her affections or becoming visibly repulsed whenever she had been open with her feelings. To a child, feeling as though the adults in your life really did not wish for you to speak more than two or three sentences at a time had a severely destructive effect that no one seemed to put together at the time, or seemed to even care about for that matter. The amount of shame that came with that neglect had her withdraw emotionally at a very young age, and she had stayed there inside of her head ever since.

    “I’m proud of you.” Lena whispered as she leaned closer.

    Kara smiled. “Thanks.”

    A sharp inhale shot through the dark-haired woman sitting in front of her as she tossed her brow high with wide, blinking eyes as if to reinvigorate herself, then she clapped her hands onto her thighs and bounced where she sat.

    “Cupán tae?” Lena asked with a fresh smile on her face.

    The blonde huffed a pleased sound with a gust of breath through her nose, which very nearly sounded like a small laugh, causing the smile sitting on Lena’s face to rise higher onto her cheeks.

    “Sure.” Kara answered.

    With that, Lena fell forward quickly and planted a small kiss on the slope of Kara’s jaw. “Okay.”

    The sounds of Lena’s barefooted steps out into the hallway faded with the ringing of Kara’s phone on the nightstand beside her, and she reached out to answer it with an efforted groan as she bent over.

    “Hello?”

    “Hey Kara—” Alex’s voice was calm, but curious.

    “What’s up?”

    “I need to come by and show y’all some photos, if that’s okay? I know things have been rough and Sam says y’all are taking some time off to be at home but—"

    “It’s cool. Come on, then.”

    Her sister paused on the other end for a moment. “Are you sure?”

    “Yeah- yeah it’s fine.”

    “Alright, thanks. I just really need some help with something.”

    Kara furrowed her brow slightly. “With what?”

    “It’s… better if I just show you.”

    “Okay.” Kara replied after pulling in another sigh.

    “I’ll be over in a bit.” Alex said, and then the call had ended.

    The blonde tapped the lock screen button on the side of her phone and tossed it face down onto the top quilt of the bed, and then turned her face back toward the sun’s rays coming through the window. She figured she may as well get on with the day, knowing that the sooner she got up and began to do things around the house, the quicker it would be over with and she could struggle though another night of restless sleep just to greet another slow morning.

    Shit.

    Kara suddenly groaned upwardly into the air.

    Now I gotta put pants on.

     

     

    Folders stuffed thick with unspecified papers and forms littered the round coffee table in the seating area of the house, nearly covering the expanse of its glass surface from edge to edge. Lena returned from the kitchen with two tea plates in hand balancing a nearly full cup in each. Porcelain chittered with each of her careful steps until she bent down and rested them on the table in front of Kara and Alex, being sure to first move some of the scattered paperwork out of the way to make room.

    The sisters Danvers were both staring intently at flight manifests which Alex had explained with a slight air of frustration spanned more than a few months. Each of their brows were screwed together tightly as they took in the magnitude of Alex’s workload. Kara, snapping out of her consternation with the subtle biting noise a tea plate made when it had been sat upon glass, blinked upward at her wife who smiled back down at her endearingly. Kara reached up then and took Lena by one of her small wrists and tugged her lightly to sit in between herself and her sister on the long, tanned leather couch.

    “Alex has something she needs to tell us.” The blonde said in a hushed tone toward Lena, who sat ramrod straight with her hands clasped and her knees pulled tightly together. She nodded and then turned her face toward Alex.

    “That and I could actually use your help, I think.” Alex admitted.

    Lena quirked her head agreeingly and unclasped her hands to run her palms dry along the tops of her blue jeaned thighs. “Okay.”

    Kara leaned forward to catch her sister’s eyes and widened her own with a knowing look, as if to say, “Get on with it.”

    “So.” Alex began, “Just so we can get the shock of it all out of the way, I need to preface all of this—” She gestured outwardly with her hands toward the mountain of papers, receipts and photographs strewn about on the table in front of them. “…by saying that we are investigating your brother, Lena.”

    The raven-haired woman took a moment to inhale deeply and ran her eyes over the table, knowing now what it all meant and then gave a small nod in return. “What do you need from us?”

    Alex blinked for a few seconds to gather in what had been an unexpectedly quick emotional turn around, and then reached forward to grab a folder near her.

    “Full disclosure; I’ve pretty much run out of time with my unit chief to give him enough evidence for an arrest in regard to the bombing- so it’s been just me working it, and I know now that I need additional pairs of eyes on all the material I’ve collected. And this—” Alex shook a hand out flatly at the table again. “This isn’t exactly protocol. I’m not actually supposed to be showing y’all, or anyone for that matter, anything that has to do with an on-going investigation. But this is what it’s gonna take, so I’m willin’ to run the risk. The goal is to either tie him to something, or rule him out and find a new lead. One of those things needs to happen, and fast.”

    At the other end of the couch, Kara leaned and grabbed a stapled stack of papers to draw them close to her face. She flipped them back and forth as if trying to make heads or tails of their purpose.

    “What’s this?”

    Alex squinted at top page of the papers in Kara’s hands for a second before replying. “Customs forms. We’re looking for… his name.”

    “You can say it, you know…” Lena added with a small, almost shy voice.

    Alex dropped her mouth open in surprise. “Say what?”

    “Lex… Alexander Joseph Luthor.”

    They held themselves still with a short pause before the sound of Alex’s voice broke the uncomfortable silence. “You’re right.” She agreed, shifting a bit in her seat. “Lex. We’re looking for Lex’s name, or the name of Luthor anywhere on these flight manifests—” Alex said as she gently waved the folder in her hand. “Or any names that stick out more than once, especially if you cross them against those customs forms.”

    The loud -clack- of a teacup being placed on the glass top of the table a little too hard made both Lena and Alex jump slightly, and their heads both turned toward the blonde sitting on the other end of the couch. Kara appeared red in the face and was clenching her teeth hard enough to raise a stiff temporomandibular joint at the end of her jaw. A fist unfurled and settled back into her lap when she straightened her back and attempted to take in a long pull of a breath to relax. Clearly, the mere mention of Lex’s name had agitated her beyond her ability to conceal her feelings on the matter. It seemed that Kara had reached a limit for the first time in her life. What she could and could not handle. Which, being shot at for four and a half minutes seemed like a reasonable enough assessment of “I don’t fucking like that shit.”

    The blonde quickly felt the concerned pairs of eyes against the side of her face and turned to give her wife a short, reassuring look that said, “But I’m alright.”

    Lena was the one to break the tension as she stood and crossed her arms in front of the couch. “Have you eaten breakfast, Alex?”

    The reddish-haired woman tilted her head slightly and sat back against the couch. “Oh, I had- Well this morning, I um...”

    Lena narrowed her eyes toward the woman fumbling to come up with an answer. Kara caught on quickly and leaned across the couch to give her sister a light smack on the arm with her stack of customs forms.

    “Don’t bother thinkin’ too hard, you might hurt yourself. She’s gonna make you eat either way.”

    Alex tucked her chin inwardly and let out a small gasp, seemingly unprepared for her sister’s nonchalant teasing.

    “Hey!” Alex took a swipe back at Kara with her own folder, who dodged and sat back with a small smile. “I just couldn’t remember.”

    “Which means you haven’t.” Lena added with an air of confidence as she turned to walk toward the kitchen.

    “How do you even know that?” Alex called out to the raven-haired woman exiting the living room.

    Lena called back to her once she entered the kitchen. “I know your sister.” The sound of pots and pans echoed into the living room as they were pulled out of cabinets. “And you two share more traits than just an obsessive fondness for chocolate pecan pie.”

    The look on Alex’s face morphed into that of a deer staring into headlights. Kara chuckled to herself upon seeing her sister so aghast. Alex leaned closer to Kara then and whispered, “You gave her Mama’s chocolate pecan pie recipe?”

    Kara shrugged her shoulders innocently. “Well, I sure as hell can’t make it… I burn air.”

    For the first time in weeks, the sounds of laughter filled the Luthor-Danvers household once again.

     

     

    Throughout the next few hours Lena tended to both her wife and Alex with food and water, and the occasional urging to stand up and stretch their legs. She figured that a break every now and again may end up proving helpful in the way that the human brain requires time to reset in order to take in new information.

    “There’s nothing.” Alex said with a tired huff. “Just… nothing.”

    The oldest Danvers woman tossed a stack of receipts toward the table which fell short and landed near Kara’s feet. The blonde leaned down to take them and flipped through them curiously with her thumb and forefinger as if spreading a deck of cards.

    “Where are these from?”

    Alex hummed and folded her arms over her chest. “Those are from the corner store near where he’s- Lex… is holed up in that hotel off I-35 near Wells Point.”

    Kara scrunched her eyebrows together with her confusion. “And why are they important?”

    “Well, there’s that hardware store right next to it… and I’m really just grasping at straws here- but if I can match bomb-making material purchases to the same dates that we surveilled him going in and out of that corner store…”

    “I’m not following.” Kara interrupted. She wanted to be able to follow her sister’s train of thought so that she could actually help. It pained her to think that there was nothing useful she could do with all of it.

    Alex leaned an elbow on one of her knees and wrapped a hand around her forehead, massaging her temples firmly. She then sighed and continued to explain, “That entire strip mall was built by the same developer back in the sixties, and each of those storefronts all have one thing in common. Connecting interior doors. Winn says that it was a popular and cheap work-around to appease building inspectors looking for fire code violations back then, but I think he’s full of—"

    “So… he wouldn’t have to actually go through the front door…”

    Alex threw her hands into the air with a flourish that said “Now she gets it” and fell tiredly into the back of the couch.

    “Okay- so show me those pictures again.” Kara asked just as the buzzer on the dryer in the laundry room near the back of the house went off.

    Alex leaned forward again and scooped a handful of the surveillance photos toward herself and spread them out across the table as Kara scooted closer to sit on the edge of the couch.

    “And these are of the hotel he’s staying at?” The blonde asked with a pointed finger.

    Alex nodded and pulled one photo out from under the rest. “Yep. That’s him going in—” She said, tapping it with a knuckle.

    Kara stiffened at the sight of Lex in broad daylight again, walking with his head down toward the front of the hotel.

    “These are all you’ve got?”

    Her sister grumbled under her breath for a moment at the question and then sat back again. “Chief cut back on the surveillance after a few days of nothing. It was either take one shot at it and bring him in for questioning, which would only spook him if I couldn’t find anything to jam him up with… or tail him. I practically lived off of gas station coffee and burgers in an unmarked until the plug got pulled.” Alex leaned her head into her hands and rubbed it again, looking defeated. “I’ve made zero progress this whole time and I’m starting to think that I’m just lookin’ for shit that ain’t even there. Don’t get me started on the office building employees from downtown… nobody saw anything, nobody heard anything, blah blah blah.”

    Just then Lena stepped back into the room carrying a full laundry basket of freshly dried clothes. “I’ll just be upstairs folding- do you need anything right now babe?”

    Kara looked up from the photo she held in her hand and attempted a smile at her wife. She was just so goddamned tired. Lena looked back at her with a small frown.

    “Are those more photos? Can I have another quick look?” The dark-haired woman asked, seemingly just as eager to help as her wife had been.

    “Please.” Alex cut in abruptly, her voice echoing with the sound of her desperation.

    Lena strode over and sat the laundry basket next to the table and stood with her hands on her hips as she peered down at the few dozen photos laid out along the round edge of the table. Kara continued her line of questioning toward her sister after another few minutes or so, trying her best to offer some spark of hope or overlooked clue. “None of them had anything useful to say?”

    Alex squinted for a moment until she realized who Kara was talking about. “The people in Lena’s office? Nah. Not a peep.”

    “Really?” Kara asked again with a hint of disbelief.

    “Marketing can be a somewhat cutthroat industry. Reps can be pretty tight-lipped by force of habit. Mine are, anyways.” Lena chimed in, not bothering to take her eyes off the photos below.

    “You’re tellin’ me. And that’s just the ones that I could find… that Eve, uh—” Alex paused as she snapped her fingers near her own head to recall the name.

    “Eve Teschmacher?”

    “Yeah-" Alex exclaimed with a slight bounce. “That one, she’s all but vanished- still haven’t been able to track her down—”

    “Well she’s just there, isn’t she…” Lena interrupted with a point of her finger toward a blonde woman in one of the photos on the table.

    “What are you talking about—”

    “That’s Eve…”

    In that moment, the drop of a pin could have been heard from clear across the house. 

    “That’s Eve?!” Alex echoed and snatched the photo off the top of the table to hold it out toward Lena. “Are you sure?”

    “Yes, I’m sure, Alex- I know my own secretary, don’t I?” Lena insisted as she sat down on the other side of Kara, who was now turning her head back and forth between her wife and her sister vigorously enough to injure her neck.

    “You didn’t think to stop and talk to her when you took this picture in front of my office?” Lena added.

    Alex paused with her jaw slack in surprise before she clapped it shut and swallowed hard. “That’s not your building, Lena… That’s the hotel your brother has been staying at.”

    Lena dropped her hands into her lap and sputtered for words. “I—”

    “We clocked this woman—” Alex pointed repeatedly to Eve in the picture. “…Going in and out of the hotel about half a dozen times before our stake out got called off.”

    The blood drained from Lena’s face as the realization struck her. She hadn’t heard from her secretary since she had told Lena that she was taking some time away to recuperate after the bombing, and after all this time Lena supposed that she had just forgotten. With everything that had happened since… Lena guessed that it had been easy to lose track of who she had been keeping in touch with now that all projects and campaigns out of her Austin office had been paused in the interim. They were hemorrhaging contract pause fees until further notice, but the current state of her family was infinitely more important to her. Lena realized only now the number of emails she had sent to Eve over the following weeks that had gone without reply.

    “Oh my God.”

     

     

    Nothing felt real. It was as if Lena was living outside of her body as she stood with her hand on the ornate bronze handle of their front door. After their revelation that Lena’s secretary Eve had not actually skipped town but was in fact seen going in and out of the hotel where Lex was living, Alex quickly gathered up all of her investigative materials and was in a hurry to get back to the station to start putting her next move into place.

    “Listen…” Alex said as she reassured Lena with a soft hand at her shoulder. “This is good, I finally have the missing piece of this puzzle thanks to you.”

    Lena withered slightly and darted her eyes, struggling to find words. “I…” She trailed off into a pitiful whine to convey that there just weren’t any for how flummoxed she had been by the photograph, but Alex understood perfectly.

    Kara stood closely at her side and inconspicuously slipped her hand into Lena’s smaller one, which helped to settle her nerves a great deal. Her wife had an unbelievable talent for recognizing when she needed to be held.

    “It’s alright, Lena.” Alex reminded her again in her haste to leave. “Before, even with the note from the park and the other one from the flowers at your office I hadn’t been able to convince the district attorney that they were connected just based on translation alone, but now that I can connect someone from your office to Lex directly, I can probably get a judge to sign off on a warrant to search his room at the hotel. Screw the DA.”

    Alex paused for a moment before continuing. “I’m going to ask you one last time- do you really think it was your brother Lex that shot at us in the park?”

    Lena swallowed past the dryness that caught in the middle of her throat and made her want to choke. “I do.”

    Alex nodded and then took a step back to leave.

    “He’s an expert marksman.” Lena added, stopping Alex in her tracks. “If he wanted me- or anyone dead… then that’s how it would have happened.”

    “Duly noted.” Alex replied softly.

    It wasn’t until the large front door of their home shut securely in front of her that she began to feel her body again. Lena felt like she was going to be sick.

    “I need to sit down.” The raven-haired woman managed to squeak.

    Kara placed a hand at her lower back and began to turn back toward the living room area, but Lena stayed completely motionless. The blonde pulled her eyebrows together with her bemusement and did her best to direct Lena’s thousand yard stare back toward her.

    “Can you walk?” Kara asked.

    Lena shook her head. “I- I’m not sure.”

    Kara moved to wrap her arms around Lena then and began to sway from side to side with the smaller woman held tightly against her front. The side of her face rested against Lena’s warm temple as she exhaled slowly, as if to breathe an amount of calmness into her. After a long moment she whispered softly into her ear, “I’ve got you.”

    When Lena seemed to ease somewhat, Kara reached down and scooped the backs of her knees with an arm and lifted her into a bridal carry. It was all Lena could do not to fall apart at her joints, but she knew that if she had, her wife would be right there to kneel down and pick her back up again. She sighed and leaned her head against Kara’s firm pectoral just above her breastbone and listened to the deep, steady drumming there in her chest.

    “Tá mo chroí istigh ionat.”

    A small peck of a kiss fell upon her forehead before Kara replied, “And mine in you.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Stay tuned for next Tuesday, you won't want to miss it. Lena's strong resolve and deep well of patience seem to be running dry, don't they? Do you blame her?

    Thank you for all of the comments on the last chapter. I'm pleased to know people have still been enjoying this ride.

    Translations:
    Irish:
    "Tá mo chroí istigh ionat" = "My heart is in you", a heartwarming Irish expression of love.
    "Cupán tae" = Cup of tea

    Chapter 14: An Autumn Promise

    Chapter Summary

    Kara reaches a limit. A secret is revealed, and a heart is broken.

    Chapter Notes

    A multi-POV chapter.

    Chapter Fourteen: An Autumn Promise

       

    Some would claim that swinging an axe has a sort of therapeutic effect. For Kara, this was absolutely true. As the days passed, the blonde found herself more and more often in the vast expanse that was the back yard of their home. With neighbors too far off in distance to drop in unannounced for idle, mid-day conversation, the large house sat on an even bigger lot with plenty of space on all sides, where its back property line bordered along a thick grove of small, skinny trees. It was a thicket of sticks, rather, and much too cumbersome to walk through without tearing a pant leg on saw vines or getting poked in the eye. Kara felt comfortable in such an open space that held so much privacy, and splitting logs for their winter wood pile was practically the only thing that brought her comfort lately, as it was the only thing that emptied the swirling vortex of chaos that lived in between her ears. Everything was fine, just as long as she kept swinging.

    -thwack-

    Two long halves of a log toppled their separate ways off the large, sheared stump slice that was her chopping block, and each fell down onto their respective, clumsy piles with hollow, woody -clunks-. The cracking echo of Kara’s axe calmed her as it traveled back from the distant wooded barricade. Here, she knew exactly what to expect. Set up the log. Raise the axe. Bring it down. Repeat. Her deltoids and triceps burned with a sense of familiarity, much like they did whenever she would throw bags of feed or pitch hay with a fork. Admittedly, the five-pound splitting axe took care of most of the labor but her arms would eventually tire, and then she would be stuck with her thoughts again. When all else failed, hard work was the thing that grounded her and kept her sane.

    -thwack-

    Kara gazed at the growing piles on either side of her splitting block and then twisted her head to look over her shoulder toward the back of the house where the wood pile sat. The structure was built six foot in length by four foot in height and stood several feet from the back wall of the house so as to not attract a host of termites into their home. Kara had seen fit to construct it after witnessing the exorbitant price of their electric bill during their first cold season in Austin, saying “To hell with that, we can make our own heat” and in true fashion had leaned heavy on that with innuendo, because that was also the evening they had broken their first bed frame. Things had been good then. Now, their wood pile was stacked full and had been for days, but Kara continued to swing her axe.

    -thwack-

    Two more halves fell away from the stump slice where they clattered noisily onto the ground. Kara leaned and set up another by letting the weight of the axe head come down onto the end of a log that she trapped under her boot heel. The tapered blade bit into the wood and came up with the axe, and with an abrupt bounce of its handle the log dropped onto the chopping block and teetered for a moment before coming to rest. Squaring her feet, Kara took both hands at the pommeled end of the handle and raised it over her head, but the sound of a voice calling out her name stopped her from letting gravity do its thing.

    “Kara! Phone—”

    Lena stepped out onto the back porch with Kara’s phone in her hand, offering it skyward as if the blonde could see the name of who was calling from where she stood near the tool shed, which was effectively the size of small barn. Kara dropped her axe onto her shoulder and turned toward the house with a twist of her upper body, displaying her oblique and latissimus dorsi muscles tightly against the pulled fabric of her long-sleeved, plaid buttoned shirt.

    “Tell ‘em I’ll call back later.”

    Brainy knew to leave voicemails if there was anything she needed to be made aware of at the shop and Nia, who had a very outspoken and adverse reaction to talking on the phone, usually texted. Her other employees simply called the shop phone for anything else. Parts suppliers did not have her private cell number, and when they managed to get it, they didn’t end up keeping her business for long.

    “It’s Alex.” Lena replied into the cool November breeze as she began to walk toward the blonde with the phone. Kara bristled for a moment, then turned back to the upright log and quickly lifted her axe from her shoulder, bringing it down with another splitting -thwack-.

    It wasn’t that she didn’t want to her from her sister. It was the fact that she absolutely dreaded what it was she might have to say. Kara leaned for another log that she kicked under her foot just as Lena came up behind her with a soft touch on her axe hand, stopping her.

    “Please baby… Just talk to her for a minute and take a break?” Lena asked. Though her words were compassionate, they didn’t exactly land as a request. Kara knew better than to try and find a way out of it. Plus, her arms felt heavy and somewhat desperate to hang limply at her sides with their tiredness.

    Nodding with her agreement, Kara dropped the axe head into the middle of the block where it sank neatly, jutting its handle out at a firm two o’clock position and then took the phone from Lena’s patient hand.

    “I’ll get you a glass of water.”

    Kara began to shake her head. “That’s alright darlin’, I’m not thirsty—”

    “I didn’t ask if you were thirsty.” Lena interrupted with a comforting smile, and then leaned forward on her toes to place a kiss upon Kara’s cheek before turning back toward the house.

    The blonde stood gobsmacked into silence for a moment with her wife’s well-learned southern sass and then lifted the phone to her ear.

    “Hello?”

    “Kara.”

    Oh yeah, Alex. Right.

    “I wanted to call and tell you that we’ve got him.” Alex said calmly on the other end.

    Kara remained still, nearly breathless. All this time putting off the thought of how she’d feel when this moment arrived did exactly what she expected. It left her ill prepared and anxious as hell.

    “Lex. We’ve got—”

    “I know who you’re talking about.” Kara blurted at the sound of his name. Her words harped a little stronger than she meant them to, but Alex would just have to understand.

    “Okay…” Her sister replied, stepping around her own defensiveness. “I want you to know that you’re safe now. Both of you. Everything’s gonna be fine.”

    Kara toed her boot in the dirt for a moment where there used to be grass. She had spent many an autumn afternoon here over the years, running a track back and forth between her chopping block and the wood pile at the back of the house.

    “How can you be sure?” The blonde decided to ask after a beat.

    “I—” Alex stammered for a second. “Well, there’re things I can and can’t tell you right now, but what I can tell you is that while I’ve got him under my thumb, the man won’t be able to so much as spit without me knowing about it.”

    Kara paused for another moment and chewed the inside of her lip before speaking again. “Okay.”

    “…Are you alright?” Her sister’s voice now casting a shade of concern through the phone. “Have you been going to therapy still—”

    “Yes.” Kara replied with a curt tone. Clearly, she was not in the mood for a heart-to-heart, least of all one that involved how fucking terrified she had been for so long. But Kara had her reasons for keeping it all to herself.

    What good would it do to tell anyone?

    Why make everyone else scared too?

    It won’t last forever.

    …

    Will it?

    She would never claim that they were good reasons… but they were hers to handle so nobody else had to.

    “Let me let you go, Alex. But um- thanks for callin’.”

    “Okay… I love you.”

    “I love you too.”

    Kara couldn’t shove her phone into her back pocket quick enough. Why did everything lately irritate her so much? She supposed her continuing lack of sleep more than likely had something to do with it. That and everyone seemed to take the knowledge that she was now speaking with a therapist as an invitation to have different conversations. Ones that made her want to crawl outside of her skin.

    Kara gripped the curved wooden pommel of the axe and pulled it free from the block, swapping it back and forth in her hands before bending down to set up another log. Why was this the only thing that cleared her head? She was just so damn tired, but she knew better than to lay her head down. That would only restart the horror movie of unlocked childhood memories and recent events that plagued her every thought.

    The sound of the bomb going off in her head… Every time she reached to hug Eliza as a child who would then scold her for being too close underfoot… The panicked screams from the park… A chewed cigar stuck between an impish, veneered smile… The loud clang of metal from a runaway display wheel… Every time she glimpsed the fear in her wife’s eyes as they stared at her from the opposite end of the bed…

    God, it killed her to know that she had scared Lena. Why was everything so much more difficult to keep a lid on now? Kara had always managed to persevere, in her own way. But her body had been betraying her for some time now, and she was slowly becoming more and more aware that she really did not have a say in it at all.

    Why is it so much harder?

    She let her arms come down with another -thwack- and the log halves fell to their sides, which unfortunately did nothing to quell her racing thoughts now.

    Why is it all going wrong?!

    Kara lifted the axe again in a sudden burst of anger and brought it down with all of her might, cleaving the heavy chopping block itself completely in half.




    Alex checked the thermostat outside of Interview Room One for the third time as she paced in front of the two-way mirror. Lex Luthor was sitting on the other side of that mirror, cuffed securely to a bolt that rose from the middle of a table. The room held him, a chair with one leg shorter than the rest which made it rock if you weren’t mindful of where you placed your weight in it at all times, a stainless steel table, and another chair with four perfectly level legs. An intense overhead light beamed down onto the baldness of his head, no doubt adding a measure of warmth into the already stuffy room.

    She had held him for two hours before picking up the phone to call her sister to give her the good news. It was against protocol to divulge making an arrest, but if anything, she merely wished to ease Kara’s troubled mind. What troubled her own though, was the fact that no lawyer or public defender had made an appearance on Lex’s behalf, which was strange. She’d have to walk a tightrope for information if her suspect was without counsel and she were to question him. Alex checked the thermostat near the door one more time where it still read seventy-seven degrees and chose to finally enter the room.

    “Mr. Luthor, I’m Detective Danvers—" Her stride was confident as she made her way toward the middle of the room without so much as looking at him. “There are a few questions I need to ask you- but first—” Alex fanned the collar of her shirt from around her neck with her hand several times to convey how uncomfortable the heat was in such a small room. “Let me see if we can’t fix the air in here, maybe we can get someone from maintenance down today- it may take a few hours though. I’ll go and grab you some water…” She quickly turned on her heels and was halfway through the door again when Lex began to babble his discomfort. When the door latched shut all the way behind her, he proceeded to shout.

    “Don’t leave me in here again- Hey!" He yelled toward the mirror, pounding the sides of his fists onto the table with the chain banging against its metal loudly. “Come back!”

    Alex smirked on the other side of the mirror glass and walked over to the thermostat to tap it back down to a comfortable room temperature. When she opened the door again, the a/c kicked on and the man handcuffed to the table dropped his shoulders with a relieved sigh as cool air began to push through the ceiling vents.

    “Do you recognize me?” Alex said stiffly as she sat down into the empty chair across from him.

    Lex screwed his eyes together as if to feign ignorance and then darted them to the left. A first sign of dishonest behavior. Alex clocked it immediately.

    “No. Should I, now?” He asked arrogantly.

    “Oh, that’s right, you probably only saw the back of my head when you shot at my family.”

    Lex huffed a pretentious breath through his nose with the full use of his chest and drew his hands on the table closer to his body until the length of chain from his cuffs pulled taut. Another sign of guilt. There was a reason Alex had graduated top of her class in the academy and had been elevated to Detective so quickly in her career. She could smell a lie from a mile away. It was like blood in the water, and she was a shark.

    “You’ve got shite for proof.” He sneered.

    “If by proof you mean the Dragunov long-scope hidden at your girlfriend’s house, then that’s all the shite I need.”

    Lex’s eyes went wide with that.

    “I don’t think poor Eve got the memo, because the rest of us girls learned to quit hiding our valuables under our mattresses when we were about fifteen.” Alex stood suddenly and leaned the top half of her body over the table, bringing herself just out of headbutting distance; A mistake she had made only once as a rookie during one of her first arrests.

    “Let me tell you something…” Alex said as she narrowed her eyes, her voice as cold as the grave. “I. Do. Not. Like. Being shot at.” One of her hands came down flat on top of the table with a loud bang, making the man sitting below her jump slightly. “And when my guys from ballistics match that slug we pulled out of the sidewalk to the barrel of that rifle which you and I both know is gonna be covered in your prints- where the hell do you even get a Dragunov in this city anyway—” She quickly waved herself free of the derailed thought with her other hand in the air and continued.

    “I am gonna put you in a hole so deep and so dark, and with so many bars that you will have wished you had never, ever seen sunlight a day in your life.”

    Lex began to stutter as beads of sweat rolled into his eyes. “I—”

    A commotion outside the room stopped him and simultaneously turned Alex’s head toward the door at her back. Voices were yelling now as they grew closer and then suddenly, the door burst open and slammed against the concrete wall behind it with a loud -bang-. A rush of blonde hair ran into the room, and two strong arms scooped up the man in the chair and pinned him against the far wall, dragging the metal table along with them.

    “You…!”

    Alex froze, dumbfounded for a split second as she watched her sister shake with a rage that she had never seen before. Kara shoved hard against Lex’s chest with her forearms, pushing all of the air out of his lungs with an audible whine. A uniform followed directly behind her, which then sprang Alex into action.

    “Kara!” Alex shouted, crossing the room quickly. “Stop!”

    “You shot at my wife?” Kara snarled as she furled her fists into Lex’s sweater and yanked him away from the wall only to pound him against it again. “My niece was there- she’s five!”

    Alex continued to scream for Kara to let him go, but her voice did nothing to sway her sister. Kara only gripped Lex tighter and dragged her fists up to his chin, effectively lifting him clear off of his feet.

    “I’m not afraid of you!” Kara yelled as she pushed into him as hard as she could now. “I’m not afraid of you anymore!”

    A slight purple hue began to flush Lex’s cheeks from the force being applied to his chest. Alex could see that he couldn’t breathe now as she kept trying to pry her sister’s arms away, but it was futile, like pulling a deep rooted stump out of the ground. The officer pulling at Kara’s other arm seemed to be having the same amount of difficulty as he locked eyes with Alex for a brief moment, and that’s when the detective knew that it would take more than just the two of them.

    “I need officers in interrogation, now!” Alex shouted toward the still open door. Three more uniforms had already been heading their way down the long hallway of the precinct toward the room and filed in hurriedly one after the other to pull Kara away from Lex just a few moments later.

    Now less purple and able to choke out a breath, Lex began to shout in a language that Alex couldn’t understand. She had no idea what he was saying, but she surmised that it was all along the lines of very Irish, and very inventive ways of cursing. Astounded that it took four full grown men to force Kara back out of the room and into the hallway, Alex followed and pulled the door hard back on its hinges where it screeched brokenly, barely locking back into its frame.

    With an angry huff, Alex spun around and shooed the officers away when they began to cuff Kara’s wrists behind her back. There would be hell to pay for safeguarding her sister from the law but seeing Kara in handcuffs was not something that she wanted to witness, ever.

    Once she was sure no one else was within earshot, Alex dove a hand into the collar of Kara’s flannel button down shirt and quickly dragged her toward Interview Room Three at the other end of the precinct. Once the door shut behind them, she gave Kara a stiff shove at her shoulders which did nothing to move the blonde at all, sort of like pushing a boulder with a flyswatter.

    “What the hell is the matter with you?!” Alex began to shout. “Have you gone and lost your goddamn mind?!”

    “Yes!”     Kara screamed back without a drop of hesitation and trailed into one wrenching, open sob. Her face contorted into a pained expression as she rattled in another shaky breath. “Yes.”

    The word fell out of the blonde like a confession, making Alex take a surprised step back. Kara looked devastated and scared, quite like a trapped animal weighing its options for either escape or death. Alex softened her tone then, but still went after her sister for an answer. She knew something had been awry with her for a while now, but this was not something she had expected at all and knew that there was no way in hell that she was going to let it go any further. This was not okay.

    “Let me explain something to you—" Alex returned a step forward and wagged a trembling finger toward her sister. “You are going to come back up here in a few days after you have got your shit together, and you are gonna fix that door…” She gestured toward the other end of the building where Interview Room One sat and then brought her finger back toward Kara.

    “But first, you are gonna sit your ass right there in that chair and tell me what the hell is going on, or so help me God, I will throw you in lock-up myself.”

    Kara sat immediately, wiping a runaway tear with a jittery hand. She shook her head then, glancing up at her sister who stood in front of her with folded arms.

    “I don’t know.”

    “What do you mean ‘you don’t know’- did you not drive all the way across town to my place of work to burst in on one of my interrogations and assault an unarmed man? An unarmed man chained to a table no less…” Alex began to pace. “This is bad, Kara. My chief is going to have my head on a damn spike- Lex can sue the department, he can get his case thrown out…”

    Of all the bad luck Alex had weathered in pulling this case together, and all the hours she had put into it, being away from home, being away from Sam and away from Ruby… the thought of it all going down the drain made her sick to her stomach. Alex clapped a hand over her mouth to calm herself and stopped her anxious pacing to stand in front of her sister again.

    “I’m sorry, Alex.” Kara tried again.

    “Sorry doesn’t cut it, Kara!” Alex exclaimed as she took a step forward and kneeled at Kara’s feet. She would beg if she had to. The most important thing right now was her sister. Everything else could wait.

    “Please.” Alex took Kara’s hands into her own and brought her forehead down onto them for a moment. “Please tell me what would make you do that.”

    Alex had never seen Kara exhibit physical violence of such magnitude before, and it terrified her. Not only because of how strong she knew her sister was, but also because she saw so much of herself in that display of rage that it shook her right down to her bones. Right now, she was on her knees, pulling Kara’s hands close to her heart, begging her to talk because she knew what that had felt like. Every ounce of that anger was familiar to her, and it scared her badly enough to forego any shred of pride or workplace decorum she had left and to beg her sister on her knees to come clean.

    “Morgan Edge.” Kara muttered the words quietly as if she were ashamed of them.

    Alex lifted her head and pinched her brow together as she stared at her sister, confused. “He’s… we’re not—”

    “He’s still in here…” Kara said as she tapped her temple with a forefinger. Her face, crestfallen and now streaked with rolling tears peered downward into her lap as she hung her head in shame.

    Bewildered and heartbroken, Alex leaned back on her haunches and blinked the cloudiness from her eyes.

    “Kara, look at me—” Alex said as she squeezed Kara’s hands three times.

    I

    Love

    You

    “I swear on everything that I will keep you safe. Haven’t I always?”

    Kara nodded her head lowly and pulled her hands free to wipe at her tears.

    “How long have you been angry like that?” Alex asked breathily, like it was a secret between them. There had been so many of those already… If she were to save her sister from an explosive rage disorder diagnosis and a lifetime’s worth of regrets and “Wish I hadn’ts”… then she really did need to ask. Kara had been bitten, and Alex needed to draw the poison out before it was too late.

    The blonde shrugged her shoulders heavily. She kept dropping her face to her lap, letting Alex know that she was deeply, deeply ashamed.

    Not long… good.

    Not like me.

    “Listen Kara, that—” Alex took a deep breath. “That… is a line that you cannot cross again. That feeling inside of you will grab you by the neck and it will never let go. Trust me, I know.” She placed her hands on the tops of her thighs then and stood, pulling Kara up from the chair with her.

    “You’ve been walkin’ in my shoes, and it’s been givin’ you blisters. I see that now… and I am so, so sorry. Please, I am begging you baby girl, don’t let it take control of you.” Alex took her sister by her shoulders and braced her stiffly.

    “I won’t.” Kara’s voice was small, but Alex took her at her word.

    “Go on home, now. You need to run to the thing that washes that feeling away. Trust me on that too… I go home and I look at her every night.”

    “I’m sorry—” Kara began to repeat her apology when Alex lifted a hand.

    “Doesn’t help to piss and moan about it either, the guilt will eat you up and turn into something else entirely. Just go home, that’s the only thing you can do right now. I’ll be by later, okay?”

    Kara took a moment to fix the collar on her shirt and gave Alex another swift nod before exiting the room. Alex couldn’t believe she had sat idly by with her own problems while her sister was flailing invisibly, being drowned by her growing anger. It hurt because she knew exactly what that felt like. How lonely it had been, and how inevitable it had seemed. She had chosen to walk that path on her own, but Kara didn’t have to. She had people, and Alex would never let her forget it.

    The detective stood in silence as her mind raced and quickly came to realize that Lex was still cowering in the other interview room across the station. And was likely bruised to hell, too. It filled her with a sense of dread; No doubt he’d be ranting and raving about being released as soon as she stepped back in the room with him.

    Just as Alex made it back to the two-way mirror outside of Interview Room One, an analyst ran to her side to hand her a folder. Faxed documents from Interpol, finally. With a quick “Thank you”, Alex thumbed through the contents of the folder which revealed a confirmed set of Lex’s fingerprints along with a death certificate from County Down, Northern Ireland. That’s when it hit her.

    He's dead…? 

    Oh.

    “Holy shit…” Alex muttered under her breath just on the other side of the door. She understood so much now. Most importantly, she finally understood that it hadn’t been Interpol refusing to cooperate that had them hung up for so long on the case, when it was actually the prison system in County Down that had apparently felt no real sense of urgency to fax along a dead man’s records. They simply didn’t know that he wasn’t in fact dead, let alone skimming the soft, seedy underbelly of the United States all the way down south to Austin, TX. What Alex wanted to know more than anything now was why.

    When she swept into the room, Lex appeared calm and seated back in his chair with the table righted in front of him. Alex truly had the upper hand now, but why did he still appear to think that he did? She began to lay out each of the documents on the table in front of him. First, a mugshot from his arrest. Second, his prison records from Long Kesh that included his fingerprints and at last, Alex smacked Lex’s death certificate flat onto the table and slid it toward him with a finger as she pulled her chair over and sat down.

    “You’ve been a busy boy, Alexander Joseph. Tell me, how much of an absolute scumbag do you have to be to come up with a counterfeit ID so quickly after faking your death, escaping prison and sneaking into another country to try and kill your half-sister?”

    Lex shrugged with a smug look on his face that nearly resembled a smile. Was he trying to hold it back? Whether or not that was true, it infuriated Alex.

    “If I had wanted to kill her, she’d be dead for sure.” He replied after a moment. Alex remembered Lena had said something eerily similar on Halloween in the park just after the shooting. That, unfortunately, was something she could at least confirm to be true now. The more this man spoke, the more difficult it became to not leap over the table and throttle him, but Alex did her very best to keep a cool head. Anything above what she couldn’t easily explain away as an ‘accidental bump’ would only make worse what had already been a difficult to piece together case.

    “Then why all the cloak and dagger? You didn’t cross an ocean and risk getting caught this many times just to play your twisted little mind games and shoot at my family for fun.”

    “Regrettably, Lena is a Luthor, and she has what I am owed. In a way, she belongs to me.”

    “Typical.” Alex rolled her eyes hard. “Another straight cisgendered white dude thinking the world owes him something just for drawing breath.” Then she narrowed her eyes into daggers across the table at him and clenched her jaw. “And for the record, Luthor or not… Lena belongs to us now. We’re her family, and you’re in my house.”

    Lex sneered and craned his head back, throwing his eyes toward the ceiling. Alex saw then the deep, roiling burn scars that appeared from behind the tall collar of his woolen sweater. She then dug her heels in and continued on her quest to wrench answers out of the man by whatever means necessary. Legal means, that is. Though the alternatives were tempting.

    “What is it you think you’re owed, hm?” Alex asked coyly.

    A long pause hung in the air between them and mixed with the rattle from the a/c vent just above their heads. Finally, Lex brought his eyes front again and stared Alex hard in the face.

    “The half-breed bitch has money that was meant for me. My family’s money.”

    Alex kept silent in the hopes that if given enough room, Lex would volunteer more information. She crossed her arms over her chest to convey her disbelief, seeking out his insecurities. The more he talked about Lena, the more unsettled he seemed to become. That was a wound that Alex wished to dig a thumb in and twist.

    “Got a debt needs to be paid, I have.” He began to explain pompously. “Y’see, I was for a time mixed up wit a scary lot. Had themselves a right cunt of a boss. Take your fingers as payment, that sort of ting.”

    Alex fought the urge to chew the inside of her lip. She could sit there and expend her energy trying to discern the truth from a lie or she could wrap up the compelling evidence she currently had and cart him off to jail while the district attorney decided what to do next. Her eyes zeroed in on him. Lex only continued to meet her gaze with that infuriating, pretentious stare of his.

    Either he’s too stupid to be afraid, or he simply isn’t yet…

    Alex leaned forward then and scraped the pages back into the folder with a vengeance. As far as she was concerned, he was done. There was no chance of him hurting anyone else ever again. Not if she had anything to do with it.

    “Fine. I’ll make a deal with’ye.” He spat at her quickly.

    Alex scoffed. “We’ve got you dead to rights, you idiot. What could you possibly have that I would want?”

    “Oh—” Lex lifted his chin with an air of confidence. “I reckon it might or might not have something to do with that angry blonde bird that was just in here poundin’ against me chest.”

    The edge of the table suddenly shifted forward and landed hard into the man’s torso, pushing an unexpected and pained “Oof!” out of him. Alex stood abruptly and stopped herself just short of gripping him by the back of the neck and shoving his face into its cold metal.

    “You so much as utter a single syllable of a threat and I’ll bring her back in here so fast your fucking head will spin, do you understand me?” Alex hissed through the thin space of her teeth.

    “Oh aye, wind yer neck in…” Lex replied, splaying his fingers wide where he held his hands outward, caught by the chain. “As it happens, I just may know sometings about a rather tragic accident. A fire, was it?”

    The upward curve of his brow and the rest of his feigned empathy set alarm bells off in Alex’s head, warning her not to tread any further. The man had to be lying, but his eyes were telling the truth. They bore into her hard, instilling in her what she knew to be a very dangerous and upsetting line of questioning. Something about the way he was sitting, and the way his eyes stayed on her, straight forward and unrelenting in their piercing nature made her give in to her predictable sense of curiosity. It was after all her job to investigate. Though, she knew she would be thoroughly upset with herself had she chosen to entertain a madman’s sense of humor for a serious inquest.

    “You’re full of shit.” Alex stared back at him, unwavering.

    “Am I now?”

    “Think about it- how am I supposed to take you seriously? You’re supposed to be dead. I should just ship you back to Ireland and let them deal with you—”

    “Oh no, I’ll be stayin’ here.”

    Alex lowered her voice to a growl and leaned further with her palms flat on the table now. “How do you figure that?”

    Lex shifted in his seat uncomfortably for a moment. “You so much as send me anywhere outside of this building and I’ll really be as good as dead. And then you’ll never know.”

    It was that slight drop of his guard that told Alex there was some truth to the things he said. Not only did it appear like he had been running toward something this entire time, which they had previously concluded was Lena, but now it seemed as though he was also running from something. He wanted to be here.

    “Let’s say I’m interested in this information. What do you want for it?”

    “Just as I said. I want to be incarcerated here in America.”

    Alex squinted inquisitively. “Why here?”

    Lex darted his eyes to the left again.

    Warmer.

    “Is it because you think you’re safer here?”

    His face then followed his eyes, as if to run away from her.

    Warmer…

    “The people you owe money to, they’re after you?”

    The vein above his temple thumped wildly now as a red flush grew across his cheek.

    Bingo.

    “Well—” Alex turned toward the door to exit the room. “Unless you can give me something real to work with, I can’t help you.”

    “Stop!” Lex shouted after her. Alex heard a long sigh flutter out of the man behind her and then he cleared his throat for a moment before speaking again. “I only know her by the one name, but I’ll tell you everything.”

    Alex waited.

    “Calls herself Astra, she does.”

     

     

    Dusk in November was cool and comfortable, which made lounging on a porch all the more enjoyable. The steep drop in temperatures during the fall months typically prompted both reptile and amphibian creature alike to burrow for the winter. It also introduced evenings with little to no insect noise, which came as a great relief to Lena’s ears. Cicadas were fine at the beginning of summer each year, but by Autumn she was glad of the ringing in her ears to be gone. All that remained was the gentle -pop- of a moisture laden wood log in the small stone fire pit that sat on their back deck.

    Lena lay across the cushioned wicker porch swing with her head in Kara’s lap as she watched the flames dance. The sun had set far beyond the small grove of trees at the back of their property and a chill set into her skin where the heat from the fire could not reach. Her shoulders jostled with a full body shiver and Kara’s hand ceased its up and down pattern on her arm to pull a corner of a blanket over her legs.

    Kara turned the inside of her wrist toward her eyes to read the dial of her watch again. Lena could tell that she was anxious. Earlier in the day Kara had left suddenly and without a word, and when she returned she seemed as though to barely be able hold herself together. Her shirt collar had been stretched and wisps of her fine golden hair were untucked from her ponytail as if she had been thoroughly shaken. It didn’t take long for Lena to drag the confession out of her wife, who then required further convincing that the police weren’t about to come knocking down their door to take her in.

    “Alex would warn you if trouble were on the way.”

    “I know.”

    The tall glass door to the deck area turned outward and the sound of boot heel on solid wood sidled up next to the swing. Lena quickly craned her head upward from Kara’s lap and saw Alex lean a hip against one of the large columns that held up their Spanish tiled deck awning.

    “Speaking of trouble…” Lena said with a hint of playfulness. At times, her sense of humor helped to dissuade her rising sense of dread, but it did little to settle her nerves tonight.  

    “I gave a holler in the house, but no one answered so I figured y’all were out here.”

    “That’s alright, have a seat- please.” Lena gestured with a hand across the fire as she sat up from Kara’s lap.

    Alex gave a short dip of her chin in thanks as she sat in the matching wicker armchair across the short distance of the deck and clasped her hands together with her elbows resting on her knees. To Lena, she appeared exhausted, but not without an overwhelming sense of accomplishment, much like if she were a fisherman that had been out at sea for too long and had finally arrived home.

    The three of them there breathed the moment in, mentally preparing themselves for what lay ahead. It was fully dark out now, save the small fire still burning in the middle of the treated deck. No interrupting gusts of wind nor croak of bullfrog broke the silence between them, which only filled the space with even more tension. Lena noticed then how Alex seemed to be unable to tear her eyes away from Kara.

    “You doin’ okay?” Alex asked toward the blonde.

    Kara merely frowned and bobbed her head up and down in the affirmative. She appeared to be as ready as she would ever be and in that moment, Lena decided that she was as well.

    “This will be a lot to take in, so I’m just going to lay it all out and then I’m going to leave and give you two some time alone, alright?”

    “Alright.” Kara answered, taking one of Lena’s hands into her own.

    Alex shifted on her hips to straighten her back with a long sigh and then looked directly at Lena. “To start, Lex was never released from prison. He saw an opportunity to escape and took it. No one went looking for him because everyone had apparently believed him to be dead. Now that we have him, he’s been formally charged with the bombing at your office and the shooting on Halloween. Eve Teschmacher has also been charged as an accessory to both crimes. That was how the explosive device got all the way to your desk. She later concealed evidence from the shooting at her home, which we found.”

    There was a small beat to process information, and then Lena pulled her knees up onto the cushion of the swing. “Had she been seeing him the whole time, then?

    Alex shook her head, seemingly unsurprised by Lena’s dismissal of the news about her brother. “Supposedly she ran into him outside of your office building shortly after you hired her on and she claims to have fallen in love with him.”

    Lena lowered her forehead into her hand and shook it there despairingly for a moment. It wasn’t until Kara gave her other hand a small squeeze before she lifted her gaze again and stared blankly at the flames.

    “There was really no way you could have known, Lena—”

    “I’m aware, but thank you for trying to make me feel better about it.” Lena replied sullenly. There was no bite to her words, only a deep resonance of melancholy. Then she quirked a single eyebrow upward with a sudden thought.

    “The note in Ruby’s Halloween sweets?”

    Alex tilted her head back with a long breath through her nose. “Lex apparently paid a kid at the park to slip it into her bag when no one was looking.”

    “How did he know we’d even be there?” Kara chimed in.

    “Eve.” Lena’s realization was cold, and menacing. “I had mentioned in an email how excited Ruby was to go to the park this year with the other children from her class.” She shook her head again. “She stopped replying shortly after that. I just thought she needed more time after the bombing.”

    Another pause.

    “When will he be going back to Long Kesh?” Lena asked quietly.

    Alex stirred uncomfortably in her seat for a moment before answering. “He’s not.” Lena’s brow pulled together again in consternation before Alex continued. “There’s something else I need to tell you both, and it is… very unpleasant. So please, just know that I had no choice.”

    The way Alex was staring at Kara now made Lena’s stomach start doing backflips. How could there be more? Lena felt as if she’d be stuck there in that wicker porch swing fighting off waves of nausea forever if Alex didn’t just get the hell on with it.

    “Kara—” Alex began with a slight tremble in her voice. “He seems to know some things about…” She trailed off as her words fell aimlessly toward her feet, then she squared her shoulders and appeared to try again. “He knows about what happened to your birth parents.”

    Lena felt Kara’s forearm stiffen where it rested along her own. The blonde sat frozen for several beats until her lips began to move and sputtered attempts for words began to tumble their way out through her mouth.

    “What do you mean what happened…? I know what happened, they died.” Kara insisted.

    Alex looked as if she wanted to stand up and move closer but stopped herself and merely shifted forward toward the edge of her seat. “He says it wasn’t an accident… and I don’t think it was either.”

    “Since when?”

    “Since—” Alex’s eyes began to dart around the deck. “…Recently.”

    “Since Astra, you mean.”

    Alex seemed to double down with that and clasped her hands again at her front. “Yes. Lex said they had met a long time ago in London and she funded his bomb making… but getting caught and going to prison prevented him from paying her back.
