    Her fingertips gripped around the hard edges of Kara’s shoulder blades as she inevitably traveled closer and closer to her peak and finally, like a breaking dawn that crested over lonely mountaintops, Lena came with a bellowed cry into Kara’s mouth. She shook and juddered with the throbbing between her legs and after another long moment, finally managed to settle into a warm, tingling afterglow.

    As she caught her breath, Lena tossed a curtain of blonde hair from one side of Kara’s face to the other and pulled her down by the neck again to kiss her. When they parted, a hot tear fell onto her cheek from above. Kara raised herself onto her elbows and hovered over Lena calmly to stare at her with a deep sense of belonging. Her tear-filled eyes bounced back and forth with the words still trapped inside of her head, once again unable to find the door that led them out into the world. Lena sighed deeply and palmed the sharp edge of Kara’s jaw in a way that said, “It’s okay, I still love you”.

    Chapter End Notes

    Really wanted to shine a light on some of Kara's inner turmoil from an outside perspective... So much devastation deserves a small interlude to reconnect.

    I had for a time felt as though Lena was beginning to come off as a bit of a Mary Sue, so I introduced her to a limit. A person can possess miles of patience and still reach the end of a road. She's only human, after all. If you’ve been paying attention to detail and to the overall plot (I know I can blither on at times, I am sorry for that), the bulk of this story’s conflict centers around Lena and the struggle betwixt her brother… but do also keep in mind that in this universe she’s had many years to process most of that grief already, even prior to meeting Kara. (Healed people heal people. She was always meant to be a pretty solid character from the beginning.) Kara still deals with her own struggles simultaneously (ex. confronting her anger, letting go of the past…)—All of this to say that I don’t wish to write superfluous conflict, it has to mean something and from it must grow something beautiful.

    A small nod in this chapter to some CW series canon in regards to Lena's love of the movie Titanic.

    Kudos to anyone who can spot an iconic sapphic film line in this chapter. Shout at me in the comments about it, why don't you! <3

    Just two updates to go! The next chapter will for the most part wrap everything up with a nice little bow, and the last one will be more of an epilogue. Thank you so very much in advance for giving this story your precious time and for leaving all of your comments. It has all made me very happy and eager to continue writing. (More news on that in the end notes of chapter 17.)

    See you on Tuesday!

    Chapter 16: Brighter

    Chapter Summary

    A meeting had. A game played. A miracle delivered.

    Chapter Notes

    Welcome to the end of Under the Shelter of Each Other, People Survive.

    (I meant to post this update much earlier... but I sort of forgot what day it was. Forgib me.)

    Chapter Sixteen: Brighter

     

    The small fire burning in the downstairs hearth quietly crackled and spit orange glowing embers into its flue as Kara lazily stuck a poker into the logs. She mindlessly shifted the wood and ash around as she stood there with an elbow leaned on the mantel piece above, appearing to be vacant of all thought. The comforting heat from the flames warmed her shins through the material of her cuffed sweatpants and seemed to slowly melt the tension right out of her body.

    The sound of softly padded footsteps came down the stairs as Lena carried a folded quilt from their bedroom. It would cover them on the couch while they sat in front of the fire and watched television that evening, which was just another favorite part of her days at home with Kara. A smile spread across her lips and dimpled her cheek as she strode toward the hearth, swinging her hips with just the slightest emphasis in each step.

    “Come be my pillow?” The raven-haired woman asked sweetly as she draped the quilt over her legs and sat comfortably on the couch. The blonde smiled and seemed eager to oblige her as she sat down with an arm open wide. A long and satisfied sigh billowed out of her as Lena’s dark, silky hair come to rest against her shoulder.

    Everything was just as it should be.

    Lena nuzzled the top half of her body into Kara’s warmth and silently wished that they could stay like this forever. If not for the flashing and skipping of light against her eyelids from the tv above the mantel, she would soon be asleep. A weather forecaster from a passing news channel announced that there would be a ‘chance of snow’ the next day, to which Kara huffed a small breath of disbelief through her nose, rocking Lena’s head slightly from where it rested against her. Lena smiled again. She quite liked the idea of snow, especially since she hadn’t seen a single flake in nearly seven years.

    Wouldn’t it be wonderful…

    The -ding- of Lena’s phone on the table in front of the couch jarred her eyes back open again. With a small groan, she leaned forward out of Kara’s arm to silence it for the night but saw a text message notification from Alex run across its screen first.

    Call me when you get a chance, please.

    For the past couple of months, Alex and Lena had been communicating more directly when it came to things like checking in on Kara and looking after Ruby. It had been a blow at first to Kara when her sister had initially suggested that they suspend Ruby’s weekends at their house until the matter of Kara’s missing voice had been resolved, but as time went on and everyone realized that it was of somewhat more permanence than expected, Alex resolved to thoroughly discuss Kara’s lingering speechlessness with her daughter so as she would not be traumatized by her Aunt’s inability to talk. The five-year-old had taken the news with grace, as well as an immense understanding of grief for her age by simply saying, “Being sad took away her words, but maybe we can help her find them again.”

    “Hang on babe—" Lena said as she leaned back into Kara’s sturdy frame and casually tapped the call button, where not even a full ring went by before Alex quickly picked up the other end.

    “Hey.”

    “Hi, is everything alright?” Lena asked as she looped a length of her hair behind an ear and rest her head again on Kara’s shoulder.

    “Yeah- um… Listen, can you put me on speaker real quick? I need to talk to Kara too.”

    “Sure—” Lena acquiesced as she brought the phone away from her face and tapped the speakerphone button. “She’s right here.”

    “Alright…” The other woman’s voice sounded far away and tinny now through the phone. “I’ve got some news.”

    Kara shifted uncomfortably on her hips for a split second and muted the tv before Lena slipped a hand into hers.

    “We got her.” Alex cleared her throat for a moment and gave her announcement some room to settle before continuing. Lena caught out of the corner of her eye Kara’s masseter muscle working at full tilt with the clenching of her jaw. “She tried to lose us up the east coast and waited to fit in with the holiday traffic coming out of Dulles International, but we got her.”

    Kara was still except for the repetitive gnashing of her back teeth, and her brow had pulled together into a steely gaze across at the fire. Lena noticed it to be the same face she made whenever she was pierced with a stitching needle, or whenever she stared at too many numbers on a page. Kara continued her cogitation for another long moment and then pursed her lips with an acknowledging dip of her chin.

    “…Lena?” Alex’s voice rang out into the quiet again. 

    “Yeah—” Lena shot her attention back to the phone held out flatly in her other hand. “Yeah, we’re here. Thank you for telling us. Does that mean you’re coming home soon?”

    “I’ll be back tomorrow. But—” Alex paused for a beat. “I’m not supposed to be sharing this information. You understand, don’t you?

    “Of course.” Lena replied.

    “They’re holding Astra at the city jail until they can move her to another location where she’ll await trial. If Kara wanted to talk to her one last time before that happens, then this would be it.”

    As quickly as Lena could turn her head to look at her, Kara swiped the pad of her thumb out from the bottom of her chin and hooked her index finger with one downward motion to sign “Don’t need.”

    “Are you sure?” She whispered toward the blonde as she ran a thumb lovingly over the hand in her own.

    Kara signed again, this time with an added shake of her head to emphasize her decision.

    Alex chimed in from the other end of the phone then. “We’re landing early in the morning, and she’ll only be accessible for one day…”

    “That’s alright, Alex. We’ll just be glad to have you back safe and sound again.” Lena answered calmly.

    “I’ll be there soon.”

    “Okay then, slán abhaile.”

    Lena leaned back into Kara’s side again, who accepted her with a tight squeeze of her arms. She felt lighter, somehow. As if a lingering pressure had been taken off her chest, allowing her to breathe deeply and fill her lungs with full breaths for the first time in what seemed like a long time. Kara seemed to be just as calm and relaxed. Lena knew beyond a shadow of a doubt that it would relieve Kara immensely to know that Astra would no longer be able to roam about the world a free woman. She craned her head upward to look at Kara through her tired lashes, revealing that dependable, stoic expression she admired so much and soon after succumbed to the hard pull of sleep.

     

     

    “Where do you want them?” Kara asked plainly with two luggage bags tucked under each arm.

    The blonde’s voice seemed to be more striking in decibel from where she stood in the middle of the small room, as if she were very large and their surroundings had grown very small. A bed lay next to a wide window with soft eggshell curtains drawn over it, and a homely bedside table lamp bathed a corner of the room with a warm amber color from its dark shade. Beyond the window Lena could see that it was barely dusk, and a chorus of cicadas sang their reverberated hum loudly enough to seep through the walls of the oddly familiar room.

    Wait…

    Lena thought.

    It's cold outside.

    There shouldn’t be any insects…

    The sound of keys against a countertop brought her dizzied focus back to the stolid, seraphic woman standing before her. Every hair on Lena’s body stood on end and her heart hammered behind her ribs the longer Kara stared at her in the deafening silence. The brightest blue eyes Lena had ever seen fell to her slightly heaving chest, and the raven-haired woman immediately became aware that she was caught flustered. Her cheeks quickly grew warm with a deep, rosy color.

    Another charged moment passed until Kara finally leaned into the tension and bent to empty her hands of the bags at her feet. The blonde suddenly closed the distance between them and stepped into Lena’s space. Now, standing just a breath beyond her lips, Kara seemed to search over Lena’s delicate features as if taking in a thing of beauty.

She fought for breath as the rest of her body began to move surely and swiftly of its own volition, beginning with her arms. Lena was overcome with the imperative urge to explore the landscape of lean and sinewy muscle that stood but a single inch away from her. It was a feeling so powerful that it may surely have ended up killing her if she had possessed the strength to ignore it. She hadn’t, so her hands did as they pleased.

Lena then ran her hand down the length of Kara’s neck, swooping her thumb into the shallow dip just above her collarbone as she passed it by. Her fingertips continued over a hard shoulder and down a rigid forearm where she squeezed lightly. Lena wanted to feel with her own hands every solid and stellar muscle that rested on Kara’s empyrean bones.

    Kara was ethereal in her presence, an imposing and angelic being who sat on clouds as she watched the world beneath turn. Lena looked up at her like the earthly wanderer that she herself was—absolutely desperate to climb.

    This…

    A muted moan passed between their lips.

    I know this.

    I’ve been here before…

She felt as though she had met Kara all the way up on her cloud now and hung dangerously close to its feathery edge, mere seconds away from falling to her death back down upon the earth.

    Once the strong pulse between her legs subsided, Lena turned in the cradle of Kara’s arms and faced her with a satisfied, yet somewhat still sleepy smile. “Good morning.”

    The blonde simply leaned forward and pecked a kiss at the tip of Lena’s nose before closing her eyes again. Her movements stilled, and she eventually softened with her lingering tiredness.

    “Go back to sleep, a mhuirnín.” Lena whispered softly as she pulled tufts of messy blonde hair away from the peaceful expression on Kara’s face. She then carefully climbed out from the warmth of their bed and tiptoed quietly into the bathroom to shower. As the water ran over her shoulders and melted away the remnants of her worries, there was one thought that she had yet to shake from her mind since speaking on the phone with Alex the night before.

     

     

    The phone rang so many times on the other end that Lena was a split-second away from hanging up and sending a text instead, but the sound of Alex’s voice brought the device quickly back up to her ear.

    “Hello?”

    “Alex?”

    “Yeah- sorry, I was stuck in an elevator. What’s up?”

    The sounds of busy footsteps and several echoing voices came through the phone and suddenly Lena began to doubt if whether her idea to call had been a good one.

    “Is now a bad time?”

    “Oh, not really to be honest, it’s all chaos all the time, so—"

    “Okay.” Lena hesitated for a moment, but decided to push forward. “So, that offer you made yesterday when you called…”

    “Is Kara talking?” Alex asked with a hopeful tone.

    “Oh… no. I’m sorry. I was more wondering if I could…” Lena floundered again. “Well, I was wondering if I could speak with Astra.”

    There was a short pause before Alex spoke again. “Okay. Yeah, I can make that happen. I’m actually at the jail right now. If you’re serious, you should come now before it’s too late. They’re moving her today, but I don’t know exactly when.”

    “Right now?” Lena asked as her anxiety washed over her like a warm flood.

    “Yes. Now.” Alex replied firmly. “Don’t worry, I’ll be here the whole time.”

    It was a chance for some answers. Some of which she may never get if she passed up the opportunity to talk to the woman who destroyed so many lives for reasons unknown. It was a thing she found herself needing to know more than anything… Why?

    Kara on the other hand seemed to not need the closure, but Lena trusted her in that. For herself, it was something she knew she couldn’t go without. She needed it, even if Kara didn’t want it. Maybe her wife was better for that. Perhaps knowing was what hurt her so deeply. Lena simply couldn’t deny just how much of an opposite she was in this case; For it was the not knowing that killed her. Lena knew Lex. She had, for years, dealt with his mistakes and recovered from them, so surviving him yet again was a certainty.

    Astra was not.

She noticed how practically everything was bolted either together, or to the floor itself. The room held in total five tables with four orbiting seats each, one door, and no windows. The drab pale blue paint peeled away from the cinderblock lined walls in some places, leaving the visitation area much to be desired in personality. Though, Lena figured ‘personality’ wasn’t exactly high priority when it came to the jail’s budget. She admonished herself with a small, indiscernible mutter about having chosen the one seat directly situated under a rather powerful ceiling vent, which blew a menacing and unrelenting stream of acrid, cold air on her face. It smelled stale, and devoid of all hope.

    It was a jail, after all.

    Alex had managed to secure ten minutes of a meeting with Astra in between the many processes that involve the handing over of city assets to a federal case. All of her T’s needed to be crossed, and all of her I’s dotted. Ten minutes was all Lena was going to get with the woman who single handedly upended two entire families, not to mention the possibility of countless others. Ten minutes to hopefully find some answers.

Maybe it was possible that she could bear living without knowing why? The urge to get up and leave overwhelmed her, but then the shuffling of chain from the other side of a door told her that it was too late to escape. It was now, the never had just vanished.

    Infuriating…

    “You’ve asked me a question, and I’ve given you an answer. I will certainly give you more, but only if you answer some of mine in return. We take turns, but once a door to a subject has been opened, you shut it behind you.” Astra finished and smiled blandly again.

    Better now that you’re under lock and key.

    Safe, happy, and loved by many.

    She’s wonderful… and absolutely nothing like you.

    “Ah-ah, one question at a time.”

Her hand upon the smooth railing steadied her as she landed on the small carpet at the bottom. Lena caught sight of Kara in her winter jacket walking back toward the door outside with her arms full of logs for the fireplace, and she ran to meet her on the other side.

    Kara stopped in her tracks as soon as Lena came to a halt in front of her and tilted her head inquisitively at the raven-haired woman. The height of her cheeks were splotched with red from her exertion of restocking the iron cage near their fireplace inside. She must have already made several trips in and out of the house traveling back and forth between the wood pile below the deck and their living room. A knit beanie covered the tops of her ears, and her gloved hands gripped at the several logs tucked inside of her arms. She stood there staring at Lena as if suddenly awestruck, her labored breaths expelling plumes of misty clouds into the cold air.

    It was as if Lena’s blood froze in an instant. Never before had she done something of this magnitude and sought to keep it from her wife, and the mere idea of it made her feel weak in the knees. She didn’t like the way it felt on her skin at all. Lena could hardly keep gifts a secret from Kara, let alone an impromptu visit to speak with her deranged psychopath of an aunt.

    “I went to go see Astra.” Lena had all but blurted the admission out in a quick string of hurried words and clapped her hands over her mouth instinctively.

    Kara simply stood blinking at her for a moment, and then cast her eyes down to the wood planks of the deck for a brief moment before bringing them back up toward Lena’s face. The blonde then shrugged her shoulders in a manner that suggested, “How did it go?”

    “I—” Lena began to flail subconsciously. “Kara, I had to. I couldn’t…” Her words trailed off as soon as Kara bent down to drop her armfuls of logs onto the deck and closed the gap between them.

    Lena’s guilt began to fade as soon as Kara wrapped her arms around her and squeezed. The sweet, earthy scent of honey and oats warmed her soul from the stark cold that bit into her skin through the thin fabric of her sweater. Kara wasn’t upset with her. Instead, she welcomed her into her arms like she always did. Tucked safely inside of the blonde’s thick jacket, Lena remarked inwardly at the fact that no matter what seemed to happen, Kara always managed to remain two things: A mountain of strength, and a deep well of patience. She’d stumble of course, but she never fell. How someone could be so stricken with a sadness that it steals away such a vital piece of them, yet can still be strong enough to care for another... Lena would never understand. Though, she figured she didn’t need to. Love was the one thing that she didn’t have to pick apart and put back together just to figure out how it worked. Lena was just glad that it had happened to her. That Kara had happened to her.

    “I want you to know something…” Lena said as she pulled her head away from Kara’s shoulder. “I want you to know how incredible and how resilient you are… and how lucky I feel to know you, and to love you. When I count my blessings, I count you twice, Kara. I also learned something today—” She reached a hand up to cup Kara’s cheek, who smiled downward at her with her eyes. “Where you came from, and all the hurt and pain that came with it… none of that makes you who you are. You are not a product of that place, and you are not a product of those people. I’ve seen it with my own eyes. You are absolutely and irrevocably a thing all of your own… Which is a deeply, deeply kind, and wonderful human being who I am so very proud of.”

    Lena managed to finish past the cold sting of tears that rolled one at a time out of her eyes. It was as if she had shed the final remaining skin of a past that no longer tethered her to a wallowing darkness.

    A listless, frozen cloud of white fell between her eyes then and landed on the tip of her nose where it quickly melted.

    Kara dropped her mouth open for a moment in shock, and then dove forward to hug Lena again, shuffling her feet closer the more she squeezed. The blonde took deep breaths into the warm space of Lena’s neck and with her last satisfied sigh came two small, raspy words:

    “Thank you…”

    Lena gasped and shot her head back to look Kara in the face. Kara’s eyes went wide at the sound of her own voice. She looked as though she hadn’t recognized it at first, but Lena would know it anywhere. Kara’s mouth hung open for a moment again as snow began to slowly fall around them, then she licked her lips and tried speaking again.

    “Thank you—” Kara repeated, and then burst with a laugh. “Thank you!”

    Kara scooped Lena up into her arms, spinning her around into the falling, powdery whiteness as she uttered a repeated “Thank you, thank you, thank you” into the side of the raven-haired woman’s smiling face. More tears of joy leapt from Lena’s eyes as Kara began to kiss her over and over again, sobbing quiet and thankful breaths in between as if she’d never been more glad in her whole life.

    “Lena…”

    Everything was truly as it was before. Only brighter.

    Chapter End Notes

    Kissing in snow... sigh.

    Translations:
    "Slán abhaile" = "Safe home" A phrase said to someone who is traveling home.

    Epilogue on Thursday. <3

    Chapter 17: Epilogue

    Chapter Summary

    A hopeful goodbye.

    Chapter Notes

    Please be sure to read the chapter end notes!

    Epilogue

     

    —One Year Later—

     

    Wildflowers would always be Kara’s favorite. From where she stood, the vibrant bluebonnets and Indian paintbrush waved gently in the breeze against a lush sea of green. Everywhere she seemed to look purple winecups and pink evening primroses complemented the deep blues and reds that glittered the wild and open plain. The sweet, earthy perfume of sweetcorn and sorghum blew in with the north wind and greeted her like an old friend. It had been a long road, but another hot summer had passed with another winter in tow and now, two springs past some of the hardest days of her life, Kara stood at the foot of her adopted mother’s grave in the willow dotted cemetery of her hometown. Her mother and father’s resting places weren’t far off past a small slope of a hillock or two, but Eliza had raised her, loved her, and taught her most of the important things in life… as well as how to withhold yourself from the people that you love.

    For that, Kara didn’t blame her. Now that she knew everything, how could she?

    There’s damage done and damage repaired, then there’s damage so far gone and so irreparable that the responsibility to ‘know better’ ends with willful ignorance. Most of those afflicted as a by-product of this generational trauma are left with a single hard decision to make at least once in their lives; to leave or to stay. But how does one truly know when to cut your losses and to move on?

    Kara went to her knees in the coarse, wind-flattened grass and hung her head low in front of the headstone. She felt a bittersweet sense of pride knowing that despite her mother’s upbringing, which had only been a handing down of neglect for the sake of independence and parity, Eliza had managed in the end to see that real love between two people transcends any sermon which preaches hate. She had died proud of her daughters, and optimistic for their futures in a world that was learning to welcome them with open arms.

    Kara understood everything now.

    “I’m sorry, Mama.” Kara said quietly, folding her hands tightly into her lap. “I’m sorry that you felt you had to live that way.”

    Tears clouded the edge of her sight as she struggled to focus on the deeply carved letters that bannered the bottom of the stone’s face.

    ‘Loving wife, hell of a rider, and mother to many.’

    “I wish you were here.” She whispered.

    Kara calmly wiped away the moisture from her eyes with the back of a knuckle and pulled in a deep breath, straightening her shoulders. “I wanted to come by and tell you that everything’s going to be okay now.” She then shifted on her knees to sit cross-legged and pulled her straw cattleman hat from her head, hugging its crown gently against her chest. “There’s a great big world out there and a woman in it who loves me more than anything... I wanted to tell you that that’s where I’ll be. Your granddaughter, too. Ruby’ll be seven come April, and she is so much like Alex that it would blow your mind. We tell her about you all the time. Mama, I—”

    A small sob caught in her throat. “I don’t want to live a life not tellin’ the people that care about me what kind of hurt I have. I know you thought you were preparing me for a life where I’d have to fight tooth and nail just to be alright, but that ain’t so much the case anymore. Out there… I ain’t got to fight so hard. Things are different now. Things are better.”

    Kara shook the remaining tears from her eyes with a small twist of her head and lumbered upwardly with a hand on her knee to stand again. “I’ve got a family of my own now. You’ll always be a part of it, but I’ll be doin’ things differently. I know that you’re proud of me for that. I love you.”

    The walk back to the cemetery gate felt different this visit. As if she weren’t leaving something behind this time but was instead headed toward something else. That something else stood leaned against the low railed, rusted wrought iron gate with a warm smile on her face.

    “Look at all of these—" Lena said with an air of admiration at the white-petaled daisy she spun in between her thumb and forefinger. “Have you ever seen anything more beautiful?”

    Kara’s heart soared at the look of uninhibited joy on Lena’s face. She knew full well that these were not the first wildflowers her wife had seen and yet she still seemed to be amazed by them.  Kara stuck out a hand then, offering her to take it. “I have.” She winked. “Take a walk with me?”

    Lena smiled again and took Kara’s fingers between her own. “Áit ar bith leat.”


     

    Vivid blue eyes blinked another silent goodbye through the rearview mirror at the narrow two laned highway behind them. The long drive back home to Austin would give Kara plenty of time to reflect on the past two years, for it was on that morning that everything had finally come to an end.

    The dark and ominous raincloud that hung over their heads during the trial had rolled away, leaving a clear picture of the future that lay ahead of them. Months’ worth of preparation, court dates, and heartache had paid off in the end. In exchange for his damning testimony against Astra, Lex submitted guilty pleas to all of his charges in order to serve a life sentence in an American prison. After over a year of building a case and seeing it through from district, federal, and then an international criminal court, Alex had gained an enormous amount of recognition for her work in unraveling a hidden web of crimes that went seemingly unnoticed for years, and on a global scale at that.

    Astra would never see the light of day again in her natural life.  

    It had been a very trying year on everyone affected, especially Lena who was forced to relive the horror of her brother’s crimes in Northern Ireland, and Kara, who learned that it was Astra herself who had set the fire at her parent’s lumber mill instead of hiring the job out as she would have done with anyone else. As it turned out, the FBI had been tracking a nameless and faceless crime boss who had been linked to dozens of assassinations, bombings, and other acts of terror around the globe, which all they knew for years was that she was a woman who held no discernable preference of who she aided in the killing of, or why.

    Nevertheless, and against the recommendation of her legal counsel, Astra took the stand that morning. Leave it to the borderline psychotic sociopaths with narcissistic tendencies to jump at the opportunity for a grand speech in order to justify their wrongdoings.

    In a clear display of unchecked and untreated oppositional defiant disorder, Astra detailed the beginnings of her violent growth to ‘global crime boss’ with a chance meeting of a group of anarchists traveling southward through Dallas in her twenties. It wasn’t until years later that the group formed themselves into an outlaw motorcycle club known as The Children of Liberty, whose chapters spread into neighboring states and in time, far across the bible belt. They were the very same ‘one percenters’ responsible for much of the drug distribution and gun running that ran rampant within the greater cities of Texas, and it was the very same MC that Alex had stumbled upon in an abandoned warehouse more than ten years ago in an effort to rescue Sam, which nearly cost herself her life.

    Astra, claiming to be above such small-time wicked deeds, sought to exact her hatefulness upon the world at large and spent the following thirty years latching onto one organization after another, accruing a vast amount of wealth as well as notoriety.

    “Is that what you think? That my goal is simply to kill humans? You are so very wrong. I am here to save you all.”

    In a manner that was cold and calculating, Astra spent the remainder of her time on the stand before being remanded back into custody for her belligerence by speechifying her self-appointed duty to rid the world of human weakness, and to stop the procreation of those who she deemed less than herself.

    Her one mistake had been Kara’s parents.

    It seemed that Astra took a special, sickening sort of satisfaction in taking the lives of her sister and brother-in-law. Her clear and unbridled hatred and subsequent killing of them had been a thing of great pride to her, which she used as a means of threatening anyone who would dare cross her. Inevitably, this was how Lex came to discover this truth. Astra’s team of defense had at first attempted an insanity plea based on her grandiose lack of humanity, but with Alex’s testimony and close ties to the case, she and a number of expert witnesses were able to prove that Astra’s biased and defined hatred of others was in no way tantamount to insanity, no matter how hard the defense aimed to paint her childhood traumas as a source of madness.

    “Did nobody tell you? How they sent me away? Banished by my own blood, exiled for speaking the truth… I was trying to save you, little one.”

    Astra’s eyes had drilled hard into Kara from across the courtroom that morning as she proclaimed her heroic delusions. It was only when Kara said back at her with no amount of uncertainty and hesitation, “Never call me that again” that Astra became unhinged and was dragged kicking and screaming from the well of the court, only to return hours later for the sound of the judge’s gavel. Kara had stopped counting the life sentences after the second dozen. Astra would serve them consecutively in a maximum-security federal prison without the possibility of parole. It was a death sentence without living out her days on death row, for the Danvers sisters did not wish to have her executed. No, they thought it justice enough for Astra to spend the rest of her life in a small cell by herself, stripped of all human connection, never to be seen or heard from again. This way, they would take the entire world right out from under her feet.

    After the trial had concluded, Alex took the opportunity to share that she had been offered a job with the bureau as a profiling instructor, meaning not only less danger, but less time away from home as well. Kara was thrilled and saw for herself the amount of relief this new job offered her family by the look on Sam’s face as they stood in the noisy corridor of the courthouse.

    Everything really is going to be alright…

    Kara thought long and hard as she stared at the road ahead. The smaller highway had eventually dumped them back onto the interstate as they headed further north toward home. Lena’s window was cracked slightly, allowing the sweet spring air to pass pleasantly against the skin of her cheek and flitted her raven-colored hair with quick, lashing whips. Lena closed her eyes into the soft wind and smiled. Kara glanced over at her then and took a small, dainty hand from the lap across from her own. She squeezed it lightly, interlacing their fingers.

    “I want to ask you something.” Kara said as her eyes looked forward again down the road.

    Lena turned her face to the blonde and drew a knee up onto the bench seat, tucking her foot beneath herself. “Okay.”

    Kara paused for a moment to pull in a deep breath, and then let it out slowly. “Have your feelings changed about becoming a parent?”

    “They have, I think.” Lena answered after mulling the thought around.

    There was a short beat after that which allowed the blonde to process. Kara had a whole new outlook on life. There was room for a child now not only in their home, but in her heart. She could sit there for hours debating whether the fact that they could meant that they should, but she now realized that want also deserved a seat at the table.

    “Okay… for or against?” Kara asked as she rested the elbow of her steering hand on the armrest of her door.

    Another pause.

    “For.” Lena’s voice was calm, and resolute.

    “Let’s have a baby.” Kara had spit out the words so quickly that it brought a laugh out of the slightly smaller woman who was climbing closer toward her on the long seat. Lena fastened the middle seat belt around her waist and tilted her head onto Kara’s shoulder with a small sigh. The blonde’s heart rate picked up slightly with her excitement and she squeezed the hand in her own once more. Lena looked up and smiled as she ran her eyes across the face of the woman she loved.

    “Okay.” Lena answered without a single trace of doubt. Kara then beamed a smile down at her and pecked a small kiss upon her forehead.

    “Really?”

    “Mhm.”

    “Because we can still talk about it—”

    “Oh, we’re definitely going to talk about it… but as far as a firm yes or no on the matter, my answer is yes.” Lena said as she wrapped her other hand around Kara’s bicep and leaned into her again, resting her head back against her shoulder.

    “Alright.”

    Lena didn’t have to be looking Kara in the face at that moment to know that she was grinning from ear to ear. Memories of that same grin sitting in that same truck so many years ago when Kara had picked her up and chauffeured her around town danced in and out of her mind like a lovely film reel of moments that never failed to make her heart flutter.

    Civilization finally came into view down the length of the exit ramp off of the interstate. They soon passed an entrance to a Wal-Mart on the right and Kara’s head turned as it caught sight of a cardboard sign attached to the tailgate of a truck which read ‘Free Puppies’.

    Kara chuckled. “Do you maybe want to try a dog first?”

    A heavenly laugh fell out of the raven-haired woman leaning comfortably into her frame.

    “Sure.”



    They named him Waffles, a lovable Boston Terrier whom they raised alongside their son, Jeremiah.

     

     

    —The End—

     

     

    Chapter End Notes

    Translations:
    "Áit ar bith leat" = Anywhere with you

    I used some of my favorite Astra quotes from the show here. Also, Waffles is the name of a boston terrier in the 2022 animated film DC League of Super-Pets who hangs out with Krypto the Super Dog at the park. He's hilarious and I love him.

    **********************************************************

    Why do I want to cry?

    I began this saga eleven months ago. Three stories, nearly one full year. Now, at the risk of being told that I rushed the ending here, I would just like to say that the final pieces of this story fell exactly where they were meant to. I chose to wrap everything up into an epilogue simply because that's how I wanted it to end. I guess I'm not good at goodbyes? I did enjoy bringing this final update back around to the first chapter with Kara admiring the world around her. What began as a peaceful story through our girl's deep blue eyes quickly folded into chaos and expanded more into Lena's version of the world, and then ended with them both having equal parts of a journey together. It was my original intention to make this sequel 95% to the tune of Lena's POV, but that's just not what the characters ended up having me write. I don't make the rules, I just do what I'm told.

    Going forward:
    I've decided to continue writing my favorite femslash ships and having their stories set in Texas. Idk why, I'm just sticking to what I know for now. This will be it for Supercorp for a while, but how many of you are into SwanQueen? >;D I may or may not be working on a pretty cool story already for them. Give me until the beginning of the new year to come out with more on that.

    In the meantime:
    I’ve got it in my head to take a fine-toothed comb to Lone Star Lover in an effort to update a lot of the prose, fix some of the grammar and just weed out some oopsies here and there. That one was an absolute whirlwind and probably needs revisiting, so if anyone was interested in re-reading that story you can look to my tumblr update page for a green light. (Sorry Teach4life237, looks like you may have to read it a third time…? LOL)

    Last but certainly not least:
    I'd like to thank each and every one of you who took the time out of your days for nearly a year to read this crazy train from beginning to end. A lot of you have been with me from the beginning and I appreciate you so, so, so much. It would make for a very long note here to list everyone who commented and to personally thank you, but I would be remiss if I didn't mention one reader in particular: Dani49, you made sure to leave a comment on almost every single chapter from each part of this saga, starting all the way back to November of last year. That's amazing. During some slow times when I thought nobody was reading, there you were. Leaving positive comments every week for almost a whole year really kept me going. But I appreciate all of them, from everyone who stopped what they were doing to type out a few sentences in a comment. I've gotten some really wonderful feedback on these stories which really lifted my spirits at times. Thank you.

    With everything said, I've now ticked this story complete. I'll see you again soon.
